# CLAUDE.md

Notes for working on this repo with Claude Code.

## What this is

RealNPCs (formerly enRAGE Westworld, renamed in 0.6.0): an unofficial C# add-on for the enRAGE nemesis mod for Red Dead Redemption 2. It is a separate DLL (`RealNPCs.dll`) that sits next to enRAGE's `NemesisMod.dll` in the game's `scripts` folder. Its data folder is `Documents\Rockstar Games\Red Dead Redemption 2\RealNPCs` (`RealNPCs.ini`, `RealNPCs.log`, saves, diaries, `voices\`); on first start it copies the old `enRAGE-Westworld` folder over, and it refuses to run while `EnrageWestworld.dll` is still in `scripts`. Internal names (namespaces `EnrageWestworld.*`, class `WestworldScript`, folder `addon/EnrageWestworld`) were kept on purpose. Player-facing text must keep saying "an unofficial add-on for enRAGE" and link enRAGE's Nexus page. Story Mode only. Owner: Lex (GitHub ninjaalex888). Read `README.md` for features and `CHANGELOG.md` for history. The full design doc is the project doc `westworld-addon-design.md` in the claude.ai Project "Red Dead Redemption 2 Mods".

## Build

Stack: Script Hook RDR2 + Script Hook RDR2 .NET V2, net48, x64, **C# 7.3** (no newer language features).

```
cd addon/EnrageWestworld
dotnet build -c Release
```

Needs `refs/ScriptHookRDRNetAPI.dll` and `original/NemesisMod.dll`, both from Lex's own install and never committed (`*.dll` is git-ignored). Output: `addon/EnrageWestworld/bin/Release/net48/RealNPCs.dll`. Copy it into the game's `scripts` folder to test.

Built against enRAGE 0.9.5.3. When enRAGE updates: back up its data folder and the old `NemesisMod.dll` first. Then dump both versions' types and members, private ones included, with the private `tests-private/enrage-diff` tool, which reads them without running their code, and diff the dumps. Anything removed or with a changed signature that the add-on names (reflection in `Bridge*.cs`, typed uses anywhere) needs fixing. Then put the new DLL in `original/`, build, and rerun the overlap checks against the new enRAGE strings: new enRAGE text can match old lines of ours.

## Layout

- `Core/` is plain C# with no game calls: people, rumours, letters, diaries, scenes, all the lines (`Voice`, `Grammar`, `Settings`, `Store`).
- `Game/` talks to the game and to enRAGE (`WestworldScript.*.cs`, `Natives.cs`, `Spawner.cs`, `Stalker.cs`, `Bridge`).
- `addon/EnrageWestworld/package/README.txt` is the player-facing readme that goes in the zip (CRLF line endings in the zip).
- `release/` holds the download zip, because the README links to it. One zip at a time.

## Rules that matter

- **enRAGE: permission, and what stays careful (Lex, 2026-10-26).** enRAGE's author permits decompiling, reverse engineering and reusing its code, and Lex lifted every permission limit on enRAGE's records: they may take part in anything anyone else may (despair since 0.6.3, when free). Adapted enRAGE code is credited, its dialogue and text are never copied (the lines stay original; the overlap checks stay), and the decompiled source isn't committed wholesale. Three limits stay as engineering, not permission: no orders while enRAGE is driving someone (`MayDirect`) until a takeover and hand-back exist; no re-dressing a record (enRAGE knows its people by a look fingerprint, and has no method to relearn one person's look: its `Fingerprint(Ped)`, `NameByLook`, `_fpOf` and `Prints` are private bookkeeping, and records are dressed by `OutfitHash` on every spawn, so records' clothes stay alone; never write its fingerprint table by hand). Since the overnight run (2026-10-27, Lex's brief, Tier 1.4) a record may be the stalker and may despair, through record-safe paths: the stalker borrows a record lightly (`Spawner.BorrowLight`: ours to direct, its mission-entity state left to enRAGE, never marked "no longer needed" on hand-back), checks `RecordStalkWhyNot` every second (the MayDirect rules minus our own hold, our own Flee not counted as fleeing, plus `RecordFreeWhyNot`: enRAGE's role None or WorldNemesis only, no enRAGE plan due within the hour that is his (`LeaderId`), his gang's or near him) and hands them back the moment enRAGE has a use for them, after 20 minutes at most (`[Stalkers] StalkerRecords`); a hand-back for enRAGE's own reasons leaves its new orders alone (`Spawner.LeaveTasks`: only our hold and the event block go), any other gives a wander task; a record's death by despair or by the hand of the man he fought in one of our street scenes is booked through enRAGE's own `History` and `SaveRoster`, after waiting for enRAGE to resolve it (`IsAlive` only if it never does; never for a record it decided lived, `PresumedDead`; `Fate` stays enRAGE's, since a Fate keeps a record out of its funerals and burials), `[Mind] RecordFates`. Despair needs `RecordFreeWhyNot` too, at the roll and again at the last moment.
- **No enRAGE code, text or files in this repo** so far. The add-on reads what enRAGE tracks; it writes through enRAGE's own methods where one exists, and otherwise only the direct writes listed in "Writing to enRAGE" below, each followed by enRAGE's own save. Every line of dialogue must be original; do not copy or lightly reword enRAGE's strings. Never commit decompiled enRAGE source.
- If an enRAGE update breaks something, only the feature that needs it should switch off, and the log should say which.
- Never run script mods in Red Dead Online.
- No LICENSE file, on purpose (Lex, 2026-10-26): all rights reserved, so it can be opened later; the notice ends both readmes.
- Writing style for all player-facing text: plain, specific, no filler.
- Settings: add a field in `Settings.cs`, a line in `ToIni()`, clamp it in `Clamp()`. Bump `CurrentVersion` when adding options so old inis are rewritten with the new lines (values kept).
- Native out-parameters use 8-byte slots.
- Saved types (0.6.3): every `[DataMember]` in Core is `[DataMember(EmitDefaultValue = false)]` (with `Name = ...` first where it has one); give new members the same. Null, 0 and false are left out of `<player>.json` (Lex's save: 1,613 KB to 916 KB). Loading runs no constructors or field initializers, so a left-out member reads back as its default, as it did when it was written out; proved by round trips (the new code twice, byte for byte; the old code reading the new file writes exactly its old output). Empty lists are still written. Nothing that reads the save may count on a field being there.

## Writing to enRAGE (audit, 2026-10-26)

Lex asked for the bridge's writes to be audited so the docs say what really happens. Reading is free (reflection or enRAGE's own types, read only). Writing goes through enRAGE's own methods where one exists; where enRAGE has none (it does the same thing inline in its own code), a direct write is allowed only if it's on this list, followed by enRAGE's own save, and logged. Lex's call on the mind design (2026-10-26, the private Claude Doc "RealNPCs: the NPC Mind"): RealNPCs shares traits and what happens to people back to enRAGE, so this list grows by two (deaths, built in 0.6.3; tempers, with the traits), and nothing else is added without Lex.

Through enRAGE's methods: `Grace` (quiet while still); `WorldNotice`, `Feed`, `GrudgeNotice`; `ChronicleDeed` (AddDeed); `AddThought`, `Mind.Remember`, `History`, `Mind.SetGoal`, the record's own `AddHate`, `AddFear` and `Touch`; `NewRecord` (enRAGE's person generator) and `FitKinToLife`; `IntelNotice`; `SaveRoster` and `SaveWorld`; the settings panel's `Toggle` and `_reload`. Read only, by reflection: `GunMelee.KnockedOutByGunButt` (the people ledger, to tell the knocked out from the dead).

| Direct write | Where | What it sets | Then | An enRAGE method for it? |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Revive | `Bridge.Revive` | A record's IsAlive, PresumedDead and its time, KilledByPlayer, Fate, RestUntil, Hate | `Remember`, `History`, `SaveRoster` | None; enRAGE revives inline (LoreMenSeen) |
| The avenger | `Life.cs` (the avenger's arrival) | A fresh record from `NewRecord`: Name, Title, KinId, KinRelation, Hate, Origin and its place, RestUntil | `Mind.SetGoal`, `AddToRoster`, `Remember`, `History` | None for filling in a record |
| AddToRoster | `Bridge.AddToRoster` | Adds a record to enRAGE's roster list | `FitKinToLife`, `SaveRoster` | None; enRAGE adds inline |
| AddTownItem | `Bridge.AddTownItem` | A LIFE `WorldEvent` in the chronicle's events (the papers' "Around town") | `SaveWorld` | `AgendaEvent` is for gangs only |
| RevealPlan | `Bridge.RevealPlan`, `RevealPlanAs` | A plan's Known, ToldBy, ToldAbout (by reflection, `Bridge.Set`) | `SaveWorld`, `IntelNotice` | None; enRAGE does it inline (AnswerOrders, UpdateInformants) |
| Marry | `WestworldScript.Marry` | A record's Family = married, a "Married" past entry, one friend id | `SaveRoster` | `MakeFriends` could make the friend link both ways (enRAGE's own cap of 3 and log line); `ApplyLifeEffect("marry")` needs a WorldPart |
| MarkKnown | `Snapshot.MarkKnown` (`RevealToEnrage`) | Past entries told in talk: Known | `SaveRoster` (added in the audit; before, it waited for enRAGE's next save) | `RevealPast` reveals by kind, not the entries actually told |
| LikeMore | `Bridge.LikeMore` | A townsperson's Like, up or down | enRAGE's `_peopleDirty` | None; enRAGE changes Like inline (AnswerPerson) |
| Planned: a townsperson's temper | The mind design's sharing back | Acquaintance.Temper, only when enRAGE hasn't given one | `_peopleDirty` | None |
| A death enRAGE missed (0.6.3) | `Bridge.MarkFolkDead`, from the people ledger | Acquaintance.Dead (Pope McCrae: the area unloaded a second after he died, before enRAGE's 2 s people sync saw the body) | `_peopleDirty` | None; enRAGE marks deaths only in that sync |
| A record's death RealNPCs caused (overnight 2026-10-27, Lex's brief; waiting added after the review the same night) | `Bridge.BookDeath`, from `QueueRecordFate` after a despair death or a death by the hand of the man he came to blows with in one of our street scenes within 2 minutes (`[Mind] RecordFates`); looked at every second from 5 s on, the record re-found by Id (`RecordById`) | enRAGE marked the death (`!IsAlive`): only a History line in plain words through enRAGE's own `History` ("Died by his own hand, with a gun of his own, in Valentine."); enRAGE decided he lived (`PresumedDead`), he got up, or the clock went back: nothing; still alive on enRAGE's books after 30 s of the player in control (enRAGE's world loop runs only then; at most 1 s counted per look, so a fade or a load counts for nothing) or 10 minutes in all, body down or gone: IsAlive = false and PresumedDead = false too (as enRAGE's own death writes do); out cold (`KnockedOutWhy`): it waits; a man already presumed dead when he died (come back) counts as enRAGE deciding he lived only if PresumedDeadAt changed since | `SaveRoster` (`RosterChanged`); log `RECORD death booked: ... (enRAGE resolved it \| RealNPCs marked it)` or `RECORD death not booked: <why>` | None for a death enRAGE missed; its private `Gone` sets a Fate, which keeps records out of its funerals |
| A record as the stalker (overnight 2026-10-27) | `Spawner.BorrowLight` / `GiveBack` | Nothing in enRAGE's data: tasks and event blocking on the ped only, no mission-entity change; a stale light mark is cleared on any hand-back or new borrow | handed back when `RecordStalkWhyNot` says so (enRAGE's orders left alone), or after 20 minutes (a wander task) | not a write |

How they fail: the typed writes are compiled against NemesisMod.dll, so a field an enRAGE update renames throws when the feature runs, and its `Safe` guard switches that one feature off and logs it. The reflection writes (`Bridge.Set`, the plan's three) used to skip a missing property in silence; since the audit they log `enRAGE setting <Type>.<name> failed` once.

Files: RealNPCs never modifies NemesisMod.dll or enRAGE's data files directly; enRAGE writes its own saves after the calls above. The one file of enRAGE's that RealNPCs edits is NemesisMod.ini, from the settings panel, only when the player changes an enRAGE setting there, after a backup (`RealNPCs\backup`). One other mod's file is edited too: CasualHookups.ini in the game folder (once, by `HookupsSetUpOnce`, and from the panel; see "CasualHookups").

## Choosing what to do (daytime 2026-10-27, item 11; the mind design's step 6; Core only)

- `Core/Choosing.cs`; nothing in the game calls it yet (who may be moved stays `MayDirect`, and the Director's pairs from Reasons stay as they are). `Choosing.All`: 14 actions (stand, stroll, eat, rest, home, shelter, work, drink, cards, pray, talk, watch, keep clear of the player, show off), each with what it does for each need (`Serves`, NeedState order), effort, risk, where and when it's proper (`Proper`: never cards or drink on a Sunday morning, never drink or talk in church, never pray in a saloon, eating at mealtimes or where food is, work on weekdays 7-18 outside church and saloon, home after 19...) and how it suits their traits (`Suits`). `Score`: each need squared x what it serves x 1.6 (a pressing need counts far more than a passing one; a vice pulls only toward its own), + how it suits them, + the day's work as a duty for a working trade (`ChoiceContext.Works`, +0.35), a sore mood less for company and a glad one more, - effort (more for the tired), - risk (more for the anxious, less for the bold), - 0.25 for what they did a moment ago, + chance scaled by `Impulse` (low self-control and high risk more, order less). `Choose`: a feeling still felt at strong (`Interrupts` 0.65) goes ahead by its urge (confront, step in, keep clear, hide, freeze, withdraw, help, make amends, repay, tell someone, talk about it, watch it, show it, sit down); else the best proper action and the need it served. mindtest `ChoosingTest`: 600 residents, needs from `Needs.Of`, every third hour 6-21 for 14 days in the places their day takes them (50,400 choices): never improper; eat 38.5% (three of the six hours sampled are mealtimes), rest 21.5%, work 14.7%, shelter 8.4%, talk 5.0%, the rest under 4%; the hungry at noon or six eat every time; a strong fear goes ahead and a fading one doesn't; 30 tries at the same moment give the orderly 1.1 different choices and the impulsive 3.1; the same thing again 267 in 300, 84 after just doing it; at church on a Sunday the devout pray 78%, the godless never; 4.7 microseconds a choice with the needs. Next (not built): a `MIND would:` line in the shadow (what each known person would do now and why), then, behind MindLive and `MayDirect`, the first actions acted out from the catalogue. Reviewed the same day (a subagent; fixed in the follow-up commit): what's done for a while (work, rest, home, shelter, pray, cards, drink) gets +0.3 for going on and the rest -0.25 for again (before, a farmhand at three flipped between rest and work every look; now he changes course 0 times in ten); a feeling's urge is an action of the list where one fits (rest, watch, show off, talk; avoid is keeping clear of the player only when he's the one feared) and otherwise `urge:<urge>` with its own words (`WordsOf`), "dismiss" included (a strong contempt turns its back); a respectable woman of 1899 doesn't drink or play cards in a saloon (`ChoiceContext.Woman`, `SaloonGirl` for the saloon's own women); talk at church after the Sunday service (11-14); work at their own place of work (`WorkPlace`, a barkeep's saloon). Lex's calls (2026-10-27): saloons shut on Sunday mornings only, as built; eating lowers hunger (`NeedContext.AteAt`: a meal they're known to have eaten counts as their last, so nobody eats again within three hours; the game doesn't know when anyone eats until choosing is acted out). mindtest now also checks the 1899 rules on their own (not through `Proper`), women and the saloon, a farmhand's ten looks, and repetition for brief and lasting actions separately; home comes up at eight in the evening.
- Night run 2026-10-28, item 5: choosing wired into the shadow (`MindChooseStep` in `WestworldScript.Mind.cs`, under `[Mind] MindShadow`): one near person a frame from a round-robin cursor, each once a minute, every proper action scored against their needs, nature, mood (`Mood.SoreOrGlad`), the strongest feeling still running (`MindShadow.Strongest`, its urge on `FeltAt.Urge`) and who is in sight (a warm tie, a foe: the store's ties, read only); a `MIND would: Name -> goes to the saloon (company; next: heads home)` line only when the choice changes and isn't carrying on, with `; but enRAGE has him (...)` where `MayDirectWhyNot` would refuse, at most five a minute inside the 20 MIND lines; F7 `TEST mind choices:`; the mind card's `would:` clause. Nobody is tasked. `Choosing.All` gained the saloon (a man after five, Saturday afternoons; never a respectable woman, never a Sunday morning), the store (weekdays 7-20), church (Sunday, Wednesday evening), a word with a friend in sight (`ChoiceContext.TiesNear`), stepping clear of a foe in sight (`FoeNear`); `SeedFor(id, gameMinute)` one roll an hour; `Choose` gives the runner-up; `Continues` (work, rest, home, shelter, pray, the saloon, church go on; a drink or a hand of cards is brief) and `CarriesOn` (stand). `RecentActs` (`ChoiceContext.Recent`: the last three brief things with their game minutes and runs, `Note`, allocated once a person) and `Answered`: a brief thing done lately serves its need less by time (0.4 within the hour, 0.7 within two and a half, 0.85 within four; still at it, 0.7 a time more), since nothing but a meal moves the needs (without it a man showed off twelve hours running); a wary eye, keeping clear and stepping clear never satiate. The shadow's own meal: `MindWho.AteAt` (a choice of "eat") feeds `NeedContext.AteAt`. `MindKnown` carries the person's worked-out state (traits, needs, choice, recent, meal) over when it looks the same person up again (before, every 30 s threw it away). The ties in sight are walked once and matched against the near ids; the doer of a one-sided trouble has no foe in his victim. Choosing runs in its own `Safe("mind choosing")`; a line the caps held is written on the next look while the choice stands (`ChoiceTold`). The saloon habit is after supper (19) for the working man's trade groups (`SaloonMan`), piety -0.15; a drink only in the saloon; the store 7-20 (provisions, a cost, company); church a service (Sunday 9-12, Sunday and Wednesday 18-21), brief; stepping clear costs face (standing -0.1) with a flat +0.15; `greet` in the glad bonus; the night's sleep counts a quarter toward morning hunger. The player's place stands for the person's (as `MindNeeds`). Weights now: the need to the power 1.5 x 1.3 (was squared x 1.6), work's duty +0.2 (was 0.35), eat 0.9, rest 0.6 and only at home of an evening in a town, home's hunger 0.5, a man's saloon +0.12 after seven (19:00), hunger 0.08 + 0.10 an hour (the poor 0.125; was 0.16: everyone sat at 0.85+ before every meal). mindtest `ChoiceTownTest`: 200 residents, every hour 6-23 for 30 days where their last choice took them (108,000 choices): work 54-67% of a weekday morning and afternoon, rest and supper of an evening, church and prayer 32% of a Sunday morning and nobody working; no new choice over 26% at an hour outside meals and bed; the longest run of a brief action 5; the hothead check (twice a plain man's squaring up; measured 1.3) is printed as disputed, not counted (NIGHT.md, For Lex 4a). Needs that fall when the act serving them runs (after item 14): `NeedContext.Recent` (the shadow's `RecentActs`), `Eased(id, minutes)`: a rest eases rest two hours, a talk, a word with a friend or the saloon company, a prayer or church meaning, showing off standing, a drink or cards the vice's pull, each fading over its span; `MindNeeds` hands the person's `Recent` over. Next (not built): the first actions acted out from the catalogue behind MindLive and `MayDirect`.
## The ladder a quarrel climbs (night 2026-10-28, after item 14; roadmap 7)

- `Core/Escalation.cs` (read by the Director behind MindLive since item 16, built after the run closed on 2026-10-29; the next bullet). Lex's design (2026-10-27): an argument may go all the way to a killing when the people's inner lives, histories and surroundings lead there, true to 1899 (sudden quarrels over drink, honour, money or land, at close range, in a saloon at night between men with history; many towns banned guns inside the limits), and de-escalation by feeling too. `Rungs` words, shove, blows, draw, shoot; `Ways` down: apology, joke, backdown, walkoff, friend, drink, beaten (a fight ends with a man down), law, shot. `QuarrelContext`: the place and hour, who is drunk, who carries a gun (nobody draws what he doesn't carry), the law at hand, the town's gun rule, a friend at hand for either, who is watching, the pair's warmth and history, what it's over. `Heat(traits, side, ...)`: each rung harder than the last (0.30, 0.42, 0.10, 0.30), anger, assertiveness and risk up, self-control and compassion down (half of it at the gun rungs: a gun is mostly the surroundings), drink (the fists more than the trigger, and the trigger too), the matter (honour and a woman most; a look over nothing counts a little; half at the gun rungs), face before a crowd for everyone and more for the forward (fists only; a friend at the shoulder eggs on), the history, the warmth (a friend's words sting less and nobody draws on a friend; a foe's sting more), the aggrieved side pushing harder, the law at hand and the gun rule holding the law-abiding back by their deference, a church, the fair-fight rule (drawing on an unarmed man was murder in every witness's eyes; an armed one may fire back), a woman's quarrel stopping at the shove and a man's hand against her a tenth of his rate, and a soft cap so the hottest man leaves room for the rest to read. `Cool`: the pull down, self-control, compassion, honesty and anxiety up, anger and drink down, a friend or the law at hand, a church, warmth, backing down before a crowd costing. `Next`: the hotter pushes up by `Heat` damped by the other man's `Cool` (ties by the seed), or a friend steps in (past words, 20-35%; `By` says whose), or the cooler ends it by `WayDown` (the law breaking up a fistfight or a standoff in its sight, a fight ending with a man down, an apology for the kind and honest, a joke for the sociable, a drink in a saloon between people on terms, backing down for the anxious and meek, a walk-off for the rest); the shot is fired by the one who pushed the draw to it. `Run` the whole ladder, seeded by the pair and the moment; every seed carries the rung before the word, because `Voice.Hash` (FNV-1a) gives two seeds that differ only in their last character rolls a fixed distance apart (found by probe the same afternoon; a trap for every seeded roll in the add-on whose seeds differ only at the end). Reviewed by a subagent the same afternoon (`review-escalation.md`): twelve defects fixed (the damping by the less cool man, the shooter a coin flip, the fistfight's winner by gun heat, ties always to A, the law never ending a fistfight, whose friend, the other man's gun ignored, the nature outweighing the surroundings at the gun rungs, face only for the forward, no sex, unclamped warmth and history, the test's loose bands). mindtest `EscalationTest` (after the review: 20000 quarrels between the eight tempers: words only 52%, a fight 31%, a gun drawn 1.5%, a shot 0.40%; ended by walkoff 43%, backdown 15%, beaten 13%, friend 11%, apology 11%, joke 4%, drink 3%, shot 0%, law 0%; drunk armed men with history in a saloon at night shoot 10.8%; a friend at hand cuts fights from 46% to 30%). Built after the run closed (item 16): the next bullet.
- The Director reads the ladder (after the run closed, 2026-10-29, item 16; `Core/Scenes.Ladder.cs`, `Game/WestworldScript.Ladder.cs`; behind `[Mind] MindLive` and `[Mind] QuarrelLadder`, on, settings version 92). `Scenes.Quarrel(cast, seed, traitsA, traitsB, QuarrelContext, startRung, out steps)`: the ladder run from its start rung (`Escalation.RunFrom`; an argue from words, a brawl reason from the blows, `Scenes.BrawlStartRung` 2, so the shove and the fists are given by A, the reason's doer, and the ladder decides how it ends and whether a gun comes out; at 1 a brawl reason fought a third of the time, For Lex 18) becomes beats the Director already plays: the words open by the matter (`Opening`: honour, woman, money, land, drink, nothing, two openings each, A opens, B answers, A presses), each rung climbed is the pusher's line and act (`Up`: shove; fight 9000 and endfight; aim 300 by both, the pusher's voice "armed" only where the cast may draw, `SceneCast.Armed0/1` as BuildCast sets them: both armed, no law within 45 m, not a saloon; else the same words over a hand at the belt and the aim act only faces), and the way down is the cooler's (`Down`: apology with the other's answer and walk-off, joke, backdown with the other's "won" line, walk-off, the friend as the cast's third (approach, the word to his own man by `Beat.To` and faceone, the man's answer and walk-off; needs `SceneCast.Friend` 0 or 1, else a walk-off), a drink in a saloon, beaten (the winner's and the loser's lines on 0 and 1 after endfight, whatever the ladder's By: the fists decide; every other beat of the ladder's is `Beat.Follows`: the Director's endfight swap moves its Who and To with the people and leaves its names, so the man the ladder had speaking still speaks, by name; the friend calls his own man by name whatever the other does, `own`), the law in sight (holsters as it goes, both walk off); at the draw every other way holsters both first). The shot is never played: a run that ends "shot" plays the draw, then the one who would have fired thinks better of it and both holster and walk off, `Scene.LadderNote` says the ladder said a shot. `Scene.Kind` is the top rung (words or a shove: argue; blows: brawl; a gun drawn: standoff) and `Scene.Fight` the fists; `StartScene` notes a ladder scene by what happened in it (`noted`: a standoff that fought is a brawl to the ties, the memories, WorldEvents, the rests and the ledger's fought-with; an argue ended by an apology or a drink stood is an apology), so the ties, the memories, WorldEvents and RecordFates flow as for the fixed scenes; `Scene.Ladder` carries the steps. The Director's cap is 150 s for a ladder scene (90 for the rest: a full climb runs past 90), and its aim act puts the sidearm back in the hand first (after a fight the fists were the weapon in hand). `Scenes.MatterOf(saloon, drunk, seed, strangers)` (strangers: drink, honour or nothing, never money or land) and `MatterOfWorry(kind)` (the tests' shorthand; the game side maps a link by hand); `Reason.Matter` set where the reasons know it (drink and temper, the hothead in his cups: drink; a snap at the worst of a worry: nothing, since the other man isn't the worry's other end). Game: `LadderScene(old, first, second, doer, done, cast, seed, matter, people, now)` in both scene paths (the reason's after `BuildSceneFor`, the chance meeting's after `Scenes.Choose`), only for an argue or a brawl of two whose traits the shadow holds (`LadderTraits`: `MindKnown`, the traits worked out now if need be; else the fixed scene, `LADDER left to the fixed scene:` at Detail); the context: saloon/street/road, the clock hour, drink (`Someone.Drunk` or `InHisCups`), `IS_PED_ARMED` 4, the sexes, a lawman within 30 m, no town's gun rule (For Lex), the tie's warmth and (foes) count as history, the tie's Doer as the aggrieved (the trouble kinds only, item 17), the crowd within 12 m with the player, the matter (the reason's; a debt link between them is money only when A is the one owed, a feud honour, courting never a woman; else the place's usual), a friend within 10 m (`LadderFriend`: a ReasonCandidate with a warm tie to either, on his feet, with a clear view of A, no first name shared; the warmer side's; he joins `people` only when the run ends by him); `[Scenes] Standoffs` off disarms the context (nobody draws). The stand-up (`StandUpFor`) runs for a ladder scene only when the ladder found no friend (`s.Cast == 2`); AddMeet, the barkeep and the bystander run as before. Log `LADDER A & B (saloon at 22, A in drink, both armed, 3 watching, foes (2 quarrels before), over drink, from the blows): blows -> ended: beaten (the fists decide); played as a brawl[; guns stay holstered (a saloon)][; the ladder said a shot ...]`; F7 `TEST ladder:` (quarrels from the ladder by top rung and way, friends, guns held, shots said and not played, left to the fixed scene and why, the last). A fault logs once and the fixed scenes carry on (`_ladderFailed`). The Testing row `scene:ladder` (Street scenes, "A quarrel from the ladder (turns: words, blows)") goes through `FireScene` with `LadderScene(..., force: true)`, turn about an argue from words and a brawl from the blows (`_ladderTestTurn`), needing MindShadow but not MindLive; a test notes no ties (ticktest: 78 Testing actions). mindtest `LadderSceneTest` (4,000 seeded quarrels between the eight tempers, a fifth with a woman, a third with a stranger's cast: the kind is the top rung, Fight the fists, one shove, fight, endfight and two aims exactly where the ladder climbed, holsters only after a draw, the shot never an act and always noted, the friend's beats only with a third, the same seed the same scene, every line through PeriodWords and naming nobody a stranger's cast wasn't given; a brawl reason fights every time (from the blows rung) and an argue 12%, guns drawn 127, the shot said and not played 29, a friend stepped in 636; the iterator gives 130 distinct lines for linetools; MatterOfWorry and MatterOf). Known: the friend's approach act walks to A whoever his man is (the Director's approach for a third); the one-sided aim (an armed man over an unarmed one) can't happen through BuildCast's rule, so it is never seen; GunsBanned is false everywhere.

## Beliefs and the self v1 (night 2026-10-28, item 6; the mind design's step 10; Core, shadow)

- `Core/Beliefs.cs`: nine beliefs a person with a confidence 0..1 (the world "things are as they seem"; the town "the law here is for folk like me", "a decent place"; the player dangerous, kind, a liar; the self "an honest man", "I can look after myself", "I'm somebody here"). `Baseline` from traits, trade (`Needs.PoorTrade`/`WellOffTrade`), the Awakening's stage (read only) and the saved memories, never stored; `Move`: deltas from an appraised feeling, 0.05 at most for an everyday event (`EverydayMax`), up to 0.5 for a killing, the revived dead or one's own death (guilt chips honest; fear in the bold chips "look after myself"; the player's deeds move dangerous/kind/liar; a lawman chips "the law is for me"; the uncanny chips and the revived flip "as it seems"; a killing seen chips "a decent place"; shame and pride move "somebody"); `Holds` at 0.5; `Dissonance` (a memory against a belief at 0.7+: the honest or pious take it onto the self, the anxious or curious move town and others first, the rest rewrite the memory); `Describe`; `Disagreements` against `WakeStage` and `Regard`. The deltas live in `MindShadow` (`BeliefsOf`, `Believe`; 600 people, cleared with the rest). Game: `MindBelieve` after each appraisal (`WestworldScript.Mind.cs`; its own try/catch), a `MIND believes:` line only when a belief flips, with the held, the doubted and the disagreements ("doubts the world while the Awakening says asleep"; "Regard says fond, the belief says dangerous"); nothing drives the Awakening or Regard from it; F7 `TEST mind beliefs:`. Nothing saved, nothing tasked. mindtest `BeliefsTest`. Reviewed before the commit (a subagent, both lenses; `review-item6.md` in the session scratchpad): six defects fixed and the model points taken. The defects: one memory of the player's violence flipped "dangerous" on its own (+0.2 on a 0.3 baseline; each memory now moves a belief 0.08 at most and all of them 0.2, so no memory flips anything alone); flips the baseline caused (a memory come or gone, the Awakening moving) were invisible (the held bits are kept per person in `BeliefState`, the first look sets them, and a flip is attributed to the feeling or to "a memory or the Awakening"); the failure guard re-armed itself and F7 hid the failure (`_mindBeliefFailed`, logged once, F7 says FAILED); the belief counters survived a rewind (reset in `MindShadowForget`); "the law here is for folk like me" never moves in play (no appraisal carries a lawman as the doer) and the revived flip is reachable offline only (the revive path notes no Happening, and a new WorldEvents record is against the brief): both said in the header and here, for Lex. The judgement points: belief perseverance (a chip against a belief held or doubted with confidence weighs less, down to 0.2 at 0.9; a killing seen or the revived dead aren't argued with); the deltas clamped to -1..1 (forty shoves can't latch a belief for good); beliefs kept up as well as worn (a good turn adds a little to "a decent place" and "as it seems", pride to "an honest man"); credit sticks more than blame for the bold (shame -0.03 against pride +0.06); anger at the player feeds "dangerous" at half weight (it says hostile more than dangerous); a standing table by trade group in place of Needs' money list (`StandingOf`: the gentry, the law and soldiers up; hard-looking men, gamblers, the saloon's women and trappers down) and the header says trade stands in for what 1899 really sorted people by; the dissonance roads at z > 1 so most rewrite (6 of 8 typical tempers); `Road` named on the flip line ("a memory against it they'd rewrite"); the believes line after the feeling's own; `Beliefs.Fill` into a kept array, a static `Cap`, the trade group cached on `MindWho`, `Describe` only for a line that will be written; the MIND believes line held by the 20 counts as held, not lost. Known (the reviewer): enRAGE's townsfolk have no trade (standing 0) and Regard reads only their card. The dissonance road applied to memory's reading (after item 14, behind MindLive): `Beliefs.Slant` (one who rewrites reads his own blows as deserved, the player's kindness as a trick, his violence as provoked, a death seen as outsiders'), `MindMemory.Slant` (transient), `Engine.SlantOf` at every read, `Minds.Line`'s `slant|*` pools (sixteen lines), `MemoriesSlanted` in F7; mindtest `SlantTest`. Beliefs in the language request (after item 14): `LanguageRequest.Beliefs` (three at most, the player's only when the thought is about him; a doubted world as "no longer sure..."), the prompt's `Holds:` line, the cache key. Next (not built): the Awakening read from "as it seems" once Lex lifts the read-only rule.

## Levels of detail and the frame budget (night 2026-10-28, item 7; the mind design's step 7; [Mind] MindLevels, off)

- `Core/Levels.cs`: a scheduler keyed by the body's handle: level 0 near (within 20 m and on screen, at most 20; a look every 750 ms: identity, traits, needs, the MIND traits line, and the appraisal and choosing as before), level 1 about (within 120 m; every 10-30 s each: identity, traits, needs, no line), level 2 away (the saved residents of each town, once a game day a town, twenty a frame: a worry ended, ties older than a week cooled, a life event, worked out from id and day by `AwayDay` and only counted; one `MIND away:` line a town a day), level 3 untouched. `Place` with hysteresis (3 m past a ring's edge, two seconds out of view before the near ring lets go), `Settle` (the unseen step out, forgotten after a minute; the near ring's cap demotes the furthest), `Next` (the longest-waiting due person at a level, null once the frame's 200 microseconds are spent: `Deferred`), `Spend`, `Starved`, `AwayDue`, `Clear`. Game: `MindLevelsTick` in `WestworldScript.Mind.cs` runs in place of the old scan when `[Mind] MindLevels` is on (settings version 89; off, exactly the old path): `MindScanLevels` once a second places everyone within 130 m and makes `_mindNear` from the near ring (nearest first, as before), then each frame the near visits, the about visits (`MindAbout`), the appraisal step, the choosing step and the away pass (`MindAwayStep`) while the frame has room, each timed with `Stopwatch` and charged. F7 `TEST mind levels:`. Nothing saved, nobody tasked. mindtest `LevelsTest` (1,000 people along a street 400 by 100 m, the player walking through them at 60 fps for ten minutes: the near ring never past 20, no flicker at the boundaries, every frame under the budget but for the one job that may finish past it, nobody near waiting past 1.5 s or about past a minute, the away pass once a day a town, the record capped and cleared). Reviewed before the commit (a subagent, a senior game developer's read with a Python port of the scheduler to see the numbers; `review-item7.md` in the session scratchpad): the defects fixed: the near ring's grace off screen was anchored on the level change, not the last sight (a man glanced away from every other second flipped near/about every scan; `InViewAt` now, two seconds), the ring's cap ping-ponged (the 21st nearest demoted at the settle and promoted at the next scan, two level changes and two visits a second each; a full ring is entered only when nearer than its furthest by the 3 m margin, and the overflow waits at about without a level change), `Interval` built a string per about visit (the interval is fixed on the entry from an integer mix of the handle), the away day allocated a Person and two strings per resident (one hash now, InnerLife's own arc arithmetic), the cache of who is who was wiped past 400 handles (sized for 20 m; with 130 m it would have thrown away every choice and need every minute or two: pruned of handles unseen for a minute instead), and the test's checks that couldn't fail (the cap, the budget) replaced by ones that can (a glance trace, a full-ring trace, a per-person change count, the longest wait measured every frame, the scheduler's own time, a run with a 50 us budget that binds). Its judgement points taken: `_mindNear` is everyone within 20 m on screen or not (the near ring is only who gets the 750 ms looks), so the appraisal, the choosing, F7 and the cards read what the old scan gave them; the appraisal and the choosing run before the about ring's looks; lateness counted at the settle once per lateness, the put-off only when someone was due, the clock going back counts as due; a nobody in the about ring is visited half as often; the ties older than a week counted in one walk a town pass; the towns listed once a game day and polled once a second; the scan timed and in F7 with "on screen" said plainly; the panel label. Left (the reviewer's, as judgement): the ring walk in `Next` is one pass of the ring per call (a few hundred compares a frame), not an ordered queue; the screen test alone, no line-of-sight native, as the cards use. The about ring chooses too (after item 14): each about-ring visit (10-30 s) that finds the person's needs works out their choice from them (`MindChooseFor(w, h, now, quiet: true)`: the same scoring, nobody "in sight" out of view, once a minute at most, no line; the near ring's look writes `MIND would:` when they come near and the choice stands, `ChoiceTold`), under the choosing's own `Safe` and the frame budget (scored out of doors with nobody by: the player's room and crowd aren't theirs 120 m off); F7 `TEST mind levels:` counts "about-ring choices"; `TEST mind choices:`' last choice is someone near, never the about ring's. Off, exactly the old path. Next (not built): the away pass writing a concern that ended (it only counts: the brief's "nothing written").

## Manners of 1899 (night 2026-10-28, item 10)

- Six small rules, each its own commit, from the daytime review's list of what was plainly wrong for 1899: `Ties.Friends` (warmth 2 or more, or warm with two scenes together) is who grieves a death, condoles, tends and takes up a grudge, where one kind word used to make a friend for life; the modern phrases went ("You're welcome" sarcastic, "Take care", "I'll be seeing you") and a friend's condolence doesn't "set a place at the table"; a respectable woman neither drinks herself drunk in a saloon nor stands a round (`Vices.DrinkWhy`, `Scenes.RoundWhy` by sex and trade; the saloon's own women excepted), never spits (the cut instead: `Passing.For`, `Sights.For`), and standing up to a bully shames him ("Shame on you") while he answers her as a lady; a named person is he or she, never they (`{he}`/`{him}`/`{his}` slots, `Ties.Pronouns`, the other's sex by name when nothing better is known: `Residents.IsWomansName`, `Scenes.IsWomansName`); strangers aren't on first-name terms (`SceneCast.Knows`, from a tie or the same home town on the game side: a stranger is "mister" or "ma'am", and "the man"/"the lady" when spoken of, `Scenes.NThird`; the tests leave `Knows` null); the old men have twelve talks and the sweethearts eight quarrels, so no two pairs repeat word for word. The golden is unchanged (the harness's MindTest and TroubleTest make their friends with two scenes). Left for Lex: the Sabbath's prayers and kneeling, the callout's nerve, the fall's picks, the plea with no answer, "Mrs./Miss + surname".

## The reactions on the five-step mapping (night 2026-10-28, item 13; the mind design's step 5, second half; behind [Mind] MindLive)

- `Core/Reactions.Mapped.cs`: with MindLive on, the one spoken to in a street scene takes the line as an event (`EventOf`: a threat or an angry word a challenge, a jeer or a shaming an insult, a cheer or thanks a kindness, a plea a beggar's need; the everyday nothing), appraises it by their traits (`Appraisal.Of`), the rules of 1899 decide what shows (`Display.Shows`; masked plays nothing: the thought alone), and the catalogue picks the gesture (`Catalogue.For`, the urge's before the feeling's), read back by `KindOfGesture` as one of today's eight reaction kinds so the same clips play; the slam's hurt stays the body's. The catalogue carries the reactions' clips as proven gestures (the sets found in game 2026-10-26; a head-shake and the hurt reel seen playing). Game: `MindReaction` in `WestworldScript.Reactions.cs` feeds the listener's traits from the shadow's cache with the place, the people near, a lawman within 25 m, drink and the wake stage; the `REACT` line says "the mind: <feeling> (<strength>, urge <urge>), shows <what>"; nobody the shadow knows yet falls back to the old table; off, exactly `Reactions.Listener`. The reaction carries the pair's tie (`SceneCast.Warmth`/`Tied` from `CastWarmth`, `Director.Reaction.Known`/`Warm`: the speaker is "known" with the tie's warmth), and a masked feeling holds still for the look's length (`TASK_STAND_STILL`), so the swallow is seen. mindtest `MappedReactionsTest` (15 tones x 8 tempers x sex x street/saloon/church: a hothead bristles at a threat and a coward flinches, a proud woman mocked in the street keeps it off her face while a timid one looks down, a cold man takes a cheer with nothing and a loyal one nods, church hushes a hothead's anger, the old table untouched). Reviewed before the commit (a subagent, both lenses; `review-item13.md` in the session scratchpad): the defects fixed: the kind was read back from a clip's name, so a woman's anger with "confront" played the head-shake (women's bristle clips are named "dismiss"): the kind now comes from the urge (confront bristles, getting away flinches, a turned back dismisses, hiding looks down, repaying nods, watching looks puzzled, rest shrugs) and else the feeling, the catalogue deciding only whether something proven exists; "turned" (fear shown as bluster, a timid man in a saloon) bristles instead of flinching; shame's "avoid" looks down (eyes down and away), not a flinch; the reactions' clips in the catalogue are "set found in game, not yet seen played" (only the head-shake was seen), so the catalogue's counts stay honest; `LawmanNear` tests distance before the lawman test; the slam's hurt and a tone the mind doesn't name (the everyday, the odd) go to the old table, so the shrug, nod and puzzled look are not lost; the scenario check comes before the kind; the mind's part has its own catch (a fault logs once and the table carries on) and checks the cache's model against the body (a reused handle); the sex is the ped's; "softened" plays one step down (anger a head-shake, fear a puzzled look). Its judgement points taken: "won" is a pratfall (the loss his own), "no"/"tut"/a sharp answer a slight (no threat), "sorry" a kindness, "help" a beggar's ask; "neighbours" only in the listener's own town. Left for Lex (under For Lex): the plain man's fear at a threat is masked in public under the 1899 rules, so he does nothing where the old table had him bristle or flinch (the "holds still" gesture class the reviewer suggests); a Loyal man mocked feels mild anger by the appraisal and shakes his head where the old table had him look down.


## Memory v1, episodes that fade and get retold (daytime 2026-10-27, item 11; Core only)

- `Core/Episodes.cs`; nothing in the game calls it yet. An `Episode` is one person's memory of one recorded happening (`WorldEvents`): its key (kind, doer, target, time: the same thing however it reached them), the doer and target as they know them, town and place, when it happened and when they learned it, how (saw, heard, told), from whom, how many tellings away (`Removes`), how strongly it was felt (`Felt`, from the appraisal) and how sure they are (`Sure`: 1 seen, 0.7 heard). `Strength` (two traces, Lex's choice 2026-10-27): (0.35 + 0.65 x Felt) x [(1 - Kept) x a fast trace halving over a half-life that grows with the feeling (12 h for nothing felt, about 2 days at 0.5, 10 days at 1; their own part doubles it) + Kept x a lasting trace 20 times slower (`SlowTimes`)], times (0.5 + 0.5 x Sure). `Kept` (`KeptOf`) = 0.05 + 0.45 x Felt squared, half for something only heard, a quarter for something told, at most 0.9; `Rehearse` adds 0.05 each time it's told (and, when wired, thought of). Together the two give forgetting's long tail with no floor (the emotional enhancement of memory: arousing events consolidate). `Retold` (serial reproduction, Bartlett 1932, Allport and Postman 1947): one remove further, 0.8 as sure, 0.6 as strongly felt, the doer's name lost to "somebody" 25% + 10% a remove, the place moved to the street 12%. `Episodes.Book` (in memory, never saved; 12 a person, the weakest going; 600 people, the first in going; `Clear` for a rewind): `Add` (a known key keeps the surer version), `Forget` (under 0.05), `WorthTelling(teller, listener)` (the strongest at 0.2 or more under three days old, never told to that listener before even after forgetting and relearning it (a told set, capped), never their own doing told back to them, nothing they know), `Tell`. `Describe` in plain words ("heard from Mary that Earl Platt and Hollis Barr came to blows in the saloon in Valentine, yesterday") for the log and the language layer's `Memories`. mindtest `EpisodesTest`: a scuffle seen (felt 0.15) is gone in about 75 game hours, a rumour told on in about 4 1/2 days, a killing seen (0.95) is at 0.44 a month on, 0.21 at six months, 0.10 at a year and gone by two; told five times, a memory is kept longer than one never told; one's own part lasts longer; a chain of eight tellings ends 0.17 sure; a town of 200 with 400 ties for 20 days and 60 happenings: nothing told twice to the same person or back to the one it was about, at most 12 held, names kept at one telling 55% and at three or four 23%, a happening known a day on by about 100 of 200, three days 64, a week 14. Next (not built): an episode for each appraisal in the shadow (`MindAppraiseStep`), telling when two people with a tie meet (the nods, the scenes, real-voice talk), the memories in the language request, and retold episodes as thoughts ("heard from Abe..."). Reviewed the same day (a subagent; fixed in the follow-up commit): an episode keeps the happening's own ids (`WhoDid`, `WhoTo`) whatever the telling lost, so nobody is told of their own doing, even of something only heard; only heard (a shout, a shot) knows neither name; a newcomer weaker than everything a full person holds doesn't stay (before, it pushed out the weakest, however strong); the forgotten leave no empty places under the 600 cap; a NaN feeling is none; a thing seen and felt hard lasts (now by the two traces, which replaced a floor the same evening); retelling sharpens as well as levels (a lost doer is "somebody" or, half the time, "a stranger"; a quarrel told as blows 8% of tellings), and the listener's own stake can set how strongly it's felt (`Retold`'s `listenerFelt`, from their appraisal, when the caller has it); `Describe` words passby, scorn, a flirt and the encounters, and "an hour ago". mindtest adds: the doer of something heard never offered it, a weak rumour never evicting a strong memory, the cap with 700 people, nothing left after forgetting, a killing seen not forgotten in four months.

## Memory v1 on the saved memories (night 2026-10-28, item 4; the mind design's step 8; behind [Mind] MindLive)

- `Core/Minds.Memory.cs`, `Core/Minds.Told.cs`, on `Core/Minds.cs`'s `MindMemory` (the saved twelve a person), reusing `Episodes.cs`'s ideas (which stays unwired as the maths' reference; Lex to say whether it goes). Seven optional fields on `MindMemory`, `EmitDefaultValue = false`: `td` Told (times this person told it on), `fr` From (who told them, an id), `rm` Removes (tellings away from someone who saw it), `ln` LearnedAt (when a told thing reached them; 0: At), `px`/`py`/`pz` the spot (all 0: none); a transient `Many` (not saved) for a reading where two became three. An old save loads without them, an old reader passes over them: mindtest `StoreLoadTest` writes them and reads them back, strips them to the old form, loads that with defaults and writes it back byte for byte. No new saved collection, no new WorldEvents record, caps unchanged (`Minds.Max` 12; told things weigh 0.6 in `Keep`, so they go first). With MindLive off nothing here runs: no telling, no spot, no drift (`MemoryTest` runs the town both ways; the engine's counters stay 0).
- How sure (`Minds.Sure`, derived, never stored): did 1, saw 0.9, heard (a shout, word of a death) 0.7, told 0.7 x 0.8 a remove, +0.03 a telling (the teller firms up as he tells it, whatever happens to the content). Shown in `DescribeMemory` (the log, F7); nothing reads it yet.
- Retelling (`Engine.RememberScene` -> `Retell`, both ways, after a talk scene: smalltalk, gossip, comfort, cheer, round, hosts, haggle (`Minds.TalkKind`); and one way at a nod with a word, after item 14: `Engine.RememberPassing(speaker, other)` from `NodWord` in `WestworldScript.Passing.cs` when the greeting played and the other was there (not the Testing row's nod, `NodRun.Test`), the speaker's freshest thing by the same rules, logged `MIND told in passing:`, counted in `MemoriesToldInPassing`; a greeting carries news only some of the time (a warm tie one time in two, an acquaintance one in five, seeded by the pair and the hour) and what's heard is half-heard (the copy's strength 0.7 of a talk's telling), from the review of the same night; real-voice talk ends as smalltalk): `Minds.WorthTelling` picks the teller's freshest strong memory (strength faded from when they learned it, told things x0.6, at least 15; under three days old; not talk, a rescue, the player's flirting or a death (deaths travel by `RememberDeathHeard`); never their own doing (`Role` doer); never the listener's own business or back to the one who told it; not something the listener knows by `Key`; not told to this listener before (an in-memory set `teller>listener>key`, capped 20,000, cleared with `ForgetThinking`); news of someone the listener has a tie with weighs x1.5). `Minds.Retold`: a heard memory from the teller, one remove on, Feel x0.6, Strength x0.7 (at least 10), `LearnedAt` now, the same `Key`, never a spot; a thing the teller was in himself gets him into the cast, doer first (a lift's victim stays the one it was done to). Only between people who already have a saved state (`Store.Find`; never `Store.Get`); the telling counts (`Told`++, `MemoriesTold`) only once the copy stayed under the cap. Log (Detail) `MIND told: Abe Cobb tells Ruth Platt of told by Abe: brawl (Abe and Hollis) in Valentine, 2 h ago (sure 0.56, 1 removes)`.
- Drift, worked out when read (`Minds.Drifted(m, readerId, now, known, foe)`: the saved record itself when nothing has drifted, else a transient copy; nothing written back): for a told or heard thing, one draw per reader and remove (`Key|reader|Removes`) with thresholds rising by the day, so the same person has the same reading each time and what has drifted stays drifted: the unnamed doer becomes someone the reader already holds against (a hostile tie's living other, never one already in it nor the teller: Allport and Postman's assimilation to prejudice), a told slight grows (brushoff -> argue -> brawl: sharpening), two become three (`Many`: `Fill` says "{a}, {b} and another"); a thing they saw or did never changes who did it; the player's deeds (`AboutYou`) never drift (Rumours grow those). Gist: past twice the half-life a memory keeps its kind and feeling and strangers' names become "somebody"; a tie's and the dead's (`KnownId`) never go. A told deed never counts as having seen the player do it (`SawYouViolent`, `YourKillGrieved`: `From == null`).
- Places: `Engine.NoteEvent` (MindLive on) back-fills the record's spot onto the memories of its people written in the same game minute (`Minds.FillSpot`: the scene's kind as Sub or Kind, only kinds with here words, a deed by "player" in the cast, a death by the dead; `NoteHappened` writes the memories before the record, so the record fills them in); told memories and small talk never get one; old memories have none and are never pulled by place. `Minds.NearHere` (15 m) gives the "here" candidate in `Candidates` (the nearest spot to the player, who is near whoever is thinking), pull +15, its own `here|<group>` words (`HerePools`, 36 lines).
- The words: `ToldPools` (54 lines by group, `{from}` the teller's first name, `{a}`/`{b}` the two as the teller had them, `{you}` the player as this person sees him), `ToldLine`; `HereLine`; `DescribeMemory`. All through `Language.Check`, linetools, overlap 118 and fuzzy 216 (several reworded).
- F7 `TEST mind shadow:` ends "memories told on N, placed N, drifted readings N, slanted readings N" (told on N says "(N in passing)"). Offline: mindtest `MemoryTest` (a town of 200 for 30 days: two halves that never talk across and twenty tieless; 90 happenings in one half, talk along 400 ties; nothing told across the halves or to the tieless; day one's first happening known by about 43 a day on, 22 a week on; every hearer less sure than the teller; nothing seen drifted within a day; about half the readers drift a day-old slight told three removes on; the spot on the memories of those who were there and pulled within 15 m; nobody over 12; MindLive off nothing). Reviewed by a subagent in two passes before the commit (the teller in his own story's cast, deed and death spots, no spots for small talk, cumulative drift, the foe guard, the lift's victim, told deeds not seen, the language). Not built (Lex's calls in NIGHT.md): the teller's slant by temper, drift passed down the chain, a told thing's spread past a day, Sure driving anything; telling in real-voice talk is the talk's own smalltalk retelling already.

## The language layer, offline half (daytime 2026-10-27, item 9; the mind design's step 9)

- `Core/Language.cs`: what a local model would be asked and the check every answer must pass; nothing loads a model or opens a connection, and nothing in the game calls it yet. The mind decides (the feeling, whom it's about, whether the face shows it, the place); the model would only word it, and the written lines stay the fallback. `LanguageRequest` (built from the mind's state, never free text: name, sex, age, trade, town, place, hour, year, weather, `Traits.Words`, `Mood.Words`, the feeling and its strength, whom (the player as this person sees him), what shows, the pressing need, the worry, one or two memories in plain words, first names in sight, the written line it must not copy; `Names()` every name the answer may use). `Language.Brief` (Lex's writing brief as the model is told it: short fragments, one thing noticed, words of the time, no names not given, what's really felt even when the face hides it, never games or characters, at most 4 sentences and 110 characters, the thought alone). `Language.Prompt(request)` (the state in words; the same request, the same text). `Language.Check(request, answer)` (null or the first reason: empty; too long; more than one line; quotes or markup; a symbol or emoji; more than 4 sentences; a name before it; breaks the frame (npc, video game, simulation, "as an ai", "a character in"...: not "game" or "player", which are words of 1899); a modern word (`PeriodWords.ModernIn`); a name it wasn't given (a capitalised word mid-sentence not in `Names()` nor the common ones: God, Mother, the days and months, Mister...); copies the written line). mindtest `LanguageTest`: three requests (a saloon at night, a church in 1907, the wilds), the prompt carries each field and nothing it wasn't given; 8 hand-written good answers pass and 16 bad ones each fail for their own reason; every written thought line in `MindThoughts` passes the same check (the fallback must); every modern word is caught and period lines that look modern ("no way round it", "a chilling wind", "move on, there") aren't. Night run 2026-10-28 (item 8's leftovers, `Core/Language.Cache.cs`): `Language.CacheKey` (the same person in the same situation asks once: the feeling and whom, what shows, the need, the worry, the place, the part of the day (`DayPart`), the weather, the mood, the year; not the names in sight or the memories' words), `Language.Resolve` (the fallback rule: an answer later than `LateMs` 2 s, one failing `Check`, or none gives the written line, `LanguageAnswer.From` written with `Why`; a cached answer never late), and mindtest `LanguageCacheTest` writes fifty requests from the vignettes' town to `daytime\language
equests.txt`. `Core/Language.Request.cs` (after item 14): `Language.RequestFor(...)` builds the request from a person's shadow state (traits, mood, the feeling with most left or the pressing need, the first concern, the two strongest memories, four names in sight), mindtest `LanguageRequestTest`. The game side (after item 14, read-only): `Engine.LanguageRequestFor(p, traits, needs, concerns, near, town, place, hour, year, weather, beliefs)` in `Core/Engine.MindLive.cs` takes the shadow's strongest feeling and whom it's about as the live thought has them (`WhomOf`), the mind's own line as the fallback not to copy (`MindSay` with its `request` flag, which skips the need's one-in-four roll; none with MindLive off); a feeling about nobody (one's own shame, the uncanny) is about nobody, not "a stranger", the mood from the shadow and the saved memories; the Testing row `mind:request` (`MindRequestFire` in `WestworldScript.Mind.cs`) builds it for the nearest known person from the cached `MindWho` (traits, needs, concerns, the beliefs as `Beliefs.Of` of the baseline and what the appraisals moved (`Shadow.BeliefsPeek`: a read-only row makes no belief record), the first names in sight looked up afresh, never their own, and only faces they'd call by name: a tie or the same home town, since a stranger is "mister") and writes the cache key and the prompt to the log as `LANGUAGE` lines (not MIND: the 20 a minute are the shadow's own); F7's shadow line counts the requests written; mindtest checks the engine's request both ways. The game half (2026-10-29, after Lex approved the GPU setup; the next bullet). Reviewed the same day (a subagent; fixed in the follow-up commit): a name at a sentence's start is caught when it's one of the names the add-on gives people (`Residents.IsName`, `Scenes.IsName`) and wasn't given; "Mr." and "Mrs." don't end a sentence; frame words match their plurals ("NPCs", "characters in a story") and no longer include words of 1899 ("prompt", "assistant", "a character in", "this story"; "game" and "player" never did); the common words take in the faith (Almighty, Sabbath, Providence), titles (Colonel, Judge), the peoples a person of 1899 would name (Pinkertons, Sicilians, Yankees) and the map's places and the country round it (Texas, Lemoyne, Saint Denis); a copy is 80% of the written line's words (`Alike`), so dropping a sentence is still a copy; "…" is three dots; the patterns are built once; `Brief(request)` gives the request's year and never says "games" (a card game is a thing to think about). mindtest: 23 good answers (13 of them 1899 English that only looks modern) and 23 bad ones; all 132 written lines.
- The game half (2026-10-29; `Core/Language.Client.cs`, `Game/WestworldScript.Language.cs`; `[Mind] Language` off, `LanguageUrl` http://127.0.0.1:11434, `LanguageModel` llama3.1:8b, settings version 94; the model runs through Ollama on Lex's PC, pulled with `ollama pull llama3.1:8b`; the CUDA libraries for faster-whisper (roadmap 14) went into the voice-test venv the same day: cuBLAS 12.9, cuDNN 9.27). Core: `LanguageStore` on the Engine (`LanguageAnswers`): answers kept by cache key (capped 2,000), the asks waiting (`LanguageAsk`: key, person, request, the written line, when; `Ask` queues a key once and never while kept or waiting, the queue capped at 24 with the newest dropped), `Next` for the worker, `Settle(ask, answer, ms)`: `Language.Tidy` (outer quotes off, the first line only, a "Thought:" label off) then `Resolve`; an answer that passes `Check` is kept whether or not it came within `LateMs` (the key is the situation; late counts for this showing), one that fails is counted by reason (`HeldWhy`), none at all counts as failed and the key may be asked again; `Shown` counts; `Status` for F7; `Language.RequestBody` (Ollama's /api/generate: the brief as the system prompt, the prompt, no streaming, temperature 0.9, 60 tokens, keep_alive 30m), `JsonString`, `ParseResponse`/`JsonField` (a flat object's string field, the escapes undone), all by hand: no JSON library is referenced. `Engine.LanguageSwap` (the game side's hand) and `Worded(p, source, written)` in `MindSayImpl` at both sites (the feeling's line, the need's): with Language on, the kept line for this situation or the written one; a fault logs once and the written lines carry on. Game: `LanguageStep` once a second from the tick (sets the hand, starts the worker when the setting is on), `LanguageStart`/`LanguageStop` (a below-normal background thread, joined at shutdown), `LanguageSwap` (the person's cached `MindWho` by id, `MindRequestOf(w, handle, now, out why)`, the Testing row's builder made shared, the key, `Cached` or `LanguageAsk` and null), `LanguageAskAhead(w, id, now)` after `Shadow.Feel` in `MindAppraiseOne` (so the line is ready when the thought comes), the worker `LanguageWork` (the server checked once by its model list `/api/tags`, again every minute while it fails; POST through `HttpWebRequest`, 8 s; five failures in a row back off a minute; logs through `Log`, which is locked; no game native), the Testing row `mind:ask` (People: the nearest known person asked whatever the setting, a key suffix so a kept answer doesn't short-circuit it, the answer to the log at Detail: `LANGUAGE answer for X in N ms: "..." -> kept | held (why)`), F7 `TEST language:`. mindtest `LanguageClientTest` (the JSON out and back, the tidying, the store's keeping, holding, lateness, the cap, the status) and `mindtest.exe model [passes] [url] [model]` (`ModelRun`: the fifty saved situations asked of the live server, ten passes for five hundred thoughts, every answer through the check, the pass rate and the reasons, the times, `daytimeanguagenswers.txt` and the passes as literals in `answers.cs` for the overlap and fuzzy checks). The first measurement (500 thoughts, the brief as written: a character limit alone): 494 ms each, 7% passed, 463 held as too long, the passes florid. So `Language.Brief` now gives a word cap it can count (`MaxWords` 20, "two or three clipped sentences", "no poetry, no similes") and three thoughts of the right shape (`Language.Examples`, none of them a written line: the offline check runs every written line through `Check`), `Check` holds a copy of an example ("copies an example", `Alike` 0.8) and more than `MaxWords` + 4 words ("too many words"), and `RequestBody` allows 40 tokens (was 60); a hundred thoughts after that: 240 ms each, 85% passed (13 too long, 2 a name not given). The setting stays off until the five-hundred-thought measurement with the new brief and the overlap and fuzzy checks on its passes say otherwise (NIGHT.md item 21, For Lex 24).

## Line tools (daytime 2026-10-27, item 8)

- `Core/PeriodWords.cs`: `Modern` (words and phrases certainly not of 1899: "okay", "awesome", "stressed", "my bad", "no problem", the sarcastic "must be nice"...; a phrase ending in a letter must end a word, and "my bad" or "no way" only as the phrase, so "my bad joke" and "no way round it" pass) and `Doubtful` (old enough but rare in 1899 speech, or a later sense: folks, fella, nice, hello, guy, kid, yeah, gonna, nope...: for Lex to judge, never a failure); `ModernIn`, `DoubtfulIn`. Shared by the line tools and the language layer's check.
- The line tools (`daytime\harness\linetools`, git-excluded like the other harnesses; `linetools.exe [fail]`, below-normal priority): every line in Core, from static `string[]` and `string[][]` fields, the `string[]` and `List<string>` values of static dictionaries, the `All`/`Lines`/`All*Lines` iterators, and the string literals in Core's source that read as sentences (inline `Voice.Pick` pools; not log lines, keys or code names): 5,611 lines in 2,286 pools. It reports (1) certainly-modern words (each hit; `fail` exits 1 on any) and doubtful ones counted with examples, (2) the same line in different pools and near-duplicates (word sets 80% alike, 5 words or more, across pools, not a test iterator's filled-in line against its own source), (3) the most used openings and first words. First run: no modern words left after the fixes; the doubtful mostly "folks" (90), "fella" and "nice" (14 each); 13 exact duplicates across pools kept on purpose (shared mood frames, short replies, two kinds of store) and 5 near ones (second and third person variants, a frame and its filled form). Fixed from it: grief's "last thing I said to {who} was about the weather" (it was already a memory line), the peacemaker remark (it was already a plea line), the restless courtship reply (it was the player's flirt line), and both "Must be nice" lines. Run it over every new batch of lines along with the overlap checks, after rebuilding it: it reads the static pools from the add-on DLL in its own bin, so a stale build checks the old lines (found in the night of 2026-10-28; `daytime/check-all.sh` rebuilds and runs it). Reviewed the same day: the period words match between letter boundaries (so "okay;" and "--okay--" are caught); phrases with an honest 1899 use ("deal with it", "screw up your nerve", "sucks his pipe", "the bank's closure", "rip off the bandage", "stressed") are doubtful, not modern; O.K. (in American use long before 1899) is doubtful; "no worries", "whatever.", "seriously?" and "pissed off" are modern.

## The people ledger (0.6.3, step 1 of the mind design)

- What already existed: one `PersonState` per person of every kind (residents, enRAGE's townsfolk by "folk:" key, its records by id). Step 1 adds our own facts, starting with deaths; traits and mirrors of enRAGE's people are step 2.
- Core: `Core/Deaths.cs` (`DeathEntry`: id, name, kind resident|folk|record, game minute, town and place, how, by player|self|another|unknown and who, in view, told to enRAGE; `DeathLedger`: `Note` takes the same person within 120 game minutes as one death and fills in what the first note lacked, `Of`, `ByOf`, `KnockedOut` (enRAGE's `LooksKnockedOut`: incapacitated, its gun-butt test, weapon group 2685387236 or 308416707), `Describe`, `Counts`, cap 1500). `Store.Deaths` with `DeathOf` (an index rebuilt after each change) and `NoteDeath`; `Fix` makes it for old saves; `Rewind` drops deaths after the cut, un-marks the residents who died after it (never a post's keeper or the fled) and passes deaths told to enRAGE to `RewoundShares`. `Engine.NoteDied(entry)` is the one way in and marks a resident `Dead`. `Engine.HasDied`: townsfolk, ours or enRAGE's; a saved resident, their `Dead` flag alone (the posts code clears it when a keeper turns out only shot down, `HolderOf`), an unsaved one, ours; enRAGE's records, enRAGE only, since it brings some back on purpose.
- Game: `Game/WestworldScript.Ledger.cs`. `LedgerStep` runs from `Tick04` before the player-control check, under its own `Safe` ("people ledger"), every 500 ms, never in a story mission. `KnownAt` looks a handle up once (again if the model changes, or every 10 s while it's nobody): a bound resident who keeps no counter, a record, or a named townsperson; never a story model, a child or a counter model. A known handle the game calls dead is noted once, unless `KnockedOutWhy` says out cold (then it's looked at again next time). `LedgerNote` takes how from the cause of death (`Weapons.Phrase`), by from the source of death, the town and whether it was in view. Callers: the scan, `ResidentsPrune` (which now skips the knocked out; before, a resident knocked out in a brawl was marked dead), `DespairDied` (no knocked-out test: they're known dead), hermits, gatherings, and `VacatePost` (the successor path only; keepers who `survive` never enter the ledger).
- Shared with enRAGE ([Mind] `ShareWithEnrage`, on, settings version 58): 4 s after a townsperson's death, if enRAGE's `Acquaintance.Dead` is still false, `Bridge.MarkFolkDead` sets it and `_peopleDirty`. A shared death that a reload undid is taken back 30 s later if enRAGE's own save point (`[SavePoints] AutoRestore`, which restores people.json) didn't already. `despair:rolled2` is not rewound.
- The overlap check flags `'KnockedOutByGunButt'` (enRAGE's method name, which the bridge needs to call it), accepted like `'_worldCatchingUp'`: code names, not text. The knocked-out probe (0.6.3; Lex beat a man meaning to knock him out and couldn't tell whether he died): anyone within 60 m the game has down (dead or incapacitated) whom the rule calls only knocked out is logged `LEDGER out cold: name (why; the game's incapacitation time left N)` and followed: `LEDGER came to:` (got up, after N s), `LEDGER died where they lay:`, or `LEDGER lost` (gone from the world while down); F7 `TEST ledger:` counts them. The game has a real knocked-out state (`TASK_KNOCKED_OUT`, `IS_PED_INCAPACITATED`, `_GET_INCAPACITATION_TIME_REMAINING`, `REVIVE_INJURED_PED`); whether a man beaten down by fists ever gets up is what the probe will show. Log `LEDGER died: name (kind, how, by whose hand, town, in view)`, `LEDGER told enRAGE:`, `LEDGER took back`; F7 `TEST ledger:` (kept by kind, noted, told, taken back, waiting, known people within 50 m, the last). Offline: ticktest `LedgerTest`.
- Seen before the ledger: enRAGE marked Gabriel Ridley (the wall) and Alonzo Draper (the knife) dead itself; Pope McCrae (16:11) is still alive in its people.json. If enRAGE brings him back, he's a case for the revived dead (step 10). Not yet seen in game: the ledger itself.

## The mind in shadow mode (daytime 2026-10-27, the mind design steps 2-4)

- Lex's daytime brief: the mind runs beside the game and only reports. `[Mind] MindShadow` (on while it's tested) works out traits (step 2), needs and concerns (3), feelings and mood (4) for people near the player and writes them to the log (`MIND ...`), F7 (`TEST mind shadow:`; `TEST mind:` stays the Minds top-five) and the Show minds card's last line ("mind: ..."). What it decides reaches the screen only with `[Mind] MindLive` (off). Settings version 80. Working notes, the vignettes and the day's decisions: `DAYTIME.md` and `daytime\` in the repo root (git-excluded).
- Rules it keeps (today's brief; keep them until Lex lifts them): game-side mind code reads and logs only (no task, animation, scenario, spawn, outfit, damage, no write to enRAGE, nothing new in the save); traits stay out of the share-back to enRAGE (a dry run logs what would be shared: `MIND share (dry run): ...`); it reads only people with an identity and never makes one (never `ResidentOf`, `MeetResident`, `PersonOfFolk` or `Store.Get`: they make state, roll the shared Random and add to the save; `Store.Find`, `_resBind` and `PersonOfResident` are fine); hooks it adds to other features catch their own errors and only queue, capped; its live state is in memory, capped (`MindShadow.Cap` 600), cleared by `Engine.ForgetThinking` (the store's rewind) and with a new Engine (a new save); at most 20 MIND lines a minute (`MindLinesAMinute`), one per person per event; cheap (budget 0.2 ms a frame with 20 people near). Two changes from the night run of 2026-10-28 (Lex's brief): **the save**: nothing new is stored for the mind's live state (traits, needs, feelings, mood, beliefs and choices stay in memory, capped, cleared on `Store.Rewind`); a new optional field on a record already saved (a `Happening`, a `MindMemory`) is allowed when an old save loads without it, `Store.Fix` fills it and `Store.Rewind` handles it, with the round trip both ways proved in mindtest's `StoreLoadTest`; no new saved collections, no new records in the saved WorldEvents log, caps unchanged. **Frozen case sets**: the vignettes and any case set built later (`replay.txt`) are frozen once written; a case the code can't meet is marked disputed with a reason for Lex, never edited to pass.
- Night run 2026-10-28, item 1 (the review of the day's work; NIGHT.md in the repo root, git-excluded): nine fixes (see CHANGELOG, Unreleased) and the appraisal's gaps closed: deeds carry the victim's id where the game knows it (`HurtVictim` in `WestworldScript.Watch.cs`: the living person the player hurt last within 15 s and 15 m of an assault, from the self-defence code's hit list; `LastingId`, never `ResidentOf`; on the deed's `VictimId` and the happening's target, never among its witnesses), so `Appraisal.From` gives him the shove's self part; the robbery and shop crimes name no victim (the game's crime event gives a witness, not a victim). `encounter:execution` carries how it ended ("he died; over", "saved; over") and maps to a killing seen (the boss the killer when the record names him; nothing for the dead man) or a rescue for the captive the player saved (`Appraisal.ExecutionKind`); `encounter:visitor` maps to a new kind `uncanny` (Threat 0.45, Uncanny 1; fear, frozen when strong) for everyone who saw it. `Display`: a lawman standing by (`Law`) hushes anger and contempt. The one road (after the second late review, `Core/Engine.Appraise.cs`): `Engine.DoerOf(happening)` (the pair's tie's Doer), `WarmthBetween`, `AppraiseFor(happening, person, heardOnly, sawCount)` and `SawCount`; the game side's `MindAppraiseStart`/`MindAppraiseOne` and the offline tests (EventsTest's who-is-who, ReplayTest, Replay2Test) all go through it with ties noted the game's way (`Ties.Note`), so a drift between what the ties record and what the appraisal assumes fails offline; two reviews had found three such drifts the tests couldn't see because they handed the doer in by hand. Under it one replay2 cell moved and is disputed (a tie the scene itself notes makes a stranger "known" to that scene's appraisal: For Lex). mindtest `ShadowDayTest` runs the shadow end to end: two hundred people, three days of scenes recorded the game's way (the tie, the memories, the happening queued), every person in or watching each taken down the one road through the appraisal, the display rules, the shadow's feelings, mood and beliefs, and at the end the needs, the choosing, the mind's own thought and the language request with its fallback through `Language.Check`; nothing may throw, no feeling or request may be about the thinker (by id: two Jacobs share a first name), every fallback line must pass. mindtest `StoreSizeTest` measures the save at scale for Lex's "memories for as many NPCs as possible" (2026-10-27): 700 people with twelve memories each (memory v1's fields filled) save as 1,988 KB in 24 ms and load in 67 ms; `Store.Save`'s `Trim` keeps the save at `MaxResidents` 700 and drops the people past it with their states, so 2,000 or 5,000 people come back as 700, and the uncapped cost is about 2.8 KB a person with twelve memories (2,000 people about 5.7 MB, 5,000 about 14 MB, loading in roughly half a second at 5,000 by the same rate). A debt called in and a brush-off carry their doer since item 17 (2026-10-29, after the run closed): `Scene.Doer` (the cast index the scene's own shape fixes: the creditor who called it in, A in `Scenes.Debt` and B in the linked exchange; the one who brushed the other off, B in the exchanges, a courtship refused, a gift turned down, the lost child's cold answer, a grudge walked away from), passed by `NoteSceneTie(kind, people, doer)` into `Ties.Note`'s doer, which `DoerOf` reads as for the trouble kinds; `Ties.Trouble` and the ties' thought pools are untouched (a debt's doer changes no thought), and the ladder's aggrieved side reads only the trouble kinds. An old save's debt or brush-off ties carry no doer and still map to nothing. mindtest EventsTest's who-is-who covers both ways round and the builders' marks. The replay cases: mindtest `ReplayTest` rebuilds ticktest's MindTest day as Happenings (the robbery, the face into the bar, the brawl, the death) for its cast and checks the frozen `daytime\harness\mindtest\replay.txt` (31 cells: 21 counted, 10 disputed for Lex). A second set since the afternoon of the night run (`Replay2Test`, `replay2.txt`, frozen; `mindtest.exe replay2` prints it): the doer sides the mapping reached that day (a cheer, a round, a fall walked past, a coin given, a beggar sent off) for a cast of the eight tempers in every part, 46 cells, 32 counted and 14 disputed (a cheer's good news read as relief; the eight stood a drink feel nothing, since the round's record names one target; a friend's contempt at a slight he saw; the refuser's pity with the urge to help, since the asking's event doesn't carry the refusal).
- Traits (`Core/Traits.cs`): 14 z-scores, never stored: five broad traits with two facets each (`Curiosity`/`Imagination` openness, `Order`/`SelfControl` conscientiousness, `Sociability`/`Assertiveness` extraversion, `Compassion`/`Trust` agreeableness, `Anxiety`/`Anger` neuroticism; facets share their broad trait at `FacetR` 0.45), `Honesty` (honesty-humility), `Piety`, `Deference` (to the law), `Risk`. Each is built from 19 independent normals (`Load`: the five broad traits and each trait's own part; anger leans against agreeableness -0.3 and conscientiousness -0.2, as angry hostility does in the NEO's data; honesty on agreeableness; piety on order and kindness; deference on order and against openness; risk against anxiety's own part, with anger's, against conscientiousness, with extraversion and openness; each trait's own part fills it to SD 1); a normal is six uniforms from splitmix64 (`Next`), each try's stream started from a hashed point (`seed + try * const`, through `Next`), so no two tries share numbers (the first version stepped one state per try and its tries shared 113 of 114 numbers: the review of 2026-10-27).
