- `Of` in three steps, so a person stays the same person when something small changes (enRAGE moving a record's facet after a memory, the year turning to 1907; mindtest: Bravery -6 moves a trait 0.31 SD at most, eight years 0.26, no temper changes): (1) the draw, by id and temper alone (`LabelOf` of the bare draw must be the decided temper; a plain person reads plain; with nothing decided, an enRAGE townsperson with no temper, the first draw stands and its label is what would be shared); (2) the decided pulls (`Pulls`): Devotion -> piety (Devout +1.1, Churchgoer +0.35, Lapsed -0.4, Godless -1.2), Sociability +-0.55, Disposition (Friendly compassion +0.35 anger -0.2; Surly the other way), Smarts curiosity +-0.3, trade (`TradePull` by `TradeOf`, all 0.25 SD or less; no "bounty" with the outlaws, no deference pull for saloon girls), age (`AgeChange`, 20 to 60, linear), women anxiety and compassion +0.2, risk -0.2; enRAGE's records get only age and sex here (their facets already carry faith, disposition and trade) and then `Import` (facet-50)/12, mixed with their own draw where the facet is mostly noise: Anger; Bravery -> low anxiety, assertiveness and risk; Pride and Ambition -> assertiveness; Honesty, Greed, Pride -> honesty-humility; Loyalty -> trust; Cruelty, Loyalty -> compassion; Piety (half-weighted: faith moves it 35 points); Humour -> sociability; Ambition -> order; the Law value -> deference (God, Money and Honour come from the facets, so not again); then `Hold` keeps what's decided on its side (Devout piety >= 0, Churchgoer >= -0.8, Lapsed -1.2..0.8, Godless <= -0.3; Outgoing sociability >= -0.5, Introvert <= 0.5; Friendly compassion >= -0.6, Surly <= 0.6); (3) if the temper no longer reads back, `Nudge` moves along its profile (or, for the plain, away from what they lean to), holding at each step (about one in nine; `Nudged`; `Mismatch` if it still doesn't read back, never seen). The temper's profile: `SigT`/`SigW` (the mind doc's table), `Score` scaled by its real spread (`MakeLens`, from the loadings), `LabelOf` = the highest if at least `LabelAt` (1.08) after a small evening-out (`Even`), else plain. `Words(n)`: traits past `WordsAt` (1.4) in words of the time (not enRAGE's facet words; the fuzzy check matched "quick to anger"; "outspoken" not "forward", "no friend of the law" not "lawless", "matter-of-fact"; low honesty-humility is "boastful" for the forward, "grasping" for the rest), else "middling in most things" (about one in thirteen); `Describe()` for the log.
- `Core/MindShadow.cs` (`Engine.Shadow`): the trait cache by id and temper, `Generation`, `Clear`. `Game/WestworldScript.Mind.cs`: `MindShadowStep` from Tick04 after grudges, `Safe("mind shadow")`; once a second `MindScan` (living humans within 20 m, nearest first, reused lists), other frames two people each (`MindLook`); `MindKnown` (cached by handle, again when the model changes, after 10 s for nobody and 30 s for somebody): a story model or child is nobody; enRAGE's record (`RecordAt`, `PersonOf`), townsperson (`AcquaintanceAt`, `Snapshot.Of`, a bare Person: not `PersonOfFolk`), or a resident bound to the body (`_resBind`); everyone else is passed over; the traits are kept on the cached `MindWho`, so a look allocates nothing. Log `MIND traits: Name (kind, temper): words (reads as X); O C E A N, honesty, piety, law, risk[; moved to fit their temper][; share (dry run): ...]` once per person a session, marked told only when written (the 20 a minute may hold one back). The card line and F7 read only that cache, inside their own try/catch (a fault there never switches off Show minds or loses the F7 lines after it). `using Traits = EnrageWestworld.Core.Traits;` (enRAGE has a `Traits` of its own).
- Needs and concerns (step 3, `Core/Needs.cs`): nine needs, each 0..1, worked out when asked and never stored (`Needs.Of(Person, Traits, NeedContext)`, about 2.4 microseconds): hunger (hours since the last meal of their day, or since a meal they're known to have eaten (`NeedContext.AteAt`, Lex 2026-10-27: eating lowers hunger): country folk eat at 5, 11, 17, town folk at 7, 12, 18; flat while asleep; the poor a little hungrier), rest (hours awake from 5 or 6; drink tires), warmth and dry (the weather out of doors, the night; nothing under a roof), money for the week (by trade: the poor 0.32, the middling 0.2, gentry 0.08; Monday to Friday it tightens, Saturday's wages ease it; the sly feel it more; a theft done to them and a money worry (debt, bank, cards, savings, drought) by stage, each combined by `Or`, so stacked troubles near the top without pinning there), safety (violence seen or done to them, by its age, half-life 2 h; night out of doors; someone they saw do violence near; anxiety weighs it), company, standing, meaning (sociability, assertiveness, piety; a friend's death; Sunday; the Awakening's stage), and a vice's pull (`ViceOf`: drink or cards, by quirk or worry; evening, the saloon, drink, a worry that ended badly; self-control against). Unknown trades count as middling. Concerns (`Needs.Of(Person, gameDay, links)`): the worry from `InnerLife.WorryNow`, read as it is (nothing it produces changes; urgency by stage), a worry link's other end, an enRAGE record's goals (Avenge 0.75, the rest 0.45), the three most urgent; `Needs.Words` ("worried about the bank's note (the crisis)"). Game: `MindNeeds` every 30 s per known person (`NeedsMs`), from the clock, `InnerLife.Weekday`, the engine's `NowWeather`/`NowIndoors`/`NowPlace`, the people near, `InHisCups`, `Engine.SawYouViolent` and their memories (`Store.Find`, read only); on the first-look `MIND traits:` line, F7 `TEST mind shadow:` and the card. mindtest `NeedsTest`: the town for 30 game days an hour at a time (720,000 looks): hunger falls after meals, rest builds to the night, rain out of doors 0.61 against 0 under a roof, farmhands' money Friday 0.50 against Sunday 0.30, a fight seen raises safety to 0.27 and is gone twelve hours on, concerns come and go (median five a month, none with fewer than two), at most three at once and the most urgent first, nobody pinned at a need's top for a day.
- Appraisal and emotion (step 4, `Core/Appraisal.cs`): `Appraisal.Of(AppraisalEvent, Traits)` names one feeling (fear, anger, shame, guilt, grief, pride, gratitude, envy, pity, contempt, relief, joy, or none under `Felt` 0.12), its strength (mild under 0.35, moderate under 0.65, strong) and its urge. The event is what the game can tell (the vignettes' facts: the kind, this person's part self/doer/kin/friend/onlooker, who did it and their warmth to them and to the one it was done to, place, drink, company, sex, witnesses, the law, a gun). Each kind has a `Meaning` (harm, status, loss, gain, threat, wrong, intent, feared, need, the doer's need, uncanny, `Own`: how far a loss of face is their own failing, `Money`). The appraisal (Lazarus, OCC, Scherer): care for whoever it happened to (kin, friend, by warmth, compassion, sociability), the danger to them (shared by those present, less from a drunk), coping (assertiveness and nerve against a gun, the law, two men; friends at hand, drink), blame (intent x wrong by their values; low trust sees more; the law's hand is less wrong to the law-abiding; a coarse word to a woman graver), who saw (before people they know, more in church), how much they care how they're seen (`standing`: anxiety, assertiveness, low honesty, sociability, kindness, convention). Anger needs power to cope (fear is what comes without it) and blame; the hot-tempered flare at provocation (Berkowitz); on a friend's behalf the loyal flare too. Shame by their own failing, by another's mostly anger (but for the anxious and meek). Guilt by conscience (honesty, kindness, faith, convention). Grief sharper seen. Envy with low honesty-humility and an anxious, angry nature. Contempt for the wrong or the weak they don't fear (an onlooker's by how involved they are). Urges by feeling and context: anger confronts (a killer), steps in for a friend, watches when mild; a woman's anger at a stranger is the cut, at a neighbour talk; a cold man's or at a drunk, a turned back; fear freezes before a gun or the uncanny, else gets away, a thief hides; shame hides, the rootless run from money trouble, shamed by someone they fear they get away; guilt makes amends unless afraid of being found out or too proud; grief withdraws (goes to the friend shot down); a stranger steps into a fight only past `CrowdBar(witnesses)`, which rises with the crowd (`CrowdBase` 0.10 + `CrowdRise` 0.025 ln(1+n): mindtest's town gives somebody stepping in 68% at 5, 85% at 10, 95% at 20, 2.8 people at 20, each one's chance falling 20% to 14%; Philpot et al. 2020: 90.9%, 3.76). `Appraisal.From(Happening, id, ..., doerId, heardOnly, sawCount)` maps what WorldEvents recorded to the event for this person's part, or null. A scene's cast is by who speaks first, not by who did what, so for one-sided scenes (rough, pickpocket, scorn, charity, help, passby, faced, defend, round, settled, apology, comfort, cheer, debt, brushoff, drunk) the doer is the pair's tie's `Doer` (looked up by the game side); with none known, nothing (rather than the wrong way round). Mutual scenes (brawl, argue, quarrel, snap, standoff): each did it to the other; a witness with a warmer tie to one fighter watches it done to that one. Nobody knows who lifted a purse (a lift: nothing); a killing is felt from the ledger's `death`, never again from the deed; the dead feel nothing; someone who only heard it (`How` "heard") gets a `heard` event (fright only, no face to blame, no friend's grief: word takes time); the witness count is those who saw, not the player. Night 2026-10-28 (after item 14): four more of the vignettes' kinds reach the game through the scenes: a cheer is `goodnews` for the one with the news (A, who speaks first) and, through how much they care for him, for a friend glad with him (an onlooker nothing; the friend's joy is about him, `Feeling.About`: joy at another's luck names the one it happened to, joy at one's own whoever brought it); the man who stood a round is `praised` (role self, doer self); the one walked past after a fall feels the `pratfall` before the street (doer self; whoever saw him stepped round still sees the slight); the giver of charity and the man who sent a beggar off were asked (`beg`, the beggar the doer: pity or contempt by their nature). The doer's own side of the other one-sided scenes stays null. Two conventions the second review of the afternoon caught: a beggar's scene is recorded with the beggar as A and the tie's doer (the asking is his doing, `Ties.Trouble`), so `From` swaps the pair for charity and scorn (the appraisal's doer is the one who answered; before, the giver felt gratitude and the refuser felt refused); a cheer, a comfort and an apology carry no doer on their tie, so `From` takes it from the cast (a cheer is A's news, a comfort and an apology B's doing), where before they mapped to nothing in play. One's own part is never about oneself: pity is for the one who asked, relief for the rescuer or nobody (`Of`). The uncanny keeps its whom ("a pale figure over a body"). mindtest `EventsTest` builds a scorn the game's way (`Ties.Note` then the tie's `Doer`) and checks each. mindtest `EventsTest` checks each. Not mapped yet (for Lex): the man the player robs in a shop appraises nothing (the crime names a witness, not a victim); insult (the reactions' listener has it, item 13), rivalluck, revived, lawgrab and robbed (a spawned traveller has no mind) have no game path yet (the vignettes have them).
- Mood (`Core/Mood.cs`): PAD (pleasure, arousal, dominance, -1..1). Each feeling pushes it by ALMA's OCC-to-PAD table (Gebhard 2005) x `Push` 0.6 x its intensity, halving every 180 game minutes (`MoodHalfLives`: shame and guilt 360, grief 2,880: a friend shot down isn't forgotten by breakfast); the feeling itself fades faster (fear 10 min ... grief 240). The baseline from the traits (Mehrabian 1996, the broad five / 3, kept within 0.5). Worked out in closed form when asked (`Mood.Now`), never ticked. `Words`: sore/glad, keyed up/flat, cowed/bold against their own baseline, else "even"; `SoreOrGlad` the old two words (also sore at rest for a sour nature, P -0.35 or lower, glad for a sunny one). `MindShadow` keeps eight feelings a person (the one with least weight left goes first, so the week's slights never push out a grief; 600 people, the first to feel going first) and a queue of 32 happenings (`Queue`, `Next`), cleared with the rest. `Engine.NoteEvent` queues each happening for the mind when MindShadow is on (inside its own try/catch: a fault there never costs the event). `Engine.MoodToday`: with `MindLive` on and something felt, sore and glad come from the mind's mood, and the old `InnerLife.MoodOf` when the mind's is even; off, exactly the old way.
- Game (`MindAppraiseStep`): one person a frame from the happening being appraised (a happening over 10 game minutes old by its turn is dropped, counted in F7: whoever is near by then didn't live it); each person in it or who saw or heard it (target, doer, witnesses) whom the mind knows, alive and in this second's scan, appraises it, stamped with the happening's time; the old mood is worked out only for a line that will be written for their part (warmth from `_store.Ties`, read only), the feeling kept, one line each within the 20 a minute: `MIND Name (temper): anger (strong) at you, urge confront <- scene:rough (self, 6 saw); mood sore and keyed up[; the old mood says nothing, the mind's sore]`. F7 `TEST mind shadow:` per person (Lex's example: "Name, traits, mood, last felt X at Y, needs, worried about ...") and the events appraised; the card's "mind:" line the same. Nobody tasked.
- Offline (mindtest): `AppraisalTest` (every vignette row not disputed: feeling, strength and urge allowed; the typical member of each temper and sex, the average of 2,000 built the game's way; 171 of 171 pass, 13 rows disputed for Lex with reasons), `EventsTest` (the crowd; 30 days of events for 1,000 people: no feeling over a third (anger and fear 17% each); every mood back within 0.1 of baseline 12 game hours after an everyday event, the furthest 0.02; 0.2 microseconds an appraisal, 0.13 a mood read; every happening kind for every part maps or is nothing).
- Display rules and the catalogue (step 5, Core only; daytime 2026-10-27): `Core/Display.cs` `Display.Shows(Feeling, AppraisalEvent, Traits)`: shown, softened, masked or turned (fear as bluster), by the mind doc's 1899 rules: men hide fear before other men (a timid one can't quite, and in a saloon he blusters; frozen at a gun only the proud and the cold keep their face); before the law the law-abiding let fear show and the sly keep it off; women of standing keep anger off their faces in public (a hot one can't quite); church, a graveside and the law hush anger (swallowed upward); drink and the Awakening's third stage loosen every rule; the detached (low compassion and sociability) mask nearly everything; a hot temper or a timid nature can't hold shame; the proud keep their chin up; guilt shows only when the wrong is known and they're owning it; grief shows for a woman quietly, for a man where nobody sees, and for anyone at a friend shot down in front of them; envy is masked but by a hothead; the cut is meant to be seen. The thought never follows them (the gap is the point). mindtest `ShowsTest`: the vignettes' "shows" column, 170 of 170 counted cells (the 14th dispute, V07 Plain, is that when nothing is felt nothing shows). `Core/Catalogue.cs`: one table of what the game is proven to do (`Expression`: channel gesture/emote/scenario/clip/voice/move, the game's name, the feelings and urges it can show, the least strength, who, posture, proof), 53 entries, 38 proven (played in game, or a line the banks carry, with the count of voices from amb_takes.txt) and 15 of last night's clips no log has shown playing yet, listed as "NOT YET SEEN" and left out (`Proven`) until one does (the brief: last night's sets only once a log shows them playing). `Catalogue.For(feeling, urge, strength, woman, standing)`, `Catalogue.Gaps`. The `MIND` line now says what shows and the first proven way to show it (or "the thought alone"). The fuzzy check flags seven of the catalogue's names (WORLD_HUMAN_GRAVE_MOURNING_KNEEL, WORLD_HUMAN_CROUCH_INSPECT, TASK_SMART_FLEE_PED, TASK_GO_TO_ENTITY, TASK_TURN_PED_TO_FACE_ENTITY, TASK_LOOK_AT_ENTITY and the pointlaugh emote set: the game's own names, which enRAGE names too), accepted as code names like SCRIPT_TASK_PLAY_ANIM: the fuzzy baseline is 216 from here (overlap still 118). mindtest `CatalogueTest`: every entry well formed; of 167 feelings the vignettes name, 117 have something proven, 50 are masked (the thought alone), none have nothing; to look for: envy, pity and relief (no proven gesture, clip or scenario; pity's crouch is last night's, not yet seen).
- Thoughts from feelings (behind `[Mind] MindLive`; daytime 2026-10-27, filled out in the night run of 2026-10-28): `Core/MindThoughts.cs`, pools by feeling and by whom it's about (`you`: the player, `{you}` as this person sees him; `tie`: someone they have a scene tie with, `{who}` a first name; `stranger`: anyone else; no kin pools: no two living people in the world are known kin (`Resident.Kin`/`KinOf` name a dead counter keeper's predecessor, enRAGE's `KinId` an avenger's dead, `Family` is a flag); grief only for a tie, pity and grief none for the player), 33 pools of twelve (was three) in seven forms (an observation, a judgment, self-talk, a question, a plan, a regret, one thing bringing back another), 12 `|masked` pools of eight (four until the afternoon of the night run) that say the mask when the face hides the feeling ("Smile. Let him think it didn't land."; `Feeling.Shows` is set by the game side from `Display.Shows` before the feeling is kept, `FeltAt.Masked` carries it, `MindThoughts.Line(..., masked)` tries the mask pool first), ten lines for each pressing need with their own pools by trade group or temper group where it tells (`NeedByGroup`: hunger|farm, rest|working, money|card, safety|fearful, standing|bold, meaning|old...; four lines each since the afternoon of the night run, 41 added through a subagent's period pass; `Fill` runs `Ties.Pronouns` for the tie's first name, so a line says he or she of {who} through the `{he}` `{him}` `{his}` slots (eight carried lines made slots the same afternoon); `NeedLine` takes `InnerLife.TradeGroup(p.Trade)` and `Minds.TemperGroup(p.Personality)`), the wet in the rain or snow out of doors (`Wet`, ten), and each vice (ten): 609 lines in all, in Lex's brief (what's really felt, even when the face masks it), every one through `Language.Check`, linetools, the overlap and fuzzy checks (ten reworded to keep fuzzy at 216). mindtest `ThoughtsTest` (pool sizes, five forms, the body in every pool, no line in two pools, the mask first, the trade and temper pools first, nothing twice in a day); LifeShareTest on: 7,933 distinct, generic 44.9%, the mind's own 6.6% (12.0% before item 5's hunger weights, which took hunger's 5.4% out). `Core/Engine.MindLive.cs` `MindSay`: with MindLive on, the feeling with most left in it (`Mood.Lasting`, at least `Appraisal.Moderate`); whom by id (`Feeling.AboutId` and `DoerId`, carried on `FeltAt`: the one it happened to for grief, pity and relief, whoever did it for the rest and for a feeling about oneself), "player" the player, a tie found by both ids (`TieBetween`, the day's ties then the store's), a foe (warmth below 0) never in a warm feeling's tie words; else a need at 0.85 or more, 25% of the time. Nobody thinks the same line twice in a game day (`SaidToday`, shared with the Minds; the old path notes what it says, F7 and `Candidates` only read). Both thought paths ask it: the old roll through `InnerLife.MindFirst` (a static every Engine sets in its constructor; a feeling comes first as often as it's still felt, Lasting x 100 in 100; it says nothing with MindLive off, so lifetest, which builds no Engine, and ticktest are unchanged) and the Minds' weighing (a candidate in `Candidates`: a feeling pulls 40 + 50 x Lasting, a need 38). A feeling kept drops that person's cached thought (`MindShadow.OnFeel`), so it can come at once; a fault gives the old way and is logged once (`MIND live thought failed`). ## Two players' reports (Lex, 2026-10-28)

- Relayed by Lex during the night run: "every 30 seconds somebody wants revenge for someone I killed, the popup is annoying"; an interrogated Lemoyne Raider killed afterwards "talking about how he was questioned by a stranger" in the box, then his relatives coming, "alive after death"; and "Him. I've spoken to them before" on a first meeting, "very frequently", West World off. What they are, and what changed. (1) enRAGE's own `KinReact` (NemesisMod.cs, read only) runs on every record death the player caused: every living kin and friend on its roster gets a Grief memory, a `GrudgeNotice` ("X heard you killed his friend Y...") and, when angry (45+) or Loyal, an Avenge goal; a gang's members are each other's friends, so a camp cleared is a dozen notices. enRAGE's `GrudgeNotice` shows only with its `Config.GrudgeMessages`, which is `MessageLevel == 3`: NemesisMod.ini's `[Menu] GrudgeMessages` (false sets the level 0) and then `[Menu] Messages` (0-4, read after it and overriding), so `Messages = 2` is the player's switch (our panel reads the same property for the subtitle line, `Bridge.Territory.cs`). Ours are rarer (a telegram to two relatives, `AvengerChance` 35%) and now capped: `Settings.AvengersAtOnce` (2: riding, arrived, or on enRAGE's roster and alive by `Find(a.RecordId)`), `AvengerGapHours` (48: the next DueAt pushed past the latest DueAt of any avenger, arrived ones included, since an arrival is stage 3 within its tick), `Engine.AvengerRoom` (logs `AVENGER held:`), settings version 91, the panel's Needs map. (2) `Engine.TellsNothing(id)`: `HasDied`, or dead on our ledger (`Store.DeathOf`) with no reverie woken since and no `PresumedDeadAt` of enRAGE's at or after our note (`Person.PresumedDeadAt`, from the record in `Snapshot.Of`: enRAGE presumed him dead after we saw it and means to bring him back, or he's back, its come-back or ours through `Bridge.Revive`; his to say, as `HasDied` leaves records); `Deliver` drops a rumour or a letter whose `FromId` tells nothing (`WORD dropped:`), `DueLater` the same for a telling or a diary entry due. enRAGE's `IsAlive` alone missed the case: its 2 s people sync didn't see the body (the Pope McCrae case; for a record the ledger notes the death, but `[Mind] RecordFates` books only deaths RealNPCs caused, so enRAGE's books still said alive). Not done, an audited-write scope change for Lex: booking a record death enRAGE missed through `Bridge.BookDeath` when the ledger saw it; until then enRAGE may send such a man back into the world as a nemesis, walking and thinking (the thought scan in `WestworldScript.cs` skips only `!IsAlive`, on purpose: a man in the world with no thoughts would be odder, and enRAGE's own come-backs are legitimate). (3) enRAGE's `Folk.Met` comes from its `Introduce` (naming by look) and is copied between bodies with the same name by its people sync, so `Regard.OfFolk`'s "met" is by look; `Folks.Thought` says spoken only with our own `st.Talks > 0`, else that the face seems known (four lines each). `NoteRegularMet` (`WestworldScript.Regulars.cs`) counts a meeting once per stay: `PersonState.LastSeenAt` (set at every look; a saved field the records already used) and `RegularMeetGapMin` 360. Lex's own report the same night: the "X is walking toward you" notice (`CastTick`, `WestworldScript.Life.cs`) named kin the moment an ambient passer-by 7-28 m off was sent over, with nothing marking the body, and the 20 s `TASK_GO_TO_ENTITY` on a ped the game's scripts own ran out before they arrived (eight notices, no arrival, in the log of 2026-10-28 00:41-01:43; the one-per-visit guard clears whenever the town name flickers at the edge). The notice is removed and the walk-up rebuilt (`KinWalk`, `KinWalkUp`, `KinWalkStep`, `KinEnd`, `KinAbort`, `KinStatus` in `WestworldScript.Life.cs`; Lex: "the NPCs are not making it to step 4, the Meeting part"): one relative at a time; the body a `Natives.Bystander` 5-18 m off in view (`NodInView`), not in a vehicle, of the relative's sex (someone in a scenario, the game's walking routes included, is let go of it first and gets the walk order 1.8 s on, `KinWalk.Pending`, and is taken only when nobody free of one is to hand: Lex's six refused tries in Valentine on 2026-10-29, every count 0, since the town's walkers are all in scenarios and were passed over uncounted; the refusal now counts everyone in range: the game's own, within 5 m, ours, riding, the wrong sex, named, out of view, busy, not ours to direct), carrying no name of its own (not a record, not one of enRAGE's townsfolk by `AcquaintanceAt`, not a bound resident, not ours), free (`NotFree`) and `MayDirectWhyNot` null; `TASK_GO_TO_ENTITY` with no timeout and a look-at, sent again every 4 s while they aren't 0.5 m nearer (`Reorders`), the meeting within 3.2 m through `GreetedSpecial` (the player greeting first counts: `_casts` empties), 8 s later a wander; given up after 30 s, when outrun (6 m further off than at the start), or when a scene or enRAGE takes them (`KinEnd`, a wander, and the next look waits a minute); no start while the player is dead, mounted, in a vehicle or moving over 2 m/s; the body's model kept (`KinWalk.Model`) so a handle the game reused ends the walk; a running conversation (`Feed.Talking`) holds the arrival; Pacing "kin" (chance 100, one a town a game day, the same relative once a week) noted on arrival (`KinArrived`, also when the player greets them first), not at the start; `_kinNotBefore` two minutes after a start or a load (`KinAbort` from the mission start, a load, the wipe and shutdown, beside `GrudgesAbort`); `IsAcknowledging` keeps the body out of the other features; no notice, the relative named when they speak. Log `CAST X (brother of Y) in Valentine: a passer-by 12 m off walks over`, `CAST X ... reached you after 9 s from 12 m, the order sent again once`, `CAST X gave it up: ...`; F7 `TEST kin:` (walked up, reached, gave up, who's coming and how far, the last, the last look's why with the crowd counted); Testing row `kin` (ticktest golden: 77 actions, pacing's ten hours count kin 22). Reviewed by a subagent before the commit (the reused handle, the mounted player and the outrun, the pacing noted on arrival, the greeted-first count, the arrival over a talk, the tail's give-up miscounted: all fixed; kept: `AcquaintanceAt` excluded, since enRAGE's name would show over the same body in the ticker; if a heavily named town leaves too few bodies, drop the on-screen half of `NodInView` before widening `KinFar`). Not testable offline; to see in game. mindtest `FeedbackTest` (the dead dropped, a living teller through, a woken reverie tells again; eight deaths by the player give 4 avengers uncapped, 2 at the cap 50 h apart, 1 at a cap of 1; met by look says seen); ticktest's InterestTest and own-life classifier updated for the lines.

## Thoughts that vary (Lex, 2026-10-29)

- Lex: "a lot of the same types of thoughts"; his log (139 thoughts over heads): 110 distinct, but five frames said four to six times with only the names changed, and worries 42%, memories 28%, enRAGE's townsfolk 15%, the weather and place 6%. `[Mind] ThoughtVariety` (on, settings version 93; `Core/Engine.Minds.cs`): (1) a frame guard across people: `Engine.FrameKey` (every capitalised word as X, lowercased) kept in `_recentFrames` with the game minute it was shown (capped 2,000; cleared by `ForgetThinking`); in `ThinkImpl` the first pass over the ordered candidates skips a frame anyone thought within `FrameMinutes` (60) and the second pass takes one rather than nothing (`FramesHeld` counts); (2) the sources spread: `_recentSources` keeps the last `SpreadWindow` (8) thoughts' source families (`SourceGroup`: the part before the colon, so filler:body and filler:hour are one family) and `Spread` hands `Minds.Order` copies of the candidates with each pull divided by 1 + `SpreadPerHit` (0.35) x the family's hits within the hour (the candidates themselves untouched; `SourcesSpread` counts the choices it changed). The thoughts that return before the choice go through `Varied(source, text)` (held back when the frame was heard within the hour, else noted): the world feeling unreal in `ResidentThought` and what one of enRAGE's townsfolk feels about the player in `FolkThought`. Off, exactly the old choice (the old roll path, `InnerLife.Thought`, is untouched: lifetest's golden stands; ticktest's moved on purpose for the counts). mindtest `VarietyTest` (twenty regulars, two hours, eighty thoughts: frames back within the hour on another person 16 off to 0 on, the top source's share 31% to 30%, distinct 61 to 72; the frame key strips names and only names). F7 `TEST thought variety:`. Next: the language layer (roadmap 15) for thoughts composed from the mind, once Lex approves the GPU setup.

## Subtitles, one set at a time (Lex, 2026-10-28)

- The game draws its own white subtitle at the bottom for any recorded line (ours play through `PLAY_PED_AMBIENT_SPEECH_NATIVE`) while its Subtitles option is on; no native stops or moves it (`ENABLE_MOVIE_SUBTITLES` is a nullsub), so Lex saw both sets ("I remember you" in white, ours in the box). `[Output] Subtitles` = game | ours | both | off (settings version 88; an old "on" reads as game; the panel steps the four): `Feed.SubtitleMode`, `Feed.SubtitlesAllowed()` (game: ours only while `IS_SUBTITLE_PREFERENCE_SWITCHED_ON` is false; ours: always; both: only with it on, as before; off), passed to `SubtitleRule.Text` as `settingOn` with `gameOption` true. Default game: one set at a time with nothing to set, and the game's white line shows the recording's real words. Lex's own choice: ours, with the game's Subtitles off. `SUBTITLES:` and F7 `TEST subtitles:` say which set shows.

## Controllers (Lex, 2026-10-28: "for people who use controllers and not keyboard")

- `Core/PadChord.cs`: a binding is a button held and another pressed ("RB+DPadUp") or one alone; buttons A B X Y LB RB LT RT LS RS Select and the D-pad, each read through a frontend control (`ControlOf`: INPUT_FRONTEND_ACCEPT, ..._RB, ..._UP...), PlayStation names and plain words as aliases (R1, Cross, Touchpad, Up), a raw `INPUT_` name passes through; `Parse` (refuses three buttons, one twice, an unknown name, with why), `Name`, `Normal`; `Proven` = the panel's own pad buttons (the D-pad, A, B, X, LB, RB; the rest not yet heard in play). Settings `[Controller]` (version 86): `PadKeys` (on), `PadSettings` RB+DPadUp, `PadStatus` RB+DPadLeft, `PadLetters` RB+DPadRight, `PadTickerNext` None (named so the panel never takes them for keyboard keys; `Clamp` puts one that can't be read back to its default, the panel's to RB+DPadUp). The Customizable Trainer opens on RB+D-pad down, so ours keep off it.
- Game (`Game/WestworldScript.Pad.cs`, `PadStep` from Step before `SettingsStep`, `Safe("controller")`): only while `!IS_USING_KEYBOARD_AND_MOUSE`; a chord when its press is just pressed and its hold has been down 200 ms (`PadHoldMs`; a quick tap of RB for cover then the D-pad isn't one), a lone button only when no chord's hold on it is down; never with our panel or the letters open, the pause menu (`IS_PAUSE_MENU_ACTIVE`), a game menu (`UIAPPS.IS_ANY_UIAPP_ACTIVE`: satchel, map, catalogues read the same buttons) or no player control (logged `PAD ... so nothing` once in 10 s). Each binding sets the key's own request flag (`_settingsRequested`, `_testRequested`, `_readerRequested`, `_tickerNextRequested`). `PAD heard <button>` once a session per button (which frontend controls answer in play; unproven ones say so). F7 `TEST controller:`. The letters (`Reader.PadInput`): D-pad and LB/RB turn, B or A closes, nothing until the buttons that opened it are let go; the footer says the pad's buttons. The panel: `[Controller]`'s rows on the Hotkeys page under "RealNPCs on a controller" (not a page of their own); A or Enter on one starts `PadCaptureStep` (the pad's menu buttons don't move the panel meanwhile): once everything is let go, a held LB/RB/LT/RT/LS/RS/Select and another pressed make the binding; B alone keeps it, Y alone clears it, another button alone is refused as the game's; Escape keeps it and Delete clears it from the keyboard; the same buttons on another of ours leave that one with none (`PadWrite`). mindtest `PadChordTest`. Not yet seen in game: whether the frontend controls answer in play (the log's `PAD heard` will say), RB's own job on foot (cover, by most guides) while the D-pad is pressed.

## Show minds in a crowd (Lex, 2026-10-28)

- Off as it comes since the night of 2026-10-28 (Lex: the Testing page's Switches, "Show Minds", false by default); `Settings.ShowMinds` false, the version-77 migration that forced old inis on removed, no version bump (an ini's value stands either way). Ctrl+F2 and the Switches row turn it on.
- `ShowMindsFrame`: the nearest known person (cards are built nearest first) gets the full card, the rest `MindCardShown.Short` (the name line plus the mood, the first clause of line two); each card's box is checked against `_cardRects` (this frame's, nearer first) and lifted above the first it crosses (up to 8 lifts), so no two overlap. From Lex's screenshot: four full cards piled over the bar.

## Testing

- The test cast (Lex, 2026-10-27, roadmap 32, moved up to be built that night: "we're doing a lot of testing"). `Core/TestCast.cs`: eight `Parts` (mother 31, oldtimer 68, oldtimer2 73, preacher 46 Devout, listener 38 Churchgoer woman, bully 29 Hothead, bullied 34 never a Hothead, stander 41 Loyal), `Fit` (marks `Resident.Cast` with the part, sets the age, faith and temper, and a mother's trade to "a townswoman" when hers has no family), `WhyNotFit` (the tests' own rules: `LostChild.CouldBeMother`, `Scenes.WouldPreach`, the old-timer trade from an `_m_o_` build, the temper). `Resident.Cast` (saved, EmitDefaultValue): never dropped by the 700 cap while alive (`Store.KeepResident`), never the "back" recast in `ResidentOf`, a gathering's, a hermit's (`Engine.Hermits`); not a regular, so never a worry link's other end or an execution's captive. Game (`Game/WestworldScript.TestCast.cs`): Testing rows `cast` ("Bring the test cast here") and `cast:go`; `TestCastBring` picks a spot out of sight 14-26 m behind you (`EncounterSpot`), else 8-40 m, else in the open 10-18 m from you in sight (`TestCastOpenSpot`; Lex was refused three times running in a narrow street), keeps members already within 60 m (they walk over) and queues the rest; `TestCastMake` (one a step under `Safe("test cast")`, from Tick04 after the mind shadow) brings back the part's living saved member in their build and outfit, else makes one (`MeetResident`, an outfit preset by seed, `Fit`), via `Spawner.SpawnCast` (a mission entity so the game keeps them, in Spawner's own `Cast` set, never `Ours`), binds them (`_resBind`, `KeepResident`), `TASK_WANDER_IN_AREA` 3.5 m about the spot (the preacher's listener within 8 m, the old men within 10 m of each other), and logs `TESTCAST new|back: ...` (not `CAST`, the kin's) with any `DOESN'T FIT THE PART`; a build that won't stream in tries others (old men: four `a_m_o_` builds from the API's model list) for up to 60 s. Every 2 s: a body gone is forgotten, a death left lying (the binding stays so the ledger notes it; the next bring makes someone new), a body 250 m off unseen deleted, one free (`MayDirectWhyNot`, `NotFree`, not in a scenario) 10 m from the spot walked back (every 20 s at most). `TestCastClear` at a mission start, a load, the wipe and shutdown deletes every body, in sight or not (after Lex's Insert reload one left walking off looked like the cast and wasn't); `cast:go` (which waits for a scene) deletes those unseen and walks those in sight off, left to the game, unbound. A refusal for want of someone in the old days, preacher and lost child tests ends with `TestCastHint` (the cast would bring them) when the cast isn't about. So that features take them as townsfolk: `Natives.OtherScripts(h)` (a mission entity not of the cast) replaces the mission-entity test in `Natives.Bystander`, `CanDirect`, the wake acts' and despair's refusals, `CrowdCounted`, the talk's and the flirt's rules; enRAGE knows its townsfolk by their look and took cast members for townsfolk it had met who looked the same (Lex's first bring, 2026-10-28 00:08: the preacher became an awake townsman, who walked off on his waking act, and a scene was named with enRAGE's names while its tie went under ours), so the bridge's `AcquaintanceAt` and `EnrageNameAt` answer nobody for a cast body (`evenCast` for F7): every part of RealNPCs, the people ledger included, sees them by their own identity, though enRAGE itself still takes them for its own (its own death sync may mark that townsperson dead if a cast member dies); `GiveBack` never marks one "no longer needed" (as for a light borrow); `CleanupAll` deletes the cast's bodies. F7 `TEST test cast:` (the session's brings, who's here, MayDirect or busy, which townsperson enRAGE takes them for or what it calls them, any misfit). Lex's call (2026-10-28): the cast live fully as ordinary people (ties, gossip, courting, marrying); kept out only of what would give one person two bodies (the recast, gatherings, hermits) and of the save's trim. Settings version 85. Offline: mindtest `TestCastTest` (3,200 residents fitted from every trade the game names pass their tests' rules; the mark survives a save and is written only for the cast; a full save keeps the living cast and drops the dead). Seen in game (2026-10-28 00:08, Valentine): all eight made and fitting, the old men's first build streamed in, enRAGE looked at every one and took two for townsfolk of its own. `TASK_WANDER_IN_AREA` takes: they stroll about the spot (Lex, 2026-10-28; its last three arguments are unknown, 2, 6, 0 given). The cast come back by themselves (night 2026-10-28, after item 14; `[Testing] TestCastReturns`, on, settings version 90): with nobody of the cast about, every 20 s in a town, once per visit, the living cast members of the save who live there are brought back out of sight as the row brings them (`TestCastBring(..., onlySaved: true)`: nobody made anew); log `TESTCAST back by themselves in <town>`; F7 says how many times.

An Insert reload crashed the game once (0.6.1, 15:32): our shutdown finished ("Stopped."), then the crash came while Script Hook .NET unloaded the domain. The likeliest cause was `Draw.FreeAll` in `Shutdown04` freeing the ticker's text while the game still had that frame's text to draw; it's no longer called at shutdown. If it happens again, check `ScriptHookRDRDotNet.log` in the game folder for how far the unload got. There is no way to run the game in CI. Play-test in game and read the log at `Documents\Rockstar Games\Red Dead Redemption 2\RealNPCs\RealNPCs.log`. Hotkeys: F7 (status and log dump), ] (make a stalker now; `StalkerKey`), Y (wake the person you're locked on to one stage, whoever they are, else the record, townsperson or ordinary person you face, in that order; `WakeKey`), T (next ticker line; `TickerNextKey`, also enRAGE's telegraph key near a clerk), F2 (flirt; `FlirtKey`. F6 until settings version 48, which moved it to 5; version 50 moves F6 and that 5 to F2 (not 49: enRAGE's own settings class is at 49, and the overlap check flags the line): F6 is the game's camera and 5 takes out the item wheel's melee slot), N (letters and diaries; `ReaderKey`).

## Stalkers (current design, 0.6.0)

- Trigger (0.6.0, `WestworldScript.Hosts.cs`): every `StalkerEveryMinutes` of play (Game.GameTime) a roll at `StalkerChance`; a hit (or `StalkerKey`, 0) makes one due (`_stalkDueSince`). A due stalker waits for a clear moment (outdoors, no story mission, no lawmen, not fighting, not in water, screen not fading), then `StalkerCandidate` turns the person within `StalkerNearbyMetres` with the highest `StalkerPick.Score` (enRAGE townsfolk via `AcquaintanceAt` + their `PersonState`; residents only if already bound in `_resBind`; passers-by score 0). Never enRAGE full records (`RecordAt`; enRAGE runs them), lawmen, children, story models, indoors, anyone `CanDirect` rejects, in a Director scene or a wake act, or nearer than 12 m (the 7 m "caught" check). `Stalker.Adopt` borrows them (`Spawner.Borrow`: mission entity, in `Ours` and `Borrowed`); `Spawner.Release`/`CleanupAll` always hand borrowed peds back (`GiveBack`: clear tasks, unblock events, wander, no longer needed), never delete. Nobody for 3 min (or at once for the key): a spawned stranger, the old way, if `StalkerStrangers`. Revenants (Fallen.ReturnAt) are unchanged. After a stalker ends, the next roll counts from then.
- Spawn spots: `GET_SAFE_COORD_FOR_PED` always returns false in RDR2 (seen in game, 0.5.5), so `Natives.StandSpot` falls back to `GET_GROUND_Z_FOR_3D_COORD` (from the player's height first, never inside an interior).
- Behaviour (0.5.5): follow, stare, freeze when watched, hide. Player still for 20 s (no movement or move input; the camera is ignored) -> Circle at 6-10 m; any movement -> Retreat to a hidden spot 45-70 m out (`CoverSpot`), then Lurk. Lasts `StalkerMinutes` (default 120).
- Settings (0.6.0, version 19): `[Stalkers]` master `Stalkers`, StalkerKey, StalkerEveryMinutes, StalkerChance (per roll), StalkerNearbyMetres, StalkerStrangers, appear (strangers and turning up behind you again)/follow/stare/circle/hide distances, StalkerStillSeconds, StalkerWhenStill (mix / eerie / circle / watch / rush; rush stops at 3.5 m and never attacks; mix, the default, picks one each time), StalkerActMin/StalkerActMax (2/30, settings version 31: a new distance each time he comes in), StalkerMutters. `Stalker` holds the live `Settings` object, so panel or ini changes apply at once. A revenant stalker needs West World on too.
- Eerie acts (`Game/Stalker.Acts.cs`, catalogue in `Core/StalkerActs.cs`): `Mode.Act` sprints (speed 3, one nav order, re-sent only if he stops short; the old 1.5 s re-orders made him stop and start; smooth in game, 0.6.1) to a distance picked between StalkerActMin and StalkerActMax (`_actR`, new for each spot; moving round you walks unless the spot is over 15 m off; creep is skipped within 8 m), then Pause (face and stare) -> next act -> Pause, moving round the player every 3-4 acts. Animations are townsfolk gestures (`ai_gestures@gen_male|gen_female@standing@silent|speaker`, clips per sex, `_f_` front-facing clips where they exist; `TASK_PLAY_ANIM` flags Upperbody 8 | Secondary 16, see `eScriptedAnimFlags`, which differ from GTA's; if `IS_ENTITY_PLAYING_ANIM(..., 3)` doesn't see one, it is retried with flag 0, and one success switches all gestures to flag 0 for the session; the head-shake passed on the upper body in game, 0.6.1) and the few Online emotes Story Mode has (`script_mp@emotes@<name>@male@unarmed@full`, flag 0), loaded without blocking, played once facing (within 20 degrees); twists use `_SET_ENTITY_ANIM_SPEED` (freeze logs `_GET_ENTITY_ANIM_CURRENT_TIME` before and after to show whether it held). Scenarios use `TASK_START_SCENARIO_IN_PLACE_HASH`. Creep and shift (`ActStep.Creep`/`Shift`) use "seen" = on screen and clear line of sight, with the stalker ticking every 50 ms while they run: creep walks to 4 m from the player only while unseen (`_SET_PED_CROUCH_MOVEMENT`, then `SET_PED_STEALTH_MOVEMENT` if that didn't take, logged; `Spawner.StandUp` undoes both on every exit and hand-back) and `CLEAR_PED_TASKS_IMMEDIATELY` when seen moving; shift teleports him (like `Relocate`) to a spot 60-180 degrees round at the act distance that the camera can't see (`ShiftSpot`), only after he has been seen since the last shift, 1-3 times. Checks poll and pass on the first frame it runs: anim within 1.5 s, scenario within 6 s (a start after 2 s is logged); two failures (or a missing dict) drop the act for men or women for the session. `Stalker.ProbeActs` (after "Ready") looks up every animation set once and logs `STALKER acts: N of M ... missing: ...` (also `TEST stalker acts` in F7). Watching him changes nothing in this mode; moving ends it (scenarios end with CLEAR_PED_TASKS so props aren't left hanging, anims snap off).
- **The rdr3_discoveries dumps list Online content too: a name being in them doesn't mean Story Mode has it.** In game, 25 of 28 `script_mp@emotes` dicts don't exist (`DOES_ANIM_DICT_EXIST` false; 22 in the 0.6.0 log, then gentlewave, look_yonder and cougar_snarl in the 0.6.1 probe); respectful_bow, pointlaugh and prospector_jig do and played. The four `ai_gestures@gen_male|gen_female@standing@silent|speaker` dicts exist (0.6.1 probe). 20 of 26 scenario starts worked; gravedig and hammer never did and scrub once in three (all with props). The creep's stealth walk never took; `_SET_PED_CROUCH_MOVEMENT` did (0.6.1). Only the in-game probe proves an animation set exists.
- A live encounter keeps a stalker waiting (`StalkBlocker`), but a hermit only while he's taken up with you (`HermitWithYou`: any phase past `camp`, or you within 40 m of his fire): a camp counts as out within 150 m, so before 2026-10-27 one 145 m from Valentine kept the ] key waiting the whole time. Failed spawns retry after 1, 2, 4, 8, then 10 minutes. The log says what a stalker is waiting for (`STALKER due, waiting: ...`).
- Confirmed in game (0.5.5): spawning, following, circling, running to cover. Not yet seen: the watch and rush modes and the new hour settings.

## Releasing

1. Update the version in `EnrageWestworld.csproj`, the title and "NEW IN" section of `package/README.txt`, `README.md` and `CHANGELOG.md`.
2. Build, then zip `RealNPCs.dll` and `README.txt` as `release/RealNPCs-<version>.zip`, replacing the old zip, and update the link in `README.md`.
3. Commit and push. A GitHub Release with the zip attached is nicer than the folder link if `gh` can make one.

## Roadmap

1. Conversations in their real voices (Lex, 2026-10-25: the first priority, moved to item 1; it goes with 17): NPC-to-NPC talk from the game's own paired lines (CHAT_X then RESPONSE_X, CHAT_RESPONSE_A-E with RESPONSE_A-E, the weather pairs, HOWS_IT_GOING then RESPONSE_GENERIC, CHAT_STORY_<place>), played by line name, so every player hears them. Which voices carry which lines is our own data (banks.txt, from the decoded bank list); never ship a recording or a transcript. Overheard real-voice talk: no subtitle by default, or (Alt+F11 setting) a short caption in our own words, one per kind of line ('[remarks on the weather]'); exact words only from the player's own local voices\words.txt. Lex's word list is built locally: OpenIV export (PEDS_00-10.rpf; a voice bank exports as LINE_TAKE.wav) and faster-whisper in C:\mods\voice-test (transcribe.py). The farmhand test: 251 clips, about 1 in 10 lines with a slip, 1.3x real time on CPU. 2. Everyone is someone (residents: every ordinary person gets an identity, saved once touched; never written to enRAGE; next: service people at fixed posts keep one identity, town regulars with a stored outfit preset, routine and ties, bound to matching ambient peds out of view). 3. The Awakening (hosts slowly realise they're in a loop; it spreads host to host, a trigger line, they notice contradictions such as the revived dead, the ticker counts them; phases to build: loop and blind spot, reverie, counting, the voice, breaking the loop, the centre, reset or free. Despair, more ways (Lex, 2026-10-27, after the gun arcs and poison went in): drowning (water within reach found by probes, walk them in, then `ai_damage@ko@base` drown or `mech_swim@streamed_drowning` drown; both sets in Story Mode), jumping from a height (a drop found by downward probes, walk to the edge, a push and the ragdoll), hanging (`script_special_ped@pdadd_agnes_dowd@ig@ig2` hanging_action with its p_cs_noose01x rope, a raised spot and a beam; the hardest); and more (Lex, 2026-10-28: "more despair animations"): exposure (out into the snow in the mountains, sitting down and not getting up: the sit scenario, then lying down, the cold done through their health), the railway (only where a train is due; the game's trains are scripted, so first whether one can be timed), the law's hand (drawing on a lawman who fires, with care for the player's own wanted state), a horse and a drop (a ride off an edge the jump's probes find). The moments before, each a clip or scenario to find and prove with the in-game probe: sitting with their head in their hands, a long look at the water or the sky, writing a last letter (`WORLD_HUMAN_WRITE_NOTEBOOK`; found afterwards through the letters, item 8), a keepsake given to a friend (a hand-over gesture), kneeling at a grave (`WORLD_HUMAN_GRAVE_MOURNING_KNEEL`, proven by the Sabbath), drinking alone (`WORLD_HUMAN_DRINKING_DRUNK`), a hat thrown down. And the other side, so despair isn't only an ending: someone who sees it coming steps in (a friend, a kind stranger by their mind's pity, or the player close by): a hand on the shoulder, a few words, and they come away; the town talks of it after, and the one saved remembers who stopped them. Each through `Core/Despair.cs` (`Method`, by the person and the place) and `WestworldScript.Despair.cs` (its clips, a fallback), on the Testing row's turn, and the readme's content note. Westworld concepts only, never its lines: its own dialogue is HBO's writing. Public-domain quotations are fine even where the show used them too, such as Shakespeare's "These violent delights have violent ends", Poe or Dickinson). 4. Memory that behaves like memory (fading and rewriting, places hold memories, recognised by horse, clothes and habits). 5. Inner lives (opinions of each other, dreams from real memories, changing their minds; thoughts from state: needs, mood, Big Five traits, one standing worry; era from GET_CLOCK_YEAR, 1899 or 1907). 6. Interaction menu (opened from the lock-on menu as Flirt is; options and rails in roadmap.md). 7. Spreading saloon brawls (the ladder built offline in the night of 2026-10-28, `Core/Escalation.cs`, and read by the Director behind `[Mind] MindLive` since item 16 (2026-10-29, after the run closed), `Scenes.Ladder.cs`; the shot still never played; Lex, 2026-10-27: an argument may go all the way to a lethal shootout when the people's inner lives, backgrounds and surroundings lead there, true to 1899: most killings were sudden quarrels over drink, honour, money or land, at close range, often in a saloon at night between men with history, not noon duels; many towns banned guns inside town limits. No count limit and no Pacing cap for it: the conditions make it rare, not a quota. De-escalation by feeling too (apology, a joke, backing down, walking off, a friend stepping in, a drink offered). Built on Reasons, Exchanges, the standoff, the callout, StandUp and Grudges; deaths go through the ledger and RecordFates. Built 2026-10-26, untested in game: rough handling anywhere with a surface, the drunk, the barkeep; see "Trouble between people"; Lex, 2026-10-25, first: the game's saloon encounters as NPC-to-NPC scenes, each from a reason in `Reasons.cs`, never a timer: the bar slam first, then the drunk who challenges someone and passes out, in place at first and 'take it outside' once walk-overs are reliable; never by starting the game's own encounter scripts, which own their peds and have no known way to be launched). 8. Answering letters (tones, money, a meeting; later writing your own). 9. Narratives, and worries the player can change (a saved override per arc). 10. Town events (one framework grown from the wedding code: dance, election, circuit judge and gallows, fair, Wild West show, raisings). 11. New arrivals (train time, departures, a ledger per town). 12. Natural voices (Piper). 13. Stalkers on horseback and revenants at camp. 14. The game's real words, every voice (Lex, 2026-10-27: the GPU route, later): export every ambient voice bank once with OpenIV (PEDS_00-10.rpf; 332 voices, about 72,000 takes, 32 hours of speech) into `C:\mods\voice-test\banks\`, and transcribe them on Lex's PC with `transcribe_all.py` (resumable, below-normal priority; on the RTX 5080 about an hour once NVIDIA's cuBLAS 12 and cuDNN 9 are in the `voice-test` venv, which today stops at `cublas64_12.dll`; on the CPU a day or more). One time, on Lex's PC only: the transcripts never leave it. What it gives: takes chosen voice by voice by what they really say, first despair's beats (can't go on, entrapment, burden, apology, farewell, prayer, disbelief), shipped as a list of voice, line and take per moment (our own data, no words, no audio), so every player hears them; the real words in subtitles (`voices\words.txt`, `Core/VoiceBook.cs`, already plumbed); and the index item 28 builds on. 15. A local language model on the GPU (full mode, Lex 2026-10-26; the mind design's language layer): it words thoughts, diaries and letters and never decides behaviour; the written lines are the fallback. 16. Night encounters (in: the crying woman, the hermits who woke, the gathering, the night visitor, the supper invitation (the player version, 2026-10-27); the voice below the floor (night 2026-10-28); next: a version of each where it happens to someone else (the supper's needs a homestead to send him to); see "Proposal: macabre encounters" in the private design doc; folklore themes in roadmap.md). 17. A living street, next after item 1 (Lex, 2026-10-25): people treat each other the way the game treats the player. The game's player-reaction scripts can't be pointed at NPCs, so use its building blocks aimed at NPCs (shocking events via `ADD_SHOCKING_EVENT_FOR_ENTITY`, ambient speech aimed at a ped, look-at, turn, step aside, cower, flee), driven by ties, worries and reputation. In order: (a) acknowledgement in passing between people with a tie; (b) shocking events for NPC fights, standoffs and deaths, so the game's own crowd reacts (watch for escalation, as with Abner); (c) reputation: enRAGE's outlaws and nemeses in town stared at and avoided, respected trades greeted; (d) appearance: bloodied, drunk, soaked; (e) the town keeps time: routines by hour and weekday (item 2's routines), only for people enRAGE isn't scheduling with its habit plans. Same rules as ever: hands off anyone enRAGE or a mission is using, only near the player. 18. Moods that carry (proposed overnight 2026-10-26, when Lex asked for roadmap additions: a mood from the morning, news or the weather lasts hours, colours thoughts, words and reasons, and spreads through a crowd). 19. Vices and habits (the drinker after bad news, the gambler and the rent, the pious and the Sabbath, driving reasons as worries do). 20. Grudges that travel (a wrong told to kin and friends; one of them takes it up later, through `Family` and the ties). 21. Small kindnesses (the prosocial counterweight: help up after a fall, a round bought, standing up to a bully; remembered and repaid; `charity` is the first). 22. A mind underneath (the private Claude Doc "RealNPCs: the NPC Mind", 2026-10-26: a people ledger and death ledger shared with enRAGE, a layered mind, appraisal through 1899's display rules to a catalogue of what the game can show, levels of detail). Built by the night of 2026-10-28, all in shadow: step 1 the ledger (0.6.3), 2 traits, 3 needs and concerns, 4 appraisal, emotion and mood, 5 the display rules and the catalogue, with the reactions on the mapping (behind MindLive), 6 choosing (reported, nobody tasked), 7 the levels of detail (`[Mind] MindLevels`, off), 8 memory v1 on the saved memories (behind MindLive), 9 the language layer's offline half (no model yet), 10 beliefs and the self v1 (reported). Still to build: the actions acted out, the language model on the GPU, the Awakening read from the beliefs. 23. The year as it was (the 1899 and 1907 calendars, seasons by region, 1907's events). 24. Seeing what happened (who's who, Overheard, raised-voice cues). 25. The epilogue remembers (Arthur's regulars carried into John's world). 26. Nights together (0.6.3 first version: `Interest.ForPlayer`, trysts; next: places, a price, the morning after, a husband who finds out). 27. The supper invitation switched on (Lex, 2026-10-27: built that day, off until tested): check the cash units with its Testing row (the log's `SUPPER: the cash read N (as cents, $X.YY)` against the HUD), play it through once for real, then on by default; after that the NPC version (someone else invited, and missing next day), once homestead positions are known. 28. Our own sentences in the game's voices (Lex, 2026-10-27), for people with each other and with the player as their inner minds grow: from item 14's index, whole takes chained into one thing said ("I've been better." then "Take care of yourself.", a pause between), never cut mid-take (the game plays whole recorded lines only, and splicing its audio outside it would be off-position, choppy and unshippable); truly new words only through item 12 (a local text-to-speech voice of our own, never a copy of an actor's voice). Design rule for every mind system from now on: the mind decides what is meant (a speech act: greet, farewell, apology, complaint, warning, gratitude, gossip, plea, with its feeling and whom it's to, `Language.cs`'s request is the written half), and a voice layer realises it in this person's voice: a take whose words carry it, else chained takes, else (later) a voice of our own, else silence with the thought over the head; never a line picked by name alone where its words could say something else (each voice records its own words for a name). 29. The minds drive every body, enRAGE's people too, and a nemesis system of our own (Lex, 2026-10-27, after the 0.6.3 release): the inner minds are meant to steer everyone by what they think and feel, so RealNPCs takes over the bodies of all of enRAGE's people and, later, replaces its nemesis encounters (town nemeses who spot you and come over, patrols, camps, bounty hunters, revenge) with ones driven by the minds (grudges, fear, hate, plans). enRAGE stays the record keeper (its roster, history and saves, written through its own methods; its look fingerprints, so records are still never re-dressed). In steps: (1) let go of calm town nemeses through enRAGE's own `ClearTownMan(tm, delete: false)` (its `OurRole.Town` men are its live nemesis actors, tasked every tick: coming over, confronting, fighting, leaving and deleted; the calm ones are not hunting you, not coming over, not fighting, not leaving, with no plan due), so despair, stalking and scenes can use them; (2) a takeover and hand-back for every role; (3) our own nemesis system, and enRAGE's encounter drivers switched off through its own settings where it has them. Until each step is built, `MayDirect` and `RecordFreeWhyNot` keep turning enRAGE's driven people away (the rule under "Rules that matter" stands until then). Minds already reach all of them (F7 "minds yes"). 30. Encounters people do, not encounters that happen (Lex, 2026-10-27): every street scene and encounter (the preacher, the fall, the round, the callout...) should in the end be something a person does because their mind and the moment lead there (item 11's choosing, the needs, the feelings), not a timed roll; until then they stay paced events. Ones that spawn someone (the traveller, the visitor, the supper host) wait on a decision about permanence: where a spawned person goes when their part is done, and whether they stay in the world as someone. Several street scenes at once (Lex, 2026-10-27: up to five near the player): the Director holds one scene (`_live`); several means a list, each with its own cast and timing, never sharing a person, and every "a scene is going on" check asking which. Meanwhile a Testing-page row stops a scene that is out of your sight (no one in it within 25 m and in view; `TestMakeRoom`) instead of refusing. 31. The game's own townsfolk in play (Lex, 2026-10-27): most of a town's crowd belongs to the game's own scripts (mission entities from its town and ambient scripts), which RealNPCs never directs, so in Valentine most of the people you see can't take part (the fall test's `CrowdCounted` shows the split). In steps, the later ones after the minds are much further on: (1) measure, town by town, who they are (the crowd count; which scripts own them: shopkeepers and fixed posts, scenario idlers, the ambient walkers); (2) borrow and give back the ones whose script doesn't depend on them (an idler, a walker), never a shopkeeper, a post or anyone in a story mission, handing them back so the game's script carries on; (3) in the end a population of our own: residents with routines by hour and weekday (items 2 and 17e) whom the minds drive, spawned and let go out of view, the game's ambient people left only where nothing of ours fills the street. 32. A test cast, kept in the save (Lex, 2026-10-27: a test found no woman for the lost child in Valentine, and the bully needs three free men): the Testing page makes the people a test needs (a woman of a mother's age and trade, two old men, a devout preacher and a listener, three men for a bully), each a resident with an identity and a mind like any other, kept in the save for our testing (Lex) and marked as the test cast so they never stand for the story (no records, no enRAGE roles, nobody's kin), made again near where they live when you come back to the town, and let go from the Testing page (one or all). Built the same night, moved up by Lex for the testing (see "Testing"): brought on demand by a Testing row, kept in the save; made again by themselves when you come back to their town (`[Testing] TestCastReturns`, night 2026-10-28). Tests also loosen their conditions (the fall and the lost child may take someone standing about, and a refusal counts what stood in the way).  33. The round bought, acted out (Lex, 2026-10-28, seen in game: the woman's cheer line played ("Come on!"), the three cheers were voiced, but nothing moved: no glass raised, nobody turning to the bar, the barkeep pouring nothing): the saloon's props and scenario points (the drinking scenarios at the bar, `WORLD_HUMAN_DRINKING`, a raised glass clip), the buyer walking to the bar and the barkeep answering, each cheerer turning and raising a glass; part of roadmap 17 (a living street) and 30 (encounters people do). The cheer gestures (`happy_cheer_f_*`, upper body) are given but weren't seen; check with the in-game probe whether the dict loads and the clips play on a standing ped. Items 6 to 11, 15, 16 and 22 to 25 took their detail from roadmap.md (repo root; Lex's overnight plan of 2026-10-26, with idea banks marked Want/Maybe/No, designs and Claude Code prompts Q1-Q7 and P1-P28); its status block at the top says what 0.6.2 superseded. Ask Lex before adding anything not on this list. (Everyone is someone, added 2026-10-24 at Lex's request, is the foundation: residents in `Core/Residents.cs`, enRAGE records stay separate.)

## Acknowledgement in passing (overnight 2026-10-27, Tier 2.1, roadmap 17a)

- `Core/Passing.cs`: `KindOf(warmth, romantic)` (sweet = a warm romantic tie, warm, hostile, known), `Acknowledged(tieKind)` (never a lift: the mark doesn't know who), `For(...)` gives the `Act` (gesture hattip | nod | wave | sweet | spit | none; the other's answer nod | wave | sweet | look (an enemy always looks back) | none; whether the first speaks: warm 60%, sweet 35%, known 25%, hostile never; the look's length; one thought, the first one's or (45%, only when they answered) the other's), `Spitter` (Cold, Proud, Hothead: a spit at an enemy 30%), `AFirst` (an enemy's look from the hotter head, else the tie's `Doer`, else the seed), `Thought(kind, role, gesture, temper, ...)`: pools keyed `kind|role[|temper group or gesture]`, roles did (greeted, stared, spat), answered (greeted back), stared (was stared at), spat (was spat at); a gesture's own lines join (a hat tip is only thought of after one). `Clip` (the standing silent sets, checked against anims.lua; the side toward the other where both exist), `Greeting(hour)` (GREET_MORNING 05-12 or GREET_EVENING 18-23 first, then GREET_GENERAL_FAMILIAR, HOWS_IT_GOING, through `Speech.PlayFirst`; no Speech set changed, so banks.txt stands; overheard, so no subtitle).
- Game (`Game/WestworldScript.Passing.cs`, `PassingStep` in Tick04 after the court thoughts, `Safe("passing")`): a look every 1 s at the people within 35 m who are someone lasting (`NodKnow`: `LastingId` with the temper, cached per handle for 10 s or until the model changes, pruned past 300; re-checked at the start, so a reused handle never nods as someone else), not story models, not in a Director scene, a wake act, real-voice talk, at a counter, an encounter, speaking or already in a nod or a sight reaction; pairs with a tie (`_store.Ties`, both near, not a lift) 0.8-7 m apart and closer than at the last look by 0.3 m (`_nodApart`: coming toward each other, not walking together or already past), one of them over 0.6 m/s, the nearer within 25 m of you and one in view; the one who acts may be directed and faces the other within 75 degrees (`Facing`); `Pacing.WhyNot("nod", pair)` (60% per 45-minute window, a pair once in 45 game minutes); 20 s between nods (`NodGapMs`), never while the Director is busy. `NodPlay`: both look, at 450 ms the first's gesture (upper body 8|16, nobody in a scenario) and greeting, 1.3 s the answer, then the one thought (`_ticker.Push(name + ", thinking", ..., "near", handle)`, only in view); let go the moment a scene or a talk takes either (`NodStill`). `IsAcknowledging(h)` keeps nods and sight reactions out of `ReasonCandidates` (scenes and falls) and `TalkLeftOut`, and out of each other. `PassingAbort` on a mission start, shutdown, a load and the wipe. Nothing saved but the pair's rest; no tie, memory or WorldEvent. `LastingId` gained an overload with the temper. Testing row `nod` (`NodFire`: the nearest pair with a tie within 10 m, standing or not, the roll and rest skipped). Log `NOD a & b: kind (gesture, the answer, a word, apart, from you)`, `NOD thought, name: ...`; Detail: the clip, the greeting played or not, a thought out of view. F7 `TEST passing:`. Offline: ticktest `PassingTest` (143,360 acknowledgements: kinds, enemies silent and looking back, spitters, no hat tip for women, every clip in its sex's set and on its side, the one thought true to the act, Coward and Hothead never share an enemy's line, the hotter head first, no lift, greetings by the hour, line shapes). Reviewed the same night (a subagent): the closing test, the facing test, one thought, the gesture-true pools, the race guard and the 20 s gap came from it. Not yet seen in game: whether `_l_` clips mean toward the left or with the left hand.

## Reactions to a sight (overnight 2026-10-27, Tier 2.2, roadmap 17d)

- `Core/Sights.cs`: `Feeling(state, temper, regard, seed)`: what they already think of him first (afraid -> afraid in any state; hostile -> cold for blood and drink, disgust for dirt and wet; fond -> concerned, pity for dirt, amused at drink), then `Minds.TemperGroup`: bloodied afraid | sizing | cold | concerned; filthy disgust | amused | pity; drunk afraid | disgust | amused; soaked concerned | amused (the plain split by the seed). `StrangerTemper(seed)` (half plain, the rest timid, hot, proud, cold, greedy, loyal), so a crowd of unknowns isn't all concerned. `For` gives the `Look`: gesture surprised | headshake | spit (only `Passing.Spitter` tempers) | dismiss | none (the afraid glance 0.8 s and look away; the sizing look 3 s and say nothing), words only where the feeling has them, each with a written line said to him (`Spoken`, the subtitle when the recording's words aren't known): GREET_BLOODY for the concerned about blood ("You're bleeding, mister."), GREET_MUDDY / PLAYER_LOOKING_WEIRD for the amused (its farmhand takes are jibes about his looks), VOCAL_FX_DISAPPROVE for disgust (wordless: no subtitle); `ForOther` (an onlooker of a bloodied fighter, `{who}` by first name), `Clip` (silent sets, checked against anims.lua), `StateOf` (bloodied, drunk, filthy, soaked in that order), `Remarkable` (drunk not in a saloon; filthy only in Saint Denis or Blackwater unless the dirt is 0.9+).
- Game (`Game/WestworldScript.Sights.cs`, `SightsStep` in Tick04 after `PassingStep`, `Safe("sights")`): `SightSample` every 500 ms: a health drop of 12% of max in one sample is a hurt (`_hurtAt`, bloodied for 120 game minutes); swimming or submerged 0.45+ is wet (`_wetAt`; washes the blood; soaked for 30 game minutes, not while it rains); `_IS_PED_DRUNK` or `_GET_PED_DRUNKNESS` over 0.25; `_GET_PED_DIRT_LEVEL` (by hash 0x0105FEE8F9091255, named in the natives database with (ped, BOOL useCompositeLayer) -> float; not named in the .NET API) read once at the probe and then every 5 s, and only while its readings are 0-1.5 (else filthy is off for the session, logged); `SIGHT probe:` once. `SightsReset` (death, a load, the wipe, shutdown, a jump of 150 m between samples: fast travel or a respawn). Every 1 s (20 s after a reaction, never while the Director is busy): up to two people within 10 m with clear sight of you and facing you within 75 degrees (`SightCant`: `Natives.Bystander`, not in a scene, act, talk, nod, at a counter, an encounter or speaking, `MayDirectWhyNot`), their regard (`SightRegard`: a record's fear 40+ or hate 50+; `Engine.SawYouViolent(id, 3)` from their Minds memories; a townsperson's Fear, Grudge, Like 40+), once per person (lasting id, else handle and model) and state a game day (`_sightSeen`, pruned, not saved), three per state in a town a day (`_sightTown`), `Pacing.WhyNot("sight", who = the state)` (40%, the roll held for half a game hour in a town; 30 game minutes between any two, 90 for the same state); 1.6 s apart: the look, at 400 ms the gesture (upper body, not in a scenario) and any word (within 8 m: worded lines through `Feed.Say` toYou with `Speech.WhenFirst`, subtitled; the wordless one through `Speech.PlayFirst`), at 900 ms the thought over the head (only in view); `MayDirectWhyNot` only as each beat starts, and let go if a scene or talk takes them. Bloodied fighters: anyone in `_restMsOf` at `FoughtRestMs`, 20 s to 15 min after their scene, under 85% health, within 25 m of you: one look per fight (`_bloodiedSeen` by handle and the scene's time) for one onlooker within 10 m with sight of them (not another fighter). Nothing saved; no memory or WorldEvent. Testing row `sight` (`SightFire`: your state, or each in turn). Log `SIGHT name: you look X -> feeling (temper, regard; gesture, line, m)`, `SIGHT thought, ...`; Detail the clip, the sound, your state changing, a reset. F7 `TEST sights:`. Offline: ticktest `SightsTest` (6,400 reactions with regard: feelings by regard and temper, words only where they fit and always with a line to subtitle, the afraid silent and still, clips, lines, `Remarkable`, strangers' tempers). Reviewed the same night: the regard, the rarity (the old rule passed in about 2 s), the subtitles, the dirt guard and the resets came from it. Not yet seen in game: the dirt level's scale and whether a 12% drop catches a fistfight.

## A fall and a helping hand (overnight 2026-10-27, Tier 2.3, roadmap 21)

- `Core/Scenes.Fall.cs`: `FallAnswer(warmth, manner, temper, seed)`: a warm tie helps (gruffly if sharp), a hostile one passes (a Hothead, Proud or Cold man sneers), else by `Exchanges.Manner`: kind help, cold pass (20% help), sharp gruff 60% else pass. `Fall(c, seed, answer, old)`: kind `help` or `passby`, A the one who saw, B the one who fell: B "fall" (not the old) then "sitdown" with a cry; help: A "meet", "kneel", a word each, A "stand", B "getup", B's thanks ("thanks" voice), A's parting word and "walkaway"; gruff: meet, getup, a word, thanks, walkaway; sneer: "face", a jibe and walkaway, B getup and a bitter word; pass: face, walkaway, B getup alone. `FallThanksYou`. `FallSamples` for the tests.
- Director: acts "fall" (`SET_PED_TO_RAGDOLL` 1.1-1.7 s), "sitdown" (`WORLD_HUMAN_SIT_GROUND` in place, `Live.Sat`), "getup" (`CLEAR_PED_TASKS`; reports the scene happened for help and passby, so its tie and memories are noted then: `_pendingNote` takes help and passby); `meet` accepts 2.4 m when the one waiting is sat on the ground (not the 3.6 m for a scenario); `End` gets anyone still sitting up.
- Ties: help +1 and passby -1, both one-sided (A the doer), `HelpDid/Done`, `PassDid/Done`, town lines `TownHelp`/`TownPassby` (the town talks of help too). Minds: help in warmth, passby in slight, one-sided, help felt +2 by the one helped; pools `help|did:doer|...`, `help|did:done|...`, `help|saw|fresh`, `passby|...`, and `helpup|did|...` for the player's hand up (`deed:kindness`, sub `helpup`, `{you}`).
- Game (`Game/WestworldScript.Kindness.cs`, from `DramaTick` out of doors before the reason scenes): a look every 20 s; the one who falls is a `ReasonCandidates` person walking (0.8-2.6 m/s), in view within 14 m, outdoors, not a record, in a scenario or in water, on level enough ground (`GroundBelow` from half a metre above them at their feet and 0.7 m all round, within 40 cm at three of the four points, so a boardwalk's edge passes; 25 cm at all four until Lex found it turning down even streets, 2026-10-27; never a roof or awning overhead); a refusal counts what stood in the way among the named people (further than 14 m, not on your screen, something between you, one of enRAGE's records, sitting, standing in one spot, on stairs or a slope...) and the whole crowd within 14 m (`CrowdCounted`: the game's own scripts' people, lawmen, children, riding, fighting or fleeing, busy, free); a test may tip over someone standing, leaning or working on their feet in a scenario (the tumble breaks it), never someone sitting (`IS_PED_SITTING`); real falls stay for people walking; the one who sees it another candidate within 9 m with clear sight of them, a tie first; `Pacing.WhyNot("fall", town, faller)` (6%, 150 game minutes between falls, 600 in a town, a day for the same person). `_fallWatch` (15 s): you within 2.5 m while they're down (sitting or ragdolled) and the helper not already within 3 m: `_director.Abort`, the helper wanders off, they face you and thank you (`Feed.Say` toYou, the "thanks" voice, subtitled), an unsaved resident is kept, `Engine.HelpedUp` (Talks +1, affection +10 if nobody else stopped else +6, a `helpup` memory 60/50) and enRAGE's like +3 for its townsfolk (`LikeMore`, an audited write). Testing row `fall` (`FallScene(test)`: walking not needed, the rarity and rests skipped). Log `KIND fall: X fell; Y helps them up | walks past | ... (a friend | no friend | strangers, temper, m)`, `KIND fall: you gave X a hand up`; F7 `TEST kindness:`. Offline: ticktest `FallTest` (answers by tie and manner, all 96 scenes, the old never tumble, ties, minds, pools). Not yet seen in game: whether the sit scenario takes straight after a ragdoll.
- Reviewed the same night (a subagent; fixed in the follow-up commit): your hand up needs them sat on the ground (the sit scenario, 3 s into the scene, never mid-tumble), you on foot (not mounted or in a vehicle) and either 1.5 m nearer than when they fell or standing by them 1.5 s; the one who falls is 3 m or more from you; a test hand-up keeps nobody and changes nothing (no `KeepResident`, `HelpedUp` or `LikeMore`); they wander off 4 s after thanking you (`FallWanderStep`), and the Director's `End` sets the fallen of a help or passby scene wandering too; the tumble is 0.9-1.3 s so the sit takes; the pacing's rarity is checked before the scan (then the person's own rest); the passby kept when you help late is from 9 s on; "boards" lines reworded (the streets are dirt).

- `Core/Scenes.StandUp.cs`: `StandsUp(warmthToB, warmthToA, temper, woman, seed)`: never a Coward, an enemy of B or a friend of A; a friend of B 70%; a Loyal or Proud man with no tie 35%; a woman with no tie never. `BacksOff(bullyTemper, standerTemper, standerWoman, seed)`: Hothead 40%, Proud or Cold 55%, else 75%, +10 when a Proud or Hothead man steps in. `StandUp(scene, cast, seed, backsOff)`: before the first `slam` or `fight` beat, C's `cb:stoodup` then C's line ("intervene" voice, "face": everyone turns to C); backs off: A's line ("storm", walkaway), B thanks C, the rest dropped, kind `argue`; else A's answer ("angry", "face", turning back to B) and the scene goes on. `StandUpSamples`. Not built: the bully turning on C (the Director's fight is fixed to `Cast[0..1]`).
- Ties: `defend` +1 and `faced` -1, both one-sided with C the doer (`DefendDid/Done`, `FacedDid/Done`); Minds: defend in warmth, faced in quarrel, one-sided, with their own pools.
- Game (`Game/WestworldScript.StandUp.cs`): `StandUpFor` from both scene builders (reason and chance, after `AddBarkeep`, before `AddBystander`): a rough or brawl of two, a `ReasonCandidates` person within 10 m of the pair (a friend of B first, then the nearest) with clear sight of B and a first name unlike theirs, `Pacing.WhyNot("standup", town, C)` (45%, an hour between, twice a day for one person); C becomes the scene's third. `StoodUp` (the Director's `Custom("stoodup")`): the ties between lasting people (`TIE ...: defend`, `faced`) and `RememberScene("defend", C, B)` and `("faced", C, A)` under `Safe("minds")`; a test notes nothing. Testing row `scene:standup` (a brawl with someone made to step in; since 2026-10-28 a test takes turns, the fight going on first and then the bully backing off, `_standUpTestTurn`: a plain bully backs off three times in four, so Lex's tests seldom showed the fight). Log `KIND stood up: C steps in for B against A -> backs off | tells them to keep out (...)`; F7 in `TEST kindness:`. The fall: a hand up from you after the one who saw them had walked past still notes the passby (7 s into the scene). Offline: ticktest `StandUpTest`. Not yet seen in game.
- Reviewed the same night (fixed in the follow-up commit): only a rough has a bully; in a brawl (which either man may start) the one who steps in breaks it up between both and takes no side (no defend or faced ties); they're never someone sat or leaning in a scenario, and walk over first (`approach`) when more than 3.5 m off; their lines work before any blow; a backed-off scene is `Scene.NoteAtCue` (its argue tie and memories wait for `StoodUp`, so a walk-over that never arrives leaves nothing) and A and B get `FoughtRestMs`; B thanks C to C's face (`Beat.To`, the Director's `other` from it, and a new act `faceone` that turns only that one). Ties: a warm kindness between sweethearts (help, defend, comfort, round, charity) keeps the tie's romantic kind and doesn't move its stage, so a courting couple stays courting (`Ties.Note`).
