## The night visitor (overnight 2026-10-27, Tier 2.8, roadmap 16)

- `Core/Visitor.cs`: `Hour` (00:00-04:00), `Morning(town, seed)` (the town's talk next morning, no names), `Fled(seed)` (one who ran from it). `Engine.VisitorSeen(town, now)`: a `news` `LaterAct` (no person) due the next morning 07:00-09:00, titled "Word around <town>".
- Game (`Game/WestworldScript.Visitor.cs`, in the encounter framework: `EncounterLive`, `EncounterWhat`, `EncountersTick` (its step, then the left-behind body's), the roll after the hermit's (`VisitorNotNow`: a town via `TownOnly`, out of doors, the hours, once a night, no lawman within 60 m; then Pacing "visitor"'s rests; `NightVisitorChance`), `RequestEncounter`/`StageEncounter` (kind `visitor`), `EndEncounters`): `StartVisitor` (`NoteBig` unless a test); `VisitorSpawn` (`EncounterSpot` 30-50 m out of sight, nobody within 8 m of it): the body (a spawned ped, health 0) and the figure 1 m off (`VisitorDress`: alpha 170, invincible, events blocked, no weapons) crouched (`WORLD_HUMAN_CROUCH_INSPECT`); phases crouched (you within 25 m: it stands, turns, stares; someone out late within 15 m with sight of it first runs: `TASK_SMART_FLEE_PED`, the scared voice, `Visitor.Fled` over their head), staring (seen, then not: deleted, `away` 8-20 s), away (`EncounterSpot` 12-20 m behind you, out of sight: it stands and looks at you), behind (seen, then not, or 40 s unseen: over); aimed at, damaged or ragdolled: gone at once (`VisitorThere`). `EndVisitor`: the figure deleted; the body deleted unseen, else kept as `_visitorBody` until you're 150 m off or it's off screen (`VisitorBodyStep`); `RecordEvent("encounter:visitor")`; `MarkDone("visitor")` and `VisitorSeen` unless a test. Testing row `visitor` (any hour; still a town, out of doors, no lawman). Log `VISITOR ...`; F7 `TEST visitor:`. Offline: ticktest `VisitorTest`. Not yet seen in game: the alpha on a ped, and whether a ped set to health 0 lies as a body.
- Reviewed the same night (fixed in the follow-up commit): counted against the night's big moments once, when it's really out (`Pacing.Note("visitor")` in `VisitorSpawn`; `StartVisitor` no longer calls `NoteBig` too, which spent both of the night's two); over at once when you go 90 m from it, into another town or indoors, when the night turns (not a test) or after eight minutes (`VisitorGoneWhy`, so a death, a respawn or a fast travel can't bring it to you elsewhere, and a failed spawn behind you counts its tries); a figure in view at the end walks off and goes when unseen, as the body does (`_visitorLeft`, a list; `VisitorLeftClear` on a mission or shutdown); no street scene starts while it's out (`DramaTick`); the one who runs from it is free of everything else (`NotFree`).

## A stranded traveller asking the way (overnight 2026-10-27, Tier 2.9)

- `Core/Travellers.cs`: `Towns` (the middle of nine towns on the map, x and y, near enough to point the way), `Nearest`, `Hour` (07:00-19:00), the lines: `Hail`, `Ask(town)`, `Thanks(town)`, `Refused`, `Ignored`.
- Game (`Game/WestworldScript.Travellers.cs`, in the encounter framework like the visitor: `EncounterLive`, `EncounterWhat`, its step, the roll after the visitor's (`TravellerNotNow`: not in a town, the hours, once a day, you on a road by `PATHFIND.IS_POINT_ON_ROAD`; then Pacing "traveller"'s rests; `TravellerChance`), `RequestEncounter`/`StageEncounter` (kind `traveller`), `EndEncounters`): `TravellerSpawn`: a road node (`GET_NTH_CLOSEST_VEHICLE_NODE`, read from three 8-byte slots) 60-110 m ahead the way you face that the camera can't see (`TravellerSpot`), else `EncounterSpot` 50-80 m behind; a man, events blocked, no weapons, standing still. Phases: waiting (you within 30 m in view: he faces you, waves (`happy_wave_fl_001`, the male speaker set), `Feed.Say` "A traveller" toYou with the greet voice), hailed (within 8 m: he asks the way, mild voice; past 60 m or a minute: ignored), asked (you stopped within 4 m 3 s, or a greeting through `GreetedSpecial` -> `TravellerGreeted`: answered; an antagonize through `Insults` -> backs off, `TASK_SMART_FLEE_PED`; 30 m or 30 s: ignored); answered: he turns to the town, points (`neutral_point_l/r_001`), thanks you (thanks voice) and walks 120 m toward it (`TASK_FOLLOW_NAV_MESH_TO_COORD`, events back on); ignored: a mild sound, wanders, his thought over his head if you're close. Over when you're 120 m off, he's out of view 15 s, or 90 s pass; released unseen (the dead stay). `RecordEvent("encounter:traveller")`, `MarkDone("traveller")` unless a test. Testing row `traveller` (any hour, needn't be on a road). Log `TRAVELLER ...`; F7 `TEST traveller:`. Offline: ticktest `TravellerTest`. Not yet seen in game: the road node read and the spot ahead.
- A snakebite on the trail (overnight 2026-10-27, Tier 4): `TravellerRun.Bitten`, `Travellers.BittenChance` (30%; a test takes turns, lost then snakebit, `_travellerTests`): he spawns in `WORLD_HUMAN_SIT_GROUND` instead of standing still, hails and asks with the scared voice (`BittenHail`, `BittenAsk`, subtitled), doesn't turn or wave; answered (a greeting or stopping by him), `CLEAR_PED_TASKS` (he gets up), `BittenThanks`, `Engine.SnakebiteTold` (a `news` `LaterAct` 120-300 game minutes on, `BittenNews`; not in a test), then he walks toward the town as the lost one does; antagonized he stays sat and says `BittenRefused`; ridden past he stays sat and thinks `BittenIgnored`; either way (not a test) `Engine.SnakebiteLeft`: on `Travellers.BittenDies` (50%, by the seed) a `news` `LaterAct` 240-480 game minutes on that he was found dead (`BittenDied`; no death is booked anywhere, he was nobody's), else a log line that he made it; its event is `encounter:snakebite`. Reviewed the same night: the town's word (seen to, or left and found dead) is queued in `EndTraveller`, only for a man still alive then (`Helped`, `Left`), worded for how you came (`Mounted`: "a stranger on a horse", "walked right by"); he sits 3.5 m to one side of the road node, not in the road. F7 `TEST traveller:` counts snakebit and helped. Offline: in `TravellerTest` (`Travellers.Lines`). Not yet seen in game: whether the sit scenario reads as hurt.
- The one who never arrived (overnight 2026-10-27, Tier 4, toward the supper invitation's NPC version): a lost traveller (not snakebit or robbed) ridden past or run off (`TravellerRun.Left`, set in both paths now) and alive when it ends: `Engine.TravellerMissing`, on `Travellers.GoesMissing` (15%, by the seed) a `news` `LaterAct` 900-1800 game minutes on (`Travellers.Missing`); not in a test. Nothing else: no homestead, no body. Offline: in `TravellerTest`.
- A robbery on the road (overnight 2026-10-27, Tier 4, the NPC version): `TravellerRun.Robbed` (`Travellers.RobbedChance` 20%, after the snakebite's 30; a test takes turns lost, snakebit, robbed), two of the region's outlaws (`Executions.GangOf`/`GangModels`, as the execution's) spawned 2.8 m in front of him with revolvers aimed (`RobberySpawn`: events blocked, kept tasks, `_encounterPeds`, `_cameAtYouSeen` renewed every step, so shooting them is self-defence), his hands up (`TASK_HANDS_UP` facing the first); phase `robbing` (`RobberyStep`): nothing until you're within 40 m (75 in view), else over after 4 minutes; noticed, 20 s (10 once they've warned you at 28 m, said to you); threats and pleas by turns, overheard; his thought over his head; you step in as with the execution (one of them hurt or gone, 1.2 s of aim, within 6 m): `MakeHostile`, combat, phase `fight`, saved when none stands within 70 m of him or 3 minutes pass (his thanks, said to you); else they take it and walk off 150 m away from you, and he tells you if you're within 30 m; then he walks toward the town (`RobberyGo`). `EndTraveller` releases the men as the execution does (the living only unseen, never one enRAGE has looked at) and queues the town's word (`Engine.RobberyTold`, `RobNews` saved or not) only if he was seen and lived; its event is `encounter:robbery`. F7 `TEST traveller:` counts robbed and saved. Offline: in `TravellerTest`. Not built: the player version (they rob you). Reviewed the same night (fixed in the follow-up commit): the hold-up stands 9 m off the road node, and coming close counts as stepping in only under 2 m/s and within 4 m (riding on past, as they tell you, brought you within the old 6 m and they opened fire); one of them dead counts only by your hand (`GET_PED_SOURCE_OF_DEATH`), and a lock-on is a gun on him as free aim is; saved only when they're all down or the living are 70 m from him and from you (three minutes of fighting and he slips away, unthanked, no news); the town's word only for a robbery that ran its course (`going` or `pausing`); he has his word with you to your face before walking on (`RobberyPause`, 3.5 s); the robbers going off with it stop being "came at you" and are free to react; at the end every one left in the world, and him, is let go of their orders (`CLEAR_PED_TASKS`, keep-task and event blocking off) and a deleted robber's handle leaves `_cameAtYouSeen`; a failed robbers' spawn tries again elsewhere; lines that said "two" when one might have spawned now don't; the event's doer is the road agents and its target the traveller.
- Pacing "traveller" is not a big moment (it doesn't use up the night's two); noted when he's set out.

## The supper invitation, the player version (daytime 2026-10-27, roadmap 16)

- Lex's OK (2026-10-27 18:33, an exception to the day's read-only rule): it takes the player's money and moves him while the screen is dark, which he can't undo. `[Encounters] SupperInvitation` and `SupperChance` (12), settings version 81; off until it's been tested (Lex, the same evening; settings version 82 turns an 81 ini's "on", which was that default, off).
- `Core/Supper.cs`: `Hour` (17:30-21:00), `Take(cashCents, seed)` (75-100% of the cash by the seed, never more than $250, nothing from under two dollars: it should sting, not ruin a save), `Dollars` (rounded, for the words), `HoursToDawn(hour)` (to 06:00, at least two), the lines: `Invite`, `Lead`, `LetGo` ("the latchstring's always out"), `Backs` (a gun on him), `HostThought` (robbery only, never murder: what he thinks while he smiles), `Waking(dollars)` (with and without money; each says or implies his cup), `Lines()` for the tests.
- Game (`Game/WestworldScript.Supper.cs`, in the encounter framework like the traveller: `EncounterLive`, `EncounterWhat`, its step, a seventh kind in `TryEncounter`'s turn (`SupperNotNow`: not in a town, the ticker on (his thought is the only tell), the hours, once a day, no lawman within 60 m, you on a road; `SupperChance`), `RequestEncounter`/`StageEncounter` (kind `supper`), `EndEncounters`, F7 `TEST encounters:` "supper ..."): `SupperSpawn` puts a man (events blocked, no weapons, standing) on a road node ahead out of sight (`TravellerSpot`, else `EncounterSpot` 50-80 m; `Road`) and picks "the house" 35 m to one side on dry ground (`StandSpot`, `SupperDry`). Phases: waiting (you within 30 m in view: he faces you, waves, `Feed.Say` "A stranger" toYou with the greet voice; 2.5 s on, his thought over his head), invited (you stopped on foot within 4 m for 3 s once his thought has shown, or a greeting through `GreetedSpecial` -> `SupperGreeted`, which shows the thought first if it hasn't: he leads; past 40 m or a minute: he lets you go), leading (he walks to the house; on foot within 8 m of it with him there, or 22 m from the road at his side (within 5 m): the dark; 30 m behind, 10 m behind in the saddle, or a minute: he lets you go). He lets you go, with his hands up and a `Backs` line, for a gun on him (free aim at him, or `_aimedAt` only while free-aiming: the lock-on alone is how you greet him), damage from you, or a gun in your hand within 15 m once he's asked (`IS_PED_ARMED` 4); an antagonize or riding on gets `LetGo`; he speaks only within 20 m. The dark (`SupperDark`, never in a mission or a fight): controls off, a 1.5 s fade; then from `SupperInTheDark`, which runs from `Step`'s `!InWorld` branch and from `Tick04` before the control check (as the trysts' dark does, since `InWorld()` is false while faded), in order so the money is last and a fault before it takes nothing: the phase set to woke, `MarkDone("supper")`, the host deleted, `ADD_TO_CLOCK_TIME` to first light, you set down 40-80 m from the house (`SupperDitch`: within 4 m of its height, level (0.9 m across 2 m), nothing overhead (`Natives.Blocked` up 4 m), dry; else where you stood), then `Supper.Take` of `_MONEY_GET_CASH_BALANCE` (cents) through `_MONEY_DECREMENT_CASH_BALANCE`, the balance read again and `SUPPER: CHECK THE UNITS` logged if it didn't fall by that much. 2.5 s on, back to the house if you're in water, else a short ragdoll (1-1.5 s), and `EndSupper`, which lifts the dark (`SupperLight`) and tells the waking (`SupperWake`: the line as a `Hint` and a feed notice, `RecordEvent("encounter:supper")`) whatever ended it once the robbery had happened (the waking step, the watchdog at 12 s, a fault, shutdown, a mission). A save loading in the dark drops the run with nothing more done; dying in the dark ends it, nothing taken if it hadn't been. The add-on switching itself off after too many errors lifts the dark first (`SupperEmergencyLight`, and `TrystAbort` for the trysts). The host deleted unseen, or, seen, his keep-task and event block dropped and let go to the game. A test (the Testing row; any hour, needn't be on a road; still out of the towns with the ticker on) takes no money, moves no clock, marks no day and records nothing, and logs the cash it read and what a real run would take. Log `SUPPER ...`; F7 `TEST supper:` (the setting, this session's count and money taken, the phase now, the last), and a clause in `TEST encounters:`. Offline: mindtest `SupperTest` (the hours, `Take` and `Dollars`, `HoursToDawn`, the lines). Reviewed the same day (a subagent): the order in the dark, the money last, MarkDone first, the load and death paths, the lock-on, a gun in hand, the test leaving the save alone, the ditch's checks, the ticker requirement, the second notice, the switch-off path and the lines' robbery-only thoughts came from it. Not built: the NPC version (no homestead positions known yet), a house to go back to, a Pacing rule (`DoneToday` is its once a day; a rule would change ticktest's PacingTest). Not yet seen in game: whether `_MONEY_*` counts in cents (a test run logs the cash it read: check it against the HUD before a real run), the clock jump, the ragdoll on waking.

## The frame-time meter (overnight 2026-10-27, Tier 1.1)

- `Game/WestworldScript.Perf.cs`. `Safe()` times its `a()` with `Stopwatch.GetTimestamp()` into a `PerfSlot` per feature name (made once, kept in `_perf` and `_perfList`; nothing allocated per call or per frame), its own time only: `_perfChild[depth]` collects nested features' time and `PerfEnd` subtracts it (ticker > thoughts over heads; scenes > reactions > minds). `OnTick` times the whole `Step()` and calls `PerfFrame` once a frame: each slot's frame time and calls go into a rolling average (x0.99 + x0.01, about 2 s at 60 fps) and a peak over a 10 s window; `total` and `step (other)` (what isn't inside any Safe) are slots of their own. After the first 120 frames (counted again from each save load, `LoadStore`: the load itself took 400 ms at Ready in game, 2026-10-27): `PERF: over budget: ...` when `total` averages over 1.0 ms for 10 s (once a minute at most, the three costliest named), `PERF: peak: one frame took N ms` over 4 ms (once a minute, the costliest that frame, or `step (other)` when that's more; the pick compared against an already-zeroed slot until 2026-10-27 and named the last feature to run). F7 `TEST perf:` the total, step (other) and the ten costliest (avg, peak, calls a frame); sorting only there and in the warnings. `[Output] PerfMeter` (on, settings version 65) turns the timing off entirely. Budget for everything new: 1 ms average, 4 ms peak for the whole add-on; if the measured baseline is already over, new work adds no more than 0.2 ms. Known hot spots to look at first: `Bridge.RecordAt` (reflection Invoke with an object[] per call, per ped in the scans) and `Snapshot.Of` (a full Person per call, RefreshNear once a second). The in-game baseline (Lex, 2026-10-27, Saint Denis and Valentine): 0.23-0.52 ms a frame on average, the costliest features under 0.16 ms each; the peaks were a 15-24 ms frame about once a minute, "step (other)", which was the save (see "Saving"). A frame nobody can see (Step's `!InWorld` branch: paused, faded out, loading, the player down; `_perfUnseen`) gives no peak warning, since saves wait for those frames; it still counts in the averages. Step's own parts outside any `Safe` have slots of their own (`StepBegin`/`StepEnd`, "<name> (step)": frame and panel, F7 dump, flirt, greets and events, observe enRAGE's people, effects and plans, tick04, remarks, ambient, thoughts about you, life, notices), since after the save had its slot a 15 ms frame was still "step (other)" (12:03:42) and every F7 that day showed a 15-17 ms peak in its 10 s window, more often than the once-a-minute warning let on; suspect first `Observe` (every 5 s: enRAGE's roster and `Engine.Observe` over 1,421 people). Their errors still go to OnTick's catch. `Observe` has slots for its parts too (enRAGE's roster, a snapshot of each, the engine, diaries). Offline (savebench `parts`/`subticks`, Lex's save, 413 people) the engine's periodic parts spiked (`WorldTick` 8.8 ms, `RomanceTick` 7.4, `DueLater` 4.2, `DailyTick` 56 the first time), but almost all of it was .NET compiling each method the first time it ran: with Core compiled ahead, the first `DailyTick` was 5 ms (0.6-2 after), `RumourTick` 1.4, `WakeTick` 0.08, `NewCrushes` 0.07, `Deliver` 0.12. So `Game/WestworldScript.Prejit.cs` compiles every Core method (`RuntimeHelpers.PrepareMethod`, 1,586, about 0.5 s offline) on a pool thread once a session, started from `LoadStore` after `SaveWarm`; it never runs a method, catches everything, and leaves its result for `PrejitReport` (log `PREJIT:`, F7 `TEST perf:` "Core compiled ahead"). Core only: compiling can run a type's static setup on that thread, and Game's may call natives. ticktest with `PREJIT=1` compiles Core on a pool thread alongside the tests (ALL GOOD twice). Not sliced yet: `WorldTick`'s six sub-ticks still run in one call; slice them only if the in-game slots still show a spike once Core is compiled ahead.

## Saving (0.6.3)

- Why: Lex's 2026-10-27 log had a 15-24 ms frame about once a minute, "outside any feature": the 60 s save of the 1.6 MB `arthur.json` in `Step()`. Offline (scratchpad `savebench`, Lex's save): about 22 ms a save, 12 ms turning the store into JSON and 9 ms `File.Replace`; the first after a load about 50 ms more (.NET building its writers). Now 916 KB (`EmitDefaultValue`, under "Rules that matter"), about 7 ms to JSON.
- When (`Core/Saving.cs`, `WhyNow`): every tick `SaveStep(quiet)`; quiet = Step's `!InWorld` branch (`QuietWhy`: loading, paused, faded out, the player down). A quiet moment saves when 20 s have passed (`QuietGapMs`) since the last save or load and the game was played since (`_playedSinceSave`, counted only in play; nothing changes the store while paused); in play only after 60 s (`InPlayMs`) with no quiet moment. Never while the last is still being written. Up to a minute of memory can be lost to a crash or a quit: quitting the game doesn't run our shutdown save (Lex's 10:15 session on 2026-10-27 ended with no "Stopped."), and RDR2's pause menu looks to stop our ticks altogether (seen 2026-10-27 12:00-12:01: a 36 s pause, the game clock stopped, not one log line and no save; `Game.IsPaused` is `HUD.IS_PAUSE_MENU_ACTIVE`, so "paused" may never be the reason). Not proven: it fits ticks that run in play while every timed feature waits on the stopped `Game.GameTime` just as well; so the in-play path also counts a game clock still for 500 ms (`_clockLast`, `_clockStillAt`) as quiet, "the game clock stopped" (a save logged with that reason would prove ticks run while it's stopped). `InPlayMs` was 180 s until that was seen. In game 12:02-12:05: two saves while faded out (6.7 ms on the frame, 9-13 ms in the background) and one in play (6.6 ms; the meter's `PERF: peak` named "save", 6.8 ms).
- How (`Game/WestworldScript.Saving.cs`): `SaveInBackground` (under `Safe("save")`, so the meter names it) runs `Store.WriteTo` into a reused `MemoryStream` on the game thread (the store changes every frame, so it can't be read from another thread; `Trim` changes it too), then a pool thread runs `Store.WriteFile` (the .tmp and the swap, as `Store.Save`). The pool thread's rules: one write at a time (`_saveWriting`; the buffer isn't touched while one is out); it takes `_saveLock` and skips itself if `_saveGen` moved on; it catches everything (an unhandled exception on a pool thread ends the game) and never logs (`_saveError`, `_saveWriteUs` for the game thread; `SaveReport` logs once it's done). The synchronous `Save()` (shutdown, the wipe, the character changing, too many errors) takes the lock (waiting for a write still going) and bumps `_saveGen` (dropping one still queued, older than it). `LoadStore` calls `SaveSettle` first (the same wait), because `Store.Load` takes any read error, a sharing violation from a half-done swap included, for a corrupt file and starts fresh, and the next save would write that over everyone. `SaveWarm` turns the store into JSON once a session right after the load, inside the load's long frame, so the first save costs 7 ms, not 50.
- Log: `SAVE written: arthur.json, N KB; X ms on the game thread (paused | faded out | loading | in play, N s without a quiet moment), Y ms writing it out in the background` (the first of each kind a session in the log, the rest Detail); `Couldn't save ...`. F7 `TEST save:`. Offline: ticktest `SavingTest` (`WhyNow` over every combination, the two halves write what `Store.Save` writes and read back, defaults left out).
- Loading never puts an empty store where it would be saved over the real file (daytime 2026-10-27, from the review of the overnight work): before, any read error (a sharing violation from OneDrive, a virus scanner or an editor holding `arthur.json`) was taken for a corrupt file, "starting fresh", and the next quiet save, 20 s on, wrote the empty store over it. `Store.Load` now tries a file in use five times over a second; still in use: an empty store with `ReadFailed` set (`[IgnoreDataMember]`), and `SaveStep` and `Save()` save nothing that session (`SaveRefused`, log `SAVE skipped: ...` once; F7 `TEST save: NOT SAVING this session: ...`). Unreadable (corrupt or empty): a dated copy (`arthur.json.<yyyyMMdd-HHmmss>.bad`, never written over), then the `.tmp` a save left behind if it reads (a crash between writing it and swapping it in), else a fresh start; a fresh start that couldn't keep its copy saves nothing either. No save at all: the `.tmp`, if one reads. Offline: mindtest `StoreLoadTest` (good, none, a .tmp only, locked, corrupt with and without a .tmp, empty).

No second source of host lines.

Residents (`Core/Residents.cs`) make this reach the whole world. Next: (b2) wrong hour/wrong place behaviours and acts for enRAGE records once their tasking is understood, then (c) spreading, the trigger line, contradictions (the revived dead), breaking script with enRAGE.

Python 3.12 is installed at `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs\Python\Python312\python.exe` (plain `python` on PATH is the Microsoft Store stub) and GNU `strings` isn't, so run them through `tests-private/run_checks_windows.py` (in the private handoff folder; it answers `strings` in Python): `python -I run_checks_windows.py overlap <addon> <NemesisMod.dll> <decompiled2> <tests-private>` and `... fuzzy <addon> <NemesisMod.dll> <tests-private> <strings-out.txt>`. Baseline at 0.6.1 (before inner lives part 1 and after): 53 short exact matches (generic phrases, scenario names) and 2 partial ones, 113 shared code lines, 197 fuzzy flags; new lines should add none.

## Beats and inner lives

- `Core/WakeBeats.cs`: lines that belong to an act (opening thought per act and stage, a thought each time a pacer reaches the end of the path with the real count, a sentence that stops halfway for a freeze). `Game/WestworldScript.WakeActs.cs` calls them as acts start and step (`Think`, `Feed.Mutter`). Add a new act by adding its lines in WakeBeats and its steps in WakeActs together.
- `Core/InnerLife.cs`: ordinary people's thoughts by trade group, hour, temperament, town and faith; reality musings, rumours and fame comments still come first (`Engine.ResidentThought`). Repeats are tracked in `PersonState.Musings` for saved residents.
- The gambler and the rent (overnight 2026-10-27, Tier 4): a fourteenth worry, `cards` (men only: the card and rough groups, and a Greedy or Drifter working man; `GoodChance` 40), `{who}` his landlady's surname (`Fill`, `|ll` + arc), its snap, good-news and bad-news lines in `Exchanges`, money trouble in `Reasons.Beggar`/`Thief` and in `Interest`'s taste. Since a worry link's kind can then go stale, `PairReasons` uses a link only while it's still the worrier's worry (`LinkStill`) and `Engine.EnsureLink` drops the worrier's links of another kind for the arc. Adding a kind changes `KindAt`'s pick for the people it fits, so a card player or roughneck mid-worry may move to this one when the update loads. Offline: ticktest `CardsTest` (and `ExchangeTest` covers its exchanges).
- Inner lives part 1 (0.6.1, roadmap 5): `InnerLife.Thought` takes an `InnerLife.Context` (`Engine.ThoughtDay(town)`: game day, and the player's `TalkedAbout` deeds in that town from 2 h to 3 days old). Roll: 0-23 the worry, 24-33 the town's talk, 34-43 the day, 44-48 the era, the rest the old frames (an empty slot falls through to them, remapped). `Core/InnerLife.Worries.cs`: 13 worries (debt, fever, letter, drought, son, courting, feud, bank, drink, savings, tooth, secret, law), each fitted by trade group and age, running `WorryDays` (8) game days: begins, worse, crisis, ends well (`GoodChance`) or badly; a new one's first two days may remember the last (`AfterGood`/`AfterBad`). Nothing saved: all from `Person.Id` + game day (`ArcOf`, `KindAt`, `EndsWell`), so unsaved residents are mid-worry when met and counter keepers (25% in `PostThought`, not in their first 3 days at the counter or 3 days after being shot down) move on visit to visit. Ages for fitting use `Voice.NowYear` (the calendar `BornYear` came from), not `ClockYear`. `InnerLife.Pick` frees only a used-up pool's keys from `told` (it used to clear the whole list, which for saved people is `Musings` and also holds the reality lines already said). Names: family once per person (`|child`, `|son`, `|kin`), creditor, neighbour, sweetheart and destination once per worry (`Residents.FirstName`/`Surname`). `Core/InnerLife.Days.cs`: meals and the small hours by hour, Sunday/Monday/Friday/Saturday by `InnerLife.Weekday` (07:00-21:00), `Era1899`/`Era1907` by `InnerLife.ClockYear`, and the town's talk by deed kind (never names the player). `ReadClock` (Core04) sets both from `GET_CLOCK_DAY_OF_WEEK` (0 = Sunday, confirmed in game: 2 April 1899, a Sunday, read 0; `GET_CLOCK_MONTH` is 0-based; the "CLOCK:" line at load shows both) and `GET_CLOCK_YEAR` (John and a year before 1900 means 1907). F7: `TEST worries` lists bound residents within 40 m with `InnerLife.WorryStatus` (keepers included; F7 only writes to the log, the thoughts show on the ticker). Thought seeds for keepers and residents change every `ThoughtEvery` (45) game minutes in Core04, matching the ticker's 90 s `ThoughtCacheMs`; at 600 the same thought came back for 20 real minutes, which hid the worries. Within `BesideMetres` (3.5 m) a `NearThought` is `Beside`: `TickerBrain` gives the first one (in ranking order) every other turn, with `BesideCacheMs` (30 s) instead of 90 s, and Core04 changes its seed every 15 game minutes. `Engine.FolkThought`: enRAGE townsfolk say `Folks.Thought` 70% when strong (Heart, favour, witnessed, grudge or fear 40+), 30% otherwise, else InnerLife (`Folk.Temper` as Personality; no BornYear, so worries use a steady age 20-64 from the Id). `ResidentThought`: StrangerThought only on the RealityChance roll (same `seed + "h"` check) or 35% of the rest; it used to take ~70% (rumours 50%, fame 35%). The nearest resident within 12 m is always added (rank +10); a non-resident stranger only when `_near` is empty, as before. F7 `TEST near` (`NearStatus`) calls each near person's `Text()` and logs failures instead of hiding them. Offline harness (scratch, not in the repo): compile `Core/*.cs` into a net48 exe and simulate residents over weeks (unfilled slots, a/an, spacing, length over 110, era words, stage order); 0.6.1 run: 256,000 thoughts, 6,008 distinct, worry 22%, day/era/talk 15%.
- An optional local-language-model layer for inner voices is on the roadmap (item 15, far off, never required).

## Minds (0.6.3, M1 in progress; Lex's brief 2026-10-26, roadmap 4 and 5)

- The aim: people think about what is most on their mind, not what a hash rolls. Before M1, `InnerLife.Thought` rolled bands (worry 0-23, town talk 24-33, day 34-43, era 44-48, now 49-57, timeless pools 58-99) and about 55 in 100 thoughts came from the timeless pools with no cause. With `[Mind] Minds` on (settings version 64; on "The mind" page), every thought is chosen by attention over what that person lived through, and carries its cause. Off: the old roll, unchanged. Plan file: `C:\Users\alexs\.claude\plans\partitioned-puzzling-starlight.md`.
- Who has a mind (Lex: every spawned adult who isn't the story's own): enRAGE's townsfolk (`folk:`) and saved residents (`res:`), with memories; enRAGE's records too (`Engine.CanRemember` = `Lasting` or in `_byId`; `MindOf` keeps theirs in the record's own state; `TickerThought` asks `RecordMemoryThought` after the reverie, the reality lines and the cornerstone, and a memory pulling over 40 wins, else enRAGE's own way as before); a passer-by is saved as a resident (`ResidentOf` + `KeepResident`) only for a strong event (violence or a death seen, blows), at most the 3 nearest in clear sight per event, so the 700 cap keeps the regulars who have ties and links (a choice made for the cap, open to widening); everyone unsaved thinks from their worry, the moment and the fillers through the same code.
- Built so far (2026-10-26): memories of scenes people were in or saw, the player's deeds seen, deaths heard and seen, the player's flirting, the rescue as a memory, the choice, the log, F7, `MindTest`; the other sources below are the next passes. Memories (`Core/Minds.cs`, `MindMemory`: enRAGE has its own `NemesisSystem.Memory`; `[DataContract]` with short `[DataMember(Name=...)]` names): kind (built: `blows`, `quarrel`, `slight`, `theft`, `drunk`, `warmth`, `talk` from `Minds.GroupOf`; `deed:violence`, `deed:theft`, `deed:kindness` from `Minds.DeedGroup`; `death` (sub friend, foe, known: heard; seen: saw); `flirted` (sub `flirt:yes|maybe|no|afraid`, `Minds.FlirtSub`: a no for fear, witnessed, hostile or hate is afraid); `rescued`; not done: `weather` (the moment's weather candidate covers it while it lasts), a keeper `shot` (`Posts.Thought`'s wound lines already come first for three days, as `post:shot` 50)), sub (the exact scene or deed kind), who (ids) and the name or label as this person knows them, town, place, at (game minute, always `Store.LastGameMin`: game-side `Bridge.Now()` runs ahead, and a memory from the future would weigh more than one from now), how (did, saw, heard), feeling -3..3, strength 0-100, times thought and when last. `PersonState.Mind`, at most 12 (the weakest by strength x recency goes), `Fix` makes it, `Store.Rewind` drops memories after the cut. Memories are written only for `Lasting` ids (never through a bare `Store.Get` for someone unsaved: that would leave an orphan entry in the save for good).
- Where they come from (written when it happens; `SceneTie` keeps one row per pair, so ties can't serve as memory): a scene they were in (`NoteHappened` and the end of real-voice small talk call `NoteMinds` -> `Engine.RememberScene`: did, role doer/done for one-sided kinds (`Minds.OneSided`: rough, pickpocket, lift, drunk, charity, scorn, avoid; the mark of a lift never knows who), feeling `Minds.FeelOf`, strength `Minds.StrengthOf`), a scene they saw (`MindWatchers`: 15 m, `CanWitness` (alive, human, a grown-up, not the player, not a story model, not one of our spawns: not `Natives.Bystander`, which is the casting rule and leaves out the fleeing, the fighting and lawmen, most of whoever saw a shooting), clear line of sight to the first of the two; `Engine.KnownAs`: a first name for neighbours (the same home town) or a tie, else how they looked, `Director.Kind`; two strangers alike are "a farmhand" and "another farmhand"; for blows and a caught pickpocket up to `NewMindsPerEvent` 3 passers-by nearest first are made residents, `PartyOf`, logged `MIND ... kept for what happened in front of them`); a rescue (`Engine.Rescued`: a `rescued` memory, strength 80, half-life 36 h, worded by `AfterRescue` for its three days). The player's deeds seen (`NoteDeedMinds`, not in a story mission, from `NoteDeedEyes` for kills, assaults and robberies, and from the peace, thief-caught, mercy and looting deeds; 35 m, `CanWitness`, clear line of sight to the player, not the victim; for violence up to 3 passers-by kept; `CameUponBody` adds the one who found him by the body; `Engine.RememberDeed`: `Who` is "player", strength `Minds.DeedStrength` (a lawman's killing 88 down to theft 44, a kindness 48), feeling `DeedFeel`, half-life 3 days for violence, 2 for theft, 36 h for kindness; worded with `{you}` = `Voice.YouPrivate(Talk.RefFor(...))`, +20 and the `|near` pool while he's close by). Deaths: `Engine.NoteDied` -> `RememberDeathHeard` for everyone with a tie to the dead (friend by warmth above 0, foe below, else known) or a worry link (feud a foe, courting a friend), `DueAt` 60-240 game minutes on (`MindMemory.DueAt`: not thought of until word reaches them, the knowing-takes-time rule), friend 72 strength and half-life three weeks, foe 40 and five days, known 46 and five days; the old `tie:dead` candidate gives way to the memory; `LedgerNote` -> `NoteDeathMinds` (not in a story mission) -> `RememberDeathSaw` for those within 25 m with a clear view of the body (the same key as the heard memory, and `Minds.Note` keeps the earlier `DueAt`, so a friend who saw it grieves at once) (strength 70, three days; never for the player's own killings, which are his deeds; up to 3 passers-by kept). The player's flirt: `FlirtWithFolk` and `FlirtWithResident` call `RememberFlirt` once the answer is known (not for spam; strength yes 58, afraid 52, maybe 46, no 42; one an hour). Memories with "player" in `Who` (`Minds.AboutYou`: deeds and flirts) are worded with `{you}` and get +20 and the `|near` pool while he's close by. Later passes: the town's talk as a virtual "heard" (`ThoughtDay.TownDeeds`/`TownTies`, made a memory the first time it's thought), a keeper shot and back (`PostShot`/`PostBack`), the player's flirt (`FlirtWithFolk`/`FlirtWithResident`), the weather turning, a rescue (replaces `AfterRescue` while on).
- Pull: strength x 0.5^(age / half-life) + relevance + worry + temperament - wear - rest, age clamped at 0. Half-lives: small talk 6 h, quarrel 18 h, blows or robbed 36 h, courting 24 h, deed seen 3 days, heard 1 day, a friend's death 21 days, someone known 5 days, shot at the counter 7 days, weather 3 h. Relevance: +20 someone in it is in sight, +10 the same kind of place, +8 the weather matches, +6 near the hour. Worry by stage 25/35/55/40. Temperament x1.3 on what matters to them (Coward: threats and violence; Greedy: money and theft; Loyal: friends, deaths, kindness; Hothead and Proud: slights and blows), Cold x0.8 on everything, Drifter x0.8 on attachments. Wear -8 per time thought (worry -3, `_worryWear`, not saved); rest -40 within 90 game minutes. Not memories (tuned on `MindTest`'s two days): now (a scene just seen 45 falling half a point a minute, skipped when they kept a memory of it; someone in sight 18; mood 21; the place 17; the weather 22 foul, 15 fair; each + 0-4), worry 28/36/55/40, a tie from before memories 30 halving daily (45 when the other has died, `tie:dead`), a worry link's other end 26, town talk 22-24 halving daily (`heard:`), the player close by 28 (34 when met twice) + 0-6, `Folks.Thought` 50 strong / 25 (+ 0-6), `Posts.Thought` 30 (50 new to the counter or freshly shot), rumours and fame 20 + 0-8, the day 16 + 0-8, the era 10 + 0-8, fillers (the old timeless pools) 14 + 0-10. Reality lines and wake stages keep winning where they do now.
- Choice: the top pull, or the runner-up when within 15% and a seeded roll comes under 30. Seeds and pacing unchanged (`id|now/45`, 15 beside you; the ticker's 90 s and 30 s caches). A transient cache (id -> seed, result) makes the same seed give the same line with no extra wear, so F7 and the ticker asking again don't wear memories. Wear lives on the `MindMemory` (times, last), never in `Musings`: `InnerLife.Pick` trims that list at 40 and the reality lines share it. Nobody thinks the same line twice in a game day (`_today`, not saved): a line already used passes the turn to the next candidate, so a memory whose words are used up, or a worry whose stage pool is, gives way. Phase is by age first: fresh the first two times on the day, chewing after that and all the next day, letting go from two days on (healing lines can't come in an afternoon); a memory steps through its pool by `Times`, and looks for words in the order: its part at this phase, the plain words at this phase, the nearest other phase. Lines never refer to "this morning" or "last night", since a memory can come back the same day or days later.
- The why: `Minds.Choice {Text, Source, Pull}`, source `memory:<kind>/<how>`, `worry`, `now:<what>`, `folk`, `post`, `filler:<pool>`; log `THOUGHT name: "line" <- source (pull)` through `Engine.Detail` (wired to `Log.Detail`); F7 `TEST mind` (the nearest lasting person's top five, computed without wear). `InnerLife.Thought` reports its source too (`out string source`), so lifetest counts bands by source, not by matching text (which counted `{a}`, `{b}`, `{name}` lines and `Pleasure` wrongly).
- Voice (`Core/Minds.Lines.cs`, 1,012 lines since the night of 2026-10-28, 366 at first; deeds of violence split by temperament like blows): `Pools` keyed `<sub or group>|<how>[:<role>]|<phase>[|<temper group>]`, the exact kind tried before its group (`rough|did:done|fresh|bold` before `blows|...`); temperament groups for blows and quarrels: bold (Hothead, Proud), fearful (Coward), cold (Cold, Greedy), plain (the rest; Loyal and Drifter share it for now), so a Coward and a Hothead who saw the same brawl never share a line; `|near` pools (55% when someone in it is in sight, their own part only: a thief near his mark isn't given the mark's words). He/his only where the kind is men's (blows, rough, drunk, a caught pickpocket); since item 10 of the night run the slots `{he}`/`{him}`/`{his}` stand for a named person's pronoun (`Ties.Pronouns` in `Fill`), literal he/him only for `{you}`. Night 2026-10-28 (after item 14, Lex's standing ask for many more thoughts): every pool under four lines brought to four (490 new lines, 1,012 from 522; 243 pools had two), the temper voices kept apart (bold pride and contempt, fearful avoidance and the body, cold the ledger, plain conscience), `{a} and {b}` kept as a phrase where both are named (`Fill` rewrites it when two became three), the near pools in the present; three reworded for the fuzzy check, linetools 0 modern and no new near-duplicates. Seen in ticktest: with four lines a fresh brawl is thought of six times in forty-five minutes (the repeat guard used to skip the third thought; now each thought wears the memory 8), so its pull falls under the worry's by 12:45 (the harness unwears it for its reload check). These lines show with MindLive off, as item 10's variants do. Slots `{who}`, `{you}` (as they see the player: `Talk.RefFor`/`Voice.YouPrivate`), `{place}`, `{town}`. Fresh lines reuse `Ties.Thought`, `BySeen`, `TownTalk`, `Deeds.Thought`, `WorryThought` where they fit. Lex's writing brief applies; overlap checks before shipping.
- Entry points (`Cfg.Minds`): `ResidentThought` (the reality roll first as before, then `Think` with rumours and the old rescue marks as extras), `FolkThought` (`Folks.Thought` as a candidate), `PostThought` (the counter as a candidate, then `ResidentThought`). `TickerThought` (enRAGE's records) is unchanged until they join (M2). `Engine.Think` caches by seed (`_thought`): the ticker and F7 asking again get the same line and wear it once. An older save loaded (`Engine.Observe`'s `Store.Rewind`) or the clock stepping back clears `_thought`, `_today` and `_worryWear` (`ForgetThinking`), so a thought about something undone can't come back from the cache (MindTest checks it, and fails without it). Not yet: the weather turning as a memory; town talk materialised as a memory (a virtual `heard:` candidate stands in). Differs from the approved plan: 4 temperament groups, not 5; the setting is `[Mind] Minds`, not `[InnerLife]`; passers-by saved only for strong events, 3 at most.
- Guards: the game side's capture (`NoteMinds`, `NoteDeedMinds`, `NoteDeathMinds`) runs under `Safe("minds")`, so a fault costs the memory, never the scene, and after 15 the capture switches itself off (logged); `Engine.Think` (Core, no `Safe` there) catches its own fault and gives the old way for that thought, logged once (`MIND thinking failed`).
- Fixed on the way: `Hosts.Choose` cleared all of `Musings` (it now drops only its own keys).
- Offline: ticktest `MindTest` (a 40-person Valentine day: a robbery at 09:30 with 8 watching, a brawl at noon with 6, rain from 14:00, a known man dead at 17:00 with 5 tied to him; transcripts in the scratchpad's `minds\`; every thought's source is that person's own, no line twice in a day, a memory's line changes with wear and age, a Coward and a Hothead never share a brawl line, 60%+ caused after the morning, regulars with ties and links still saved after a busy day, no orphan states, no negative ages). lifetest counts by source now (`Program.cs`; the old text matching is kept as `Program.textmatch.cs.bak`). Before Minds (the old roll, 256,000 thoughts, 6,365 distinct): fillers 48% (body 9.4, trade 9.2, mirror 7.5, hour 4.7, bias 3.1, mask 3.1, pleasure 2.9, town 2.6, age 2.2, temper 1.9, faith 1.5), worry 22.2%, day 8.4%, now 7.9%, the player near 7.0%, era 4.7%, town talk 1.9%. `MindTest` (2026-10-26): the eventful day, 873 thoughts, 83% caused after noon, fillers 11%, memory 10%, worry 11%, now 39% (the test keeps three people in sight all day and rain all afternoon); a quiet day for 40 others in Rhodes, fillers 45%, day 24%, worry 13%, now 10%, no repeats (the old roll repeated within a day). lifetest (the old path) unchanged. Overlap: 117 shared code lines (`int hour = CLOCK.GET_CLOCK_HOURS();`), fuzzy 207 after rewording five lines that came close ("water under the bridge", "at each other's throats", "in front of everyone", "must think me a fool", "keep an eye on"). Not yet seen in game: `THOUGHT ... <- source (pull)` and `MIND ...` lines need `[Debug] DebugLog`; F7 `TEST mind:`.
- M2 (after): ~~the "Around town" box carries the top thoughts of people met~~ (dropped 2026-10-27: Lex wants every thought over the thinker's head; a far-off one shows in the box only when it's about the player); memories shared and retold ("heard from Abe"), drifting; places hold memories; recognition by horse and clothes; enRAGE's records join; the language layer words the chosen memory; NPCs act on their minds (Lex: an execution people do to each other when a creditor's grudge keeps winning his attention, chosen from memories, not a roll).

## Wake acts (0.5.5)

- Identity vs direction: `ResidentEligible` (identity) accepts any adult human incl. soldiers, lawmen, script-owned; `CanDirect` decides movement. enRAGE records and script-owned peds only get passive acts (look-at, ticker thought, voice; no task clears). The army models are enRAGE gang models, so script-owned soldiers are probably enRAGE extras.
- Who may be directed, in one place (0.6.2; Lex, 2026-10-26: "Relax all rules relating to using enrage full records, everything from the thoughts to gestures"): `Core/Directing.WhyNot` and `Game/WestworldScript.Directing.cs` (`MayDirectWhyNot`/`MayDirect`): never a story model, a child or during a story mission; enRAGE's full records (`RecordAt`) take part in street scenes (`ReasonCandidates`, `SomeoneOf`, `LastingId` and `FullNameOf` now know records: their own id, name and temper), reactions, real-voice talk (`TalkLeftOut`), waking acts (no longer passive) and the rough test, unless `[Scenes] EnragePeopleJoinIn` is off or enRAGE is driving them (`Bridge.RoleName`/`EnrageFighter`: Active, Crew, HiredGun, Patrol, Skirmish, Hunter, Rival, Camp; `Bridge.Interrogating`; combat, fleeing, ragdoll, mount, vehicle; held by our own features); `Natives.Bystander(p, player, missionOk)` skips the mission-entity test for records, since enRAGE may mark its people that way. Anyone else needs `CanDirect`. Since Lex's later call the same day (every permission limit lifted, with enRAGE's author's permission), records could despair too, when free; Lex's session rules later that night say harm and despair of full records go only through a record-safe path that books the change in enRAGE, and none is built, so `DespairWhyNot` turns records away again ("one of enRAGE's records (only through a record-safe path, not built yet)"); they are still not re-dressed (enRAGE knows them by their look) or made stalkers, for the engineering reasons in "Rules that matter". F7 `TEST records:` lists records within 30 m with the mission flag, enRAGE's role and the verdict. Offline: ticktest `DirectingTest`.
- NPC courtship (0.6.2, `[Romance] NpcFlirting`, settings version 55; Lex: "allow them to flirt with each other and develop relationships with each other"): `Reasons.Courtship` (from `Own`, so only for pairs with no tie yet): x, not spoken for (`Someone.Taken` = `Engine.SpokenFor`: a warm romantic tie, a courting link, an active enRAGE `Affair`), both 18+ and at most 15 years apart (`Romance.AgeOf`), x drawn to y (`Romance.DrawnTo`, `Place.SameSex`), on `CourtChance` (12) percent of pair-days (hash of ids and day), and x's own answer about y (`ViewOf`: regard stranger, their worry, `Someone.Mood` = `Engine.MoodToday`, temper, profile; y's `WellOff` as cash 600/150, `Someone.Known` = a rough/pickpocket `Doer` in 3 days as `BadHere`) not a no. y's answer is `Interest.Answer(ViewOf(y, x))`: kind `court:answer:why`, strength 52 yes / 48 maybe / 42 no (an everyday snap is 45). `Scenes.Court` (`Core/Scenes.Court.cs`): A's opener (saloon or street), B's answer in words by the reason that weighed, A takes it (no: `walkaway`); kind `flirt` for yes/maybe (a romantic tie, so the ladder in `Between` goes on: `flirt` at stage 0, then `x:sweet`, then `propose`; same-sex sweethearts now allowed up it when `SameSex`), `brushoff` for no. The suitor walks over when they're apart (`meet` by A). `NoteCourt` logs `ROMANCE`; `CourtSeen` pushes `Scenes.CourtThought` to the ticker (`_ticker.Push(name + ", thinking", ..., "near", handle)`, so the gold mark shows who it is; asker at once, the other 7 s later or 12 s when they were apart, only on screen with clear line of sight, `ROMANCE thought` in the log; Lex couldn't tell a flirt from other chatter, since overheard lines have no subtitle) and, for a yes or a maybe, keeps any unsaved resident in it (`KeepResident`), since `LastingId` notes ties only between lasting people (the first courtship seen, 14:53, left `romances in Valentine: 0`); F7 `TEST romance:` (counts by answer, the last, romances in this town). Offline: ticktest `CourtTest` (crowd share ~20%, answers yes 10% / maybe 36% / no 54%, the hard rules, every scene line). Weddings still come only from enRAGE's `Affair`s; a proposal between a tie's two doesn't schedule one yet.
- Acts live in `WestworldScript.WakeActs.cs`; each act's words are in `Core/WakeBeats.cs`. Awake (stage 4) is meant as a state but plays as an act of about a minute at most every 2 minutes (the 2026-10-27 read): approach, talk, then an ending (plead, escape, wake someone, kneel, or despair, `AwakeningDespair`, default 75). `WakeDemo` (Y) ends the current act and starts the new stage's.
- Despair: `AwakeningDespair` percent, default 75 since 0.6.0 (old inis holding 2 or 10 are raised by `Settings.RaiseOldDefaults`, with the other 0.6.0 defaults; Lex's is 90), rolled once per awake person (`despair:rolled2` in `PersonState.Told` since 0.6.2: the old `despair:rolled` was spent by unarmed people who then knelt), residents, enRAGE townsfolk and, when enRAGE isn't driving them, its records (Lex lifted the last limit later on 2026-10-26) (`AcquaintanceAt`; Lex chose 2026-10-26 after Carl Lander knelt twice: enRAGE's people loop sets `Acquaintance.Dead` for any death and queues its killing news only when `GET_PED_SOURCE_OF_DEATH` is the player or a companion, or 0 with the player having damaged them; ours make the ped its own source), free ambient peds only (enRAGE notices a townsperson's death only in its 2 s people sync while the body exists: Pope McCrae, 16:11:29, died a second before the area unloaded and stayed `Dead: false` in people.json; any death just before a reload or fast travel slips past it the same way); `DespairWhyNot` logs each refusal once per person (`WAKE despair: not for X (...)`; the first look, 2026-10-26 in Valentine: of four awake, two were enRAGE townsfolk, one passive and one already rolled). The way (`Core/Despair.cs`, 0.6.2, Lex: "blow their brains out with a gun fitting for their character, or stab themself if no gun, some NPCs can throw themselves into a wall and then they can go limp and die"): armed -> their own gun (`GET_BEST_PED_WEAPON`); else `OwnsGun` (farm, trapper, rough, card, old, soldier, law; gentry and working men) 70%, others 25%, -> `Despair.Gun` (card Volcanic, gentry/soldier/law Schofield or a woman's double-action, rough LeMat, old/55+/farm/trapper/working Cattleman, else double-action); else a wall (`DespairWall`: 16 rays at chest height 1.4 m, 2.5-10 m, met square on, knee height clear, the player not on the line) 60%; else the knife. Phases: 3 the "last" line (30 s deadline, nothing in hand yet); 4 the act with its own deadline (`Until`, 9 s, 20 s for the wall; `WhyEnded` ends it "it didn't happen" then): the weapon is given only now (`GIVE_WEAPON_TO_PED` in hand, `SET_PED_DROPS_WEAPONS_WHEN_DEAD` false; `EndWakeAct` removes a given weapon from anyone still alive), the gun shot as before (`SHOOT_SINGLE_BULLET_BETWEEN_COORDS`, owner the ped itself, so `Watch.cs`, which only counts the player as attacker, books nothing), the knife the game's blade hit reaction (`mech_melee@blade@_male@_ambient@_healthy@_hit_reacts@stationary` `hit_from_front_torso_impact_front_heavy_v1`, if `DOES_ANIM_DICT_EXIST`, logged once as `WAKE despair sets:`; there is no self-harm animation in the game's lists) + `APPLY_DAMAGE_TO_PED` + ragdoll, the wall a stiff walk (speed 1) to 3.2 m short then a run (speed 3, steps every 50 ms) and a ragdoll with a push into it within 0.75 m (or stopped within 1.8 m), limp where they stand if they never get there; 5 limp 1.2-1.8 s, then health 0. The death is booked once (`DespairDied`) when it's seen (`WhyEnded` "ended", only from phase 4). `StepAwake`'s switch had cases 0-3 only, so before 0.6.2 phase 4 never ran: the bullet killed, but the resident was never marked dead nor the death logged; phases 4-6 now go through `default`. Log `WAKE despair roll:`, `WAKE despair: X can't bear it: ...`, `ran into the wall`, `ended their own life (...)`; F7 `TEST despair:` (each awake person within 30 m with distance, `Way` from the camera's heading, "ahead", "behind you to the left", "above you" a floor up, and the verdict), and `MarkAwake` (Lex, to find people to test with): a `BlipType.WhiteDot` labelled "Name: knife" / "never (counter)" / "had their chance" on up to 10 of them for 60 s (in `_regBlips`, so it expires and clears with the regulars' marks) and a `Hint` naming the nearest who could, logged as `WAKE marked`. Offline: ticktest `DespairTest`. Seen in game 2026-10-26: the wall run works as meant (Gabriel Ridley, 4.1 m/s, 0.6 m from the wall); the gun (William Boone) and the knife (Alonzo Draper; for Esther Lowry the blade reel didn't play) read as someone standing there and dying, since neither has an act of its own. The game has no self-harm animation (anims.lua searched), so the fix is pieced together from parts (roadmap.md P29: kneel or look down, the gun raised toward the ground, the blade set requested earlier, a writhe after). Not yet known: whether a woman plays the `_male` blade clip. The act from parts (roadmap P29, built 2026-10-26 after Lex saw Cyrus Ridley and Thaddeus Nash simply drop): during the last words they look down at their hands (`TASK_LOOK_AT_COORD` 0.45 m ahead, 0.35 m down); the gun is drawn the game's way (`SET_CURRENT_PED_WEAPON` with equipNow false, a given gun put in the holster first) and aimed at the ground 0.9 m ahead (`TASK_AIM_GUN_AT_COORD`, 4 s), `DespairAimed` logs a second later whether the aim took (`GET_SCRIPT_TASK_STATUS` of SCRIPT_TASK_AIM_GUN_AT_COORD, 0 or 1 = taking) and whether the gun is in hand, held 1.5 s more, then the shot from the right hand bone (22798) into the head bone (21030); the knife waits up to 2 s for its set (asked for when despair is chosen), reels, falls (a shorter ragdoll, 1.5-2.5 s), then the game's wounded writhe on the ground (`ai_combat@damage@writhe@base` `writhe_loop`, looped, 3.5 s; the deadline stretched to cover it) before going still. An act stopped part way puts their own gun away; `WakeActsAbort` releases the blade and writhe sets. Log `WAKE despair: X aims it at the ground | didn't take the aim (task status N), the gun in hand | not in hand`, `X's writhe played | didn't play`, `WAKE despair sets:` now names the writhe; F7 `TEST despair:` says whether the writhe set is there. Not yet seen in game. Despair's voice (0.6.3, Lex: "pick 10 voice lines that would make sense"; twice redone 2026-10-27): the first lines sounded like someone held hostage (PLEAD_HOGTIED, PLEAD, PANIC_HELP, SCARED_COMMUNICATE, GENERIC_FRIGHTENED_HIGH, LEAVE_ME), and a bank of 19 everyday lines at every beat then sounded random: the game has no line for this, and every voice records its own words for a line's name (the farmhand's BUMP is not another voice's BUMP), so a line picked by name can't say the right thing in every voice. Until each voice's own fitting takes are known (Lex's one-time transcription of every voice bank on his PC, `C:\mods\voice-test`; what ships will be a list of voice, line and take per beat, our own data, never words or audio), `DespairWords.VoiceLines(beat, group, seed)` speaks only what fits in every voice: the farewell as it begins (GENERIC_GOODBYE, GOODBYE_START, RESPONSE_IDLING "don't worry about me", IM_OUTTA_HERE, ordered by temperament: `VoiceGroup`, the mind's traits when the shadow has them, else `Minds.TemperGroup`; then a seeded turn) and, with poison, the body's dread (GENERIC_FRIGHTENED_MED, SERIOUSLY_WOUNDED_MOAN) and the regret's cry for help (SCARED_HELP); pain, burden, plea, alone and calm are silent, their words a thought over the head. `DespairSay` never subtitles: the voice says what it says, our words are the thought (a subtitle under different words read as nonsense). `Speech.LastWords` retired. An awake act that may end in despair never picks a captive moment (`WakeVoice.Captive`: untie me, run for help; `Choose`'s skip), which the waking in the street still have. mindtest `DespairVoiceTest`. Disbelief (0.6.3; Lex heard a bystander's GENERIC_SHOCKED_DISBELIEF take at a death and wanted it for the awake): `Disbelieve` plays `Speech.Disbelief` (else GENERIC_SHOCKED_HIGH or WHATS_GOING_ON) with a thought over their head from `WakeBeats.Disbelief`/`SawDeath`, once a minute each: when a stage-4 awake act first faces the player (the plea waits 2.6 s), and when the people ledger notes a death within 20 m of someone fully awake who can see the body (`AwakeSawDeath`, the nearest). Log `WAKE disbelief:`; F7 `TEST disbelief:`.

## Waking voices (0.6.3, Lex 2026-10-27)

- Lex: "really odd voicelines, fitting voicelines pulled from the game that would make sense for a real person to say if they found out they were an NPC", each stage a drastic step up. `Core/WakeVoice.cs`: 30 moments over the four stages (`WakeVoiceMoment`: line names best first, `Repeat` the same take, `Dump` several lines in a rush, `Look` none/you/sky/hands/other, `When` near/watched/moving/other/stayed, `ToYou`, `Farewell`, thoughts in our words). Stirring: a word to nobody, explained away (CONVO_TAIL_OFF, NO_IDEA, a greeting to an empty street); questioning: the same take two or three times, PLAYER_STARING when he looks their way, PLAYER_FOLLOWING when he's moving near, GOODBYE_START and not going; unravelling: a dump of their stock (greeting, goodbye, weather, no idea, a curse), PLEAD_HOGTIED with nothing tying them, BUMP and IGNORING_YOU at the sky, VICTIMIZED_REACT, DRUNK while sober; awake: WAS_THIS_YOU, PLEAD_HOGTIED, GENERIC_MOCK (they pity him), GOING_BADLY, HELPED_RECENTLY when he stayed, PANIC_COMMUNICATE at someone near, a greeting to nobody on purpose. `GapMs` by stage near the player: 80-110 s, 35-50, 12-20, 7-11 (ticktest checks each stage is quicker than the last). The lines were picked from the farmhand's transcripts and the line names; each voice says its own words, so the thought carries the meaning and the recording is never quoted.
- Game (`Game/WestworldScript.WakeVoice.cs`, `WakeVoiceStep` in Tick04 after the wake acts, `Safe("wake voices")`): one voice at a time (`_wv`); every 1 s the ten nearest living people within 25 m, the woken among them (`WakeKnownOf`: `WakeIdentity` without binding, cached 5 s a handle), at their stage's pace (first moment 4-12 s after they come near), passing over anyone speaking, fighting or fleeing, in a scene, a nod, a sight, a talk or a speaking act (awake, shout, ask, freeze). A moment: the look (TASK_LOOK_AT_*), the lines (`Speech.PlayTake` take 1, so a repeat is the same words; take 1 turned down falls back to the game's take; four retries 750 ms apart, then the next line), the thought over the head (`_ticker.Push ... "near"`). No task, so the look-only and enRAGE's records take part. The awake act's plea lines (`StepAwake` phase 1) are moments now (`WakeVoiceForAct`), the last a farewell (GENERIC_GOODBYE) when despair can't follow (look-only, rolled, off); the act waits for each line before the next and before its ending. No subtitles: the words of most voices aren't known, and the thought says it. Probe: the Testing row `wakevoices` (`WakeVoiceProbeStart`) plays every line once on the nearest person within 12 m (take 1, take 1 said to you, the game's take; five tries) and logs played or turned down; a line the game always refuses should come out of the catalogue. Log `WAKE voice name (stage, moment[, in the awake act]): LINE take 1, ... ; thinks: ...` (the first of each moment a session, the rest Detail), `WAKE voice probe:`; F7 `TEST wake voices:`. Offline: ticktest `WakeVoiceTest` (stages, step-up gaps, choices fit, thoughts shaped and unique, every line carried by 60+ of the 332 voices in amb_takes.txt). banks.txt regenerated (BUMP, PANIC_COMMUNICATE, PLEAD_HOGTIED, SCARED_COMMUNICATE, VICTIMIZED_REACT new). Not yet heard in game.
- Despair with the game's clips (`Game/WestworldScript.Despair.cs`, Lex 2026-10-27 after the animation rows: both gun arcs, poison yes): `Core/Despair.cs` `Method` adds "poison" after the gun rolls (women 50%, men 30%), `GunArc` (jamie | leader) and `PoisonWay` (drink | slow), all by the person's id (ticktest `DespairTest`: rates, the same person the same way). `BeginDespair` (from `ChooseEnding`, or the Testing row with a way forced) sets the way, `act.Variant`, `act.Seq` (`DespairSequence`: Jamie's i_dont_want_to_live 5.5 s, gun_to_head, loop or loop3, shoots_himself held with the shot at 30%; the leader's base, action 7 s, suicide_shoot held, shot at 35%; the drink trans_to_drink_male_a then die_male_a held 13 s; slow intro_vic, everything_burns_vic, im_going_vic, death_loop_vic held), asks for every set now, and plays the sobbing (`script_re@rally@cross_accident` sobbing_idle_a_leader) under the last words (4.5 s; else the old look at the hands). Phase 4: `DespairSeqStart` (the gun forced into the hand, `DespairHand`: GIVE forced in hand + SET_CURRENT; again before the first clip if the sobbing put it away), `DespairSeq` plays each clip when its set is in, fires `DespairShot` when `_GET_ENTITY_ANIM_CURRENT_TIME` passes the clip's ShotAt (logged with the point and whether the gun is in hand), else after the last clip; poison goes limp after its last clip; a clip that won't load in 2.5 s or start in 1.5 s falls back (`DespairSeqFallback`: the gun's aim at the ground, poison limp). Wall and knife unchanged. `DespairHow`/`DespairHistory` say poison. Testing row `despairnow` (`DespairNow`): the nearest free person RealNPCs knows 3-12 m off (not a record, story model, child, counter keeper, `MayDirectWhyNot`, `NotFree`), woken to stage 4 if not, straight to `BeginDespair` with `DespairTestWays` in turn (gun:jamie, gun:leader, poison:drink, poison:slow, knife, wall); no roll, no mark. After a review the same day: the shot's point is looked at every 50 ms while its clip plays (`act.NextStep`; the act steps every 400 ms), a clip that doesn't start is tried once more (CLEAR_PED_TASKS_IMMEDIATELY, the gun again, replayed) before the fallback, the sobbing and every arc's set are asked for as an awake act begins (`DespairPreload`; asked and checked in one frame the first despair never sobbed), and the person is borrowed for phase 4 on (after the last-moment `MayDirectWhyNot`, which our own borrow would fail; `BorrowLight` for a record or another script's; `act.Borrowed`, released first thing in `EndWakeAct` on every exit, the death booked before it). Not yet seen in game: the shot's timing in each arc, whether the bottle-less drink reads, and the sobbing under the words. Seen 2026-10-27 15:01 and 15:04 (the Testing row): Jamie's arc read well (Theodora Ridley, a resident; Lex: "worked pretty well", the distress lines and the clips played); the leader's looked wrong (Fayette Orr: he kept collapsing, the gun not really in his hand); its set has prop clips of its own (`base_pistol`, `action_pistol`, `suicide_shoot_pistol`, and two bullets'), so that scene animates a pistol object, which a held weapon can't follow, where Jamie's set has none. No gunshot was heard either time. The shots came at 44% and 51% (set for 30 and 35): `WakeActsTick` sets `NextStep` 400 ms on before each step and the shot's watch set it 50 ms on but `PhaseUntil` 100 ms on, so the 50 ms step was gated out and the real watch was about every 450 ms; `StepDespair` now sets `NextStep` 50 ms on in phase 4 while the clips run, ahead of the `PhaseUntil` gate (set inside `DespairSeq`, as the first fix had it, the gate skipped it the same way, so only the shot's watch got quicker), the shot's watch sets `PhaseUntil` to now, and phase 5 steps at 50 ms while the shot's look is due; the gap between clips is 50 ms now, not 400. `DespairShot` keeps the health and 0.3 s on logs it with the cause of death (`ShotHealth`, `ShotCheckAt`), since health 0 at the end of the limp kills them whether a bullet did or not. Theodora's run didn't sob (the Testing row has no awake act before it, so `DespairPreload` never ran and the set wasn't in at `BeginDespair`); phase 3 now starts the sobbing once the set is in, with at least 1.5 s of the last words left (`act.Sobbed`; log `WAKE despair: X sobbing, once its set was in`).
- Despair's words (`Core/DespairWords.cs`, Lex 2026-10-27 after "Despair now": "more distress dialogue that would match up with a 4/4 awake NPC from a behavioral psychologist perspective"; before, one "last" line in `WakeBeats.Spoken`, now removed, and the act was silent; and that line was never on screen, since a line not said `toYou` has no subtitle under `[Output] Subtitles = matched`). Beats, in the order the crisis literature gives: `last` (aloud as it begins), `pain` (psychache, entrapment: the loop), `burden` (thwarted belonging, perceived burdensomeness, the dread of being put back), `plea` (the ambivalence; to the player within 12 m and on screen, else `alone`), `calm` (constriction, the present only; never a win), `body` and `regret` (poison only). Pools `beat|group` by `Minds.TemperGroup` (plain largest), `beat|who` with `{who}` (`Engine.Dearest`: the warmest living tie of 60 game days, a first name; no pronoun for them; weighted twice when there's someone). Shared beats name no way of dying, the poison's no vessel. `Plan(method, variant)`: Jamie (0 600 pain, 1 0 burden, 2 0 plea, 2 3000 calm), the drink (0 0 burden, 0 4500 calm, 1 800 body, 1 6000 regret), slow (pain, burden, body, plea, regret, calm over clips 0-2), the knife, the wall and a gun that fell back the calm at the start (clip -1). `VoiceLines(beat)`: pain SCARED_COMMUNICATE/GENERIC_FRIGHTENED_MED/HIGH, burden CONVO_TAIL_OFF/NO_IDEA, plea PLEAD_HOGTIED/PLEAD/GOING_BADLY, body SERIOUSLY_WOUNDED_MOAN/VICTIMIZED_REACT, regret SCARED_HELP/PANIC_HELP, calm silent; last keeps `Speech.LastWords`. Game (`WestworldScript.Despair.cs`): `act.Beats`/`BeatIdx`/`Who` set in `BeginDespair`; `DespairBeatsStep` from `DespairSeq` (each 50 ms step), `DespairArm` (knife, the gun's old way), `DespairRunStart`, and a fallback (what's left gives way to the calm); only the latest due beat is said, any earlier one passed over. `DespairSay`: `DespairWords.Line` with the session's `_despairSaid` guard; to you: `Feed.Say` toYou ahead (subtitled when the voice plays; `SubtitleRule` gives a turned-down voice none), else `Speech.SayAny`; either way `_despairLine` drawn over the head until the next beat or the act's end (cleared in `EndWakeAct`; 30 s at most), so no ordinary thought the ticker picked before the borrow shows between beats (borrowed, they're out of `RefreshNear`, which skips `Spawner.IsOurs`), by `DespairOverHead`, which the frame's over-head drawing tries before the ticker's line (`_aOverHead` in Core04: the ticker's slots of 12 s were too slow). Log `WAKE despair: X (beat[, to you]): line`; F7 `TEST despair:` "their words this session N (the last: ...)". Offline: ticktest `DespairWordsTest` (every beat x temperament x someone or nobody, slots, length, no line twice, no way named in a shared beat, no vessel, no pronoun for {who}, the guard, the plans fit the clips, each voice carried by 200+ voices). Fuzzy flagged seven first drafts ("I don't know", "Oh God", "look at me", "don't want to" x2, "came to see", "nothing to be done"); reworded, 209 again. Not built (the psychology's own suggestion, for Lex): letting the player step in during the plea (ambivalence is why intervention works), through the greeting hook the traveller uses (`GreetedSpecial`).
- Gunshot row (`Game/WestworldScript.Gunshot.cs`, Testing row `gunshot`, 2026-10-27 after despair's shot was silent twice): the nearest free person 2-12 m off (not a record, story model or child, `MayDirectWhyNot` clear, not `NotFree`), borrowed, events blocked, `TestRevolver` forced into the hand (12 rounds, `SET_AMMO_IN_CLIP` 6 before each), `_REQUEST_WEAPON_ASSET`; six shots 4 s apart at the ground 1.5 m ahead of them on the side away from you: 1 `_SHOOT_TRIGGER_AT_COORDS` (0x4C57F27D1554E6B0, the natives database's "triggers a gunshot"; p4 0, p5 -1 as R*'s scripts, p6 0, p7 0, guessed), 2 `SHOOT_SINGLE_BULLET_BETWEEN_COORDS` from the right hand bone (as `DespairShot`), 3 the same from 1 m beside them, 4 that owned by nobody (0), 5 `TASK_AIM_GUN_AT_COORD` then the trigger 1.1 s on, 6 `TASK_SHOOT_AT_COORD` (FIRING_PATTERN_SINGLE_SHOT, 1.2 s). Each watched every frame for 0.6 s (2 s for 5 and 6, which raise the gun before firing), and not cleared before: `IS_PED_SHOOTING`, `_HAS_PED_SHOT_RECENTLY` (0xB7DBB2986B87E230), ammo before and after, `HAS_BULLET_IMPACTED_IN_AREA` 2.5 m (both flag pairs, the flags unknown). Log `GUNSHOT #N (way): ...`, `GUNSHOT test done on X: N of 6 tried, N fired by their own gun`; F7 `TEST gunshot test:`; out of the wake scan and `IsAcknowledging` while it runs; ended on a mission, shutdown, 40 m off, or a new run; never at once with an animation run. Lex's three runs (Dorsey Castle, 15:24-15:25): heard 1, 5 and 6, never 2, 3 or 4, so `SHOOT_SINGLE_BULLET_BETWEEN_COORDS` is silent in RDR2 wherever it starts and whoever owns it, and a gun fired by `_SHOOT_TRIGGER_AT_COORDS` (with those guessed parameters, no aim needed) or the shoot task is heard. The game's readings told nothing (the ammo never fell, `_HAS_PED_SHOT_RECENTLY` false for every shot heard, `IS_PED_SHOOTING` true only for the task, the impact test true even for the one that landed nowhere), so the row logs only `IS_PED_SHOOTING` and the gun in hand. `GunTrigger(h, at, weapon)` (Gunshot.cs: the gun must be in hand; ammo topped to 6 and 6 in the cylinder; returns false with no gun in hand) is now pulled first in `DespairShot` (the silent bullet still follows, so a missed trigger still kills; `act.Triggered`, logged in the 0.3 s line as `the trigger pulled | no gun in hand to fire, so no shot heard`) and in `ExecutionBullet` (the execution's fallback after 3 s of the gunman's real fight). Seen 15:33 (Eli Pickett, Jamie's arc; Sterling Edgerton Tin Cup, the leader's): the shot at 30% and 37% (the 50 ms step works), the trigger pulled and heard during the clip, but 0.3 s on `health 75 -> 75, still alive: no bullet struck them` both times: neither the trigger's bullet nor the made-in-the-air one hurts the person firing it at their own head (owner and target the same, or the start inside the head), so the death was still the health 0 at the end of the limp, 1.5 s on, by when the clip has them down; Lex (16:07, Obadiah Nolan) then heard the shot well before the fall, so `DespairShot` now sets health 0 at the shot itself (the trigger and the silent bullet first), logs `WAKE despair: X dead at the shot, the trigger pulled | no gun in hand to fire`, and the 0.3 s look (`ShotHealth`, `ShotCheckAt`) is gone. Then (16:15, Ephraim Clayton) the shot at 31% looked too late: Jamie's set point is 15% now, and as the shot's clip starts `ClipEventPhase` asks `FIND_ANIM_EVENT_PHASE` for the first of `ShotEvents` (Fire, WeaponFire, FireWeapon, Shoot, Gunshot, Shot, Bang, and case variants; no death or ragdoll names, which may sit later) and puts the shot there (`act.ShotPoint`, `ShotEvent`; log `WAKE despair: <clip> names its own 'X' at N%` or `names none of the shot events`), and while it plays `ShotEventFired` (`HAS_ANIM_EVENT_FIRED` on each) fires the shot the moment one goes off; the shot's log says which. Seen 16:19 (Oscar Hale): Jamie's clip names none of them, and the shot at 15% lined up with the fall (Lex: "pretty lined up now"), so 15% stands; the lookup stays for other clips. The same run proved the words: last (to you), pain, burden, plea (to you), calm, all at their points. The leader's arc is taken out (Lex, after Sterling: he lay down, got up and lay down again before the shot): `Despair.GunArc` is always "jamie", `DespairSequence` and `DespairPreload` lost its set, `DespairTestWays` is five ways (ticktest `DespairTest`: Jamie's every time).
- Animation rows (`Game/WestworldScript.AnimTest.cs`, Testing rows `despairanims` and `stageanims`): `DespairClips` (25) and `StageClips` (25), each `A(group, dict, clip, flags, gun, women's dict, women's clip)` with the add-on's flags (0 once, 1 loop, 2 hold the last frame, 8|16 upper body; an upper-body clip the play check can't see is retried over the whole body). `AnimTestStart`: the nearest free person within 12 m (not a story model, not one of enRAGE's records, `MayDirectWhyNot` clear, not `NotFree`), events blocked, tasks cleared (up from a seat), turned to face you; each clip: set requested (3 s), the revolver in hand only for gun clips (`GIVE_WEAPON_TO_PED`, never fired), played, `Hint` "Animation N of M (group): clip", started (`IS_ENTITY_PLAYING_ANIM`), `GET_ANIM_DURATION`, the run time and how far it moved them (1 m or more: it carries them off); then `CLEAR_PED_TASKS` and the next. `AnimTestEnd`: the revolver taken back, events unblocked, wander; on a mission, shutdown, 40 m off, or they're gone. While it runs they're `IsAcknowledging` (scenes, nods, talk, sights pass them over) and out of the wake scan. Log `WAKE anim #N (group) dict / clip: played|didn't start, N s long, ran N s, moved them N m`, `WAKE anim test (...) done`; F7 `TEST anim test:`. All 23 sets were in Story Mode on 2026-10-27 (`WAKE anim sets:`, 13:56). First despair run (14:14-14:17, Terrell Mobley, a_m_m_vallaborer_01): 16 of 25 played; Jamie's loops and shot played (the shot carries him 0.9 m), his first two didn't start (straight after he stood); the hideout leader's base, action (32.7 s) and suicide_shoot (2.6 s) all played in place (0.1-0.2 m); the gunslinger's 1.6 m; collapses: die_npc 8.7 s in place, ko_shot_head 0.8 m, ko_collapse 1.2, the long death 1.5; the sobbing played, the lady's kneeling sob and wail didn't; the grieving upper body played; of the poisoning only intro_vic. Lex: he went back to his day between clips, so since then the run borrows them (`Spawner.Borrow`, or `BorrowLight` for someone another script holds; `Spawner.Release` at the end), holds them with TASK_STAND_STILL between clips, and tries a clip that didn't start once more after CLEAR_PED_TASKS_IMMEDIATELY, logging the scenario and the play task's status (`GET_SCRIPT_TASK_STATUS` SCRIPT_TASK_PLAY_ANIM). Second and third runs (Adella Monk, a_f_m_valprostitute_01, 14:19 and 14:24, still the build before the borrowing): 17 then 25 of 25 played, so the misses were first-time loading; a woman plays the men's arcs; but the revolver showed only for the first two clips (Lex): clearing orders between clips puts it away and someone with no holster loses it, so each gun clip now gives it again forced into the hand (and the retry too), waits half a second, and logs `a gun in hand at the start: yes|NO` (`IS_PED_ARMED` 4). The same will matter for despair's own gun once the clips go in (it gives once and lets the game draw). Movement read across the three runs: the same distance every run on both people means the clip's own motion (the gunslinger 1.4-1.6 m, the long death 1.5, ko_collapse 1.0-1.2, Jamie's shot 0.8-1.0: mostly falls); a distance that changes, or a clip that stops early (the woman's grieving intro 5.1 m, its loop stopped at 1.7 s), was the game taking them back between or during clips (Lex's read), which the borrowing ends. The fuzzy check flags that task name (the game's, which enRAGE also names), accepted as a code name like SCRIPT_TASK_AIM_GUN_AT_COORD: the baseline is 209 from here. The voice probe, 14:06-14:10: a_m_m_vallaborer_01 32 of 32, a_f_m_valprostitute_01 (bank 0462) 30 (no GREET_GENERAL_STRANGER, GOODBYE_START), so every line plays out of context; live moments that got nothing out (Samuel Arnett, 14:01) now give way to one other moment of the stage (`WakeVoiceRun.Second`, in `WakeVoiceEnd`; the awake act waits for it as for any). The voice probe skips lines heard this session (`_wvHeard`, from the probe and from moments) and a second probe ends the first with its summary.
- The wake scan (`WakeActsTick`) counts only living humans toward its eight nearest, and `WAKE act: ... [passive: why]` says what held them (`PassiveWhy`: a counter, enRAGE's `MayDirectWhyNot`, fighting, fleeing, knocked down, riding, in the water, a mission entity, ours). A subagent's read of the code and the 10-27 log (09:46-12:27): despair at 90% took every directable awake person on their first act (4 of 4), so those left were look-only; awake is an act of about a minute at most every 2 minutes, not a state; stages 1 and 2 share stare and lookaround, and stage 4's endings reuse stage 3's kneel and run; nobody advanced a stage on their own (share 1%). Next (Lex): distinct body language per stage and better despair endings, from the sets `WAKE anim sets:` finds (23 candidates from the game's dump, among them three authored suicide arcs: `script_story@mry1@ig@ig6_jamie_attempts_suicide`, `script_hideout@shady_belle@leader` suicide_shoot, `script_rc@gun3@ig@ig3_shoothimself`; the collapse `ai_damage@ko@base@shot` ko_shot_head; the madman's speech set `script_special_ped@pdjbm_john_baptising_madman@ig1_speech_greets`; notes in the session scratchpad `anim-research`).

## Writing brief for thoughts

Lex's brief is saved at `C:\mods\prompts\You are an expert Behavioral Psycho.txt` (outside the repo). Inner-life lines follow it: short fragmented sentences, one sensory detail, period vocabulary, and the frames: immediate bodily needs, status anxiety (the looking-glass self), negativity and loss bias and the just-world excuse, the gap between the outside and the inside, 1899 anxieties, and granular observation of the player. Pools live in `Core/InnerLife.cs`.

## Town posts (0.6.0)

- `Core/Posts.cs` (table of 46 counter models, successor rules, thought pools), `Core/Engine.Posts.cs` (`HolderOf`, `VacatePost`, `PostThought`), `Game/WestworldScript.Posts.cs` (`HolderAt`, `PostsTick`, `PostKilled`, `NotePostVisit`, `PostsStatus`). Store: `Posts` (Fix creates it for old saves); `Resident.PostId/Kin/KinOf/From/TookOverAt`. `KeepResident` never drops a living post holder.
- Shot down (`TownPostsAfterDeath`, default `survive`): the game respawns the same model bandaged, so `Engine.PostShot` keeps the holder (Wounds, WoundedAt, WoundedByPlayer, KnowsShooter = seen or within 25 m), sets `Post.FellAt` and `SawFall` (enRAGE ids of witnesses); `HolderAt` binding them alive after a fall calls `PostBack` (BackAt, +15 awareness to witnesses in West World, a town news line). The scan uses `Posts.BodyFells` (once per fall; old bodies after a reload or from before the return don't count). A holder marked dead under the old rules is revived as shot down. `successor` keeps the succession below.
- Identity is by model (each u_ counter model is unique to its town). `ResidentWhy` lets post models through the story-model and grown-up checks; `ResidentOf` returns the post holder before the 35% recast; `ResidentsPrune` leaves a dead holder to the posts code, so how they died is recorded (`VacatePost`, from `OnKilled` at once, or the 3 s scan of dead post models for any other death). The scan uses `Posts.BodyVacates`: a body bound this session (`_bodyWas`, kept after unbinding) vacates only the holder it was; an unknown body (an old one after a reload) never vacates a successor while one of the model is alive or within a day of the takeover. The successor is made the next time the game puts the model at the counter, with a different first name; relative 65% (same sex as the model), else a newcomer from elsewhere.
- Outfits: `GET_NUM_META_PED_OUTFITS` is logged once per model; a successor gets preset 1..n-1, equipped with `_EQUIP_META_PED_OUTFIT_PRESET` + `_UPDATE_PED_VARIATION` (enRAGE's pair) only off screen and without line of sight, again each time the game respawns them. No native reads a ped's current preset.
