- The label over a counter keeper ("Valentine Gunsmith", "Valentine Bartender") can't be changed (tried in 0.6.1 and dropped at Lex's call): `_SET_PED_PROMPT_NAME` (with text in never-freed memory, as the game keeps the pointer) had no effect set every 15 s or every frame, and enRAGE's own `SetPromptName` skips unique models, probably for the same reason. A name drawn with `Draw.Text` over the label looked wrong (another font, and it didn't follow the head like the game's label). Their names show on the ticker ("William Garrity, the gunsmith").
- Kept still: wake acts passive, no despair, out of street scenes (`DramaTick` pool), and stalking already excludes u_ models. Not during missions. enRAGE treats these models as the game's own (`GameNamed`) and doesn't name them.

## Town regulars (0.6.1, roadmap 2)

- `Core/Regulars.cs` (`Back`: a free regular of the town, model and sex, the one touched longest ago; `NewOutfit`: an outfit no regular of that model in the town wears, at most `PerModel` (4) per model and `RegularsPerTown` per town), `Game/WestworldScript.Regulars.cs` (`RegularsTick` from Tick04 after PostsStep: every 2 s in a town, not in a mission, up to 3 casts), `Game/Bridge.Looks.cs` (enRAGE's `_named` and `_fpOf`, read only). Resident gains `Regular` and `Outfit` (old saves load false/0; `Outfit` is only read when `Regular`).
- Who can be cast: never on screen since the handle appeared (`SampleSeen` every 0.5 s within 300 m; a handle reused for another model starts over), 20-150 m off, not on screen and no line of sight, not a mission entity, `ResidentWhy` null, not a post model, and not `Bridge.Looked` (enRAGE matches its acquaintances by a look fingerprint, `Fingerprint`/`Prints`, so a ped it has looked at is never re-dressed). Everyone present at the first tick after load counts as checked. Dressing: `_EQUIP_META_PED_OUTFIT_PRESET` + `_UPDATE_PED_VARIATION`, at cast time (they're unseen then), only if `RegularsDress` and `Bridge.CanTellLooked`.
- Meeting: the three nearest eligible ordinary people within 12 m get ticker turns (rank +10); a regular's `PersonState.Sightings` goes up once per binding (`NoteRegularMet`), and `PlayerLook.Seen` lets `InnerLife.Again` lines through (22% when near and met twice or more). `ResidentOf`'s 35% recast of woken people skips regulars. `Store.MaxResidents` is 700; pruning drops non-regulars first, then regulars never met.
- Log: `REGULAR new|back: name (trade) in town, outfit N, D m off, unseen[, met N times before]`; at load `REGULARS: kept: Valentine 14, ...; N residents saved`; F7 `TEST regulars`, and `MarkRegulars` puts a `BlipType.CompanionGray` blip labelled with the name on up to 12 bound regulars within 200 m for 60 s (`_regBlips`, expired in RegularsTick, cleared in Shutdown04) and a `Hint` naming the nearest with distance and `Compass` direction. In game (0.6.1): regulars are made and come back ("REGULAR back", "met 1 time before"). Not yet confirmed: whether an outfit preset also fixes the face is the open question.

## Scenes leave a trace (0.6.1, inner lives part 2)

- Names: `SceneCast.Names` (set in `BuildCast` from `SceneNames(a, b, crowd)`, reset for the jealous scene's man/woman/third order): enRAGE's name (`Person.FirstOf`) or a resident's own (`ResidentOf`); a repeated first name becomes the later one's surname. `Scenes.N()` uses them, `StartScene` passes them as the Director's labels, so lines and labels agree (before, enRAGE townsfolk got enRAGE labels and made-up names in the lines). `Director.SwapNamesInLines` swaps the two names in the beats still to come when a fight goes against the script (the cast swap at the brawl).
- Ties (`Core/Ties.cs`, `SceneTie`; enRAGE has its own `NemesisSystem.Tie`, hence the name): `Ties.Effect` maps a scene kind to -1 (argue, brawl, cheat, debt, standoff), +1 (flirt, propose), 0 (smalltalk, gossip, haggle, hosts) or none (songs, tales, pitches, posse, widow, pickpocket, jealous: three people, roles unclear). `NoteSceneTie` (on `StartScene`, so a scene broken up still counts) keeps one tie per pair, only between people who can be met again (`LastingId`: enRAGE folk key, or a regular or already-saved resident); never saves anyone for it. `Store.Ties` (cap `Ties.Max` 300, oldest dropped; `Fix` makes it for old saves; `Rewind` drops newer ones). Log `TIE a & b: kind (warm|hostile|acquainted, N scenes between them)`; F7 `TEST ties`. The dead: `Engine.HasDied(id)` (resident `Dead`, enRAGE folk through `Engine.FolkDead` = `Bridge.FolkDead`, which reads enRAGE's `Acquaintance.Dead` by `(Id ?? FullName)`, or an enRAGE person not `IsAlive`), passed to thoughts as `InnerLife.Context.Dead`. A tie whose other end has died thinks from `DeadFoe`/`DeadFriend`/`DeadKnown` by warmth; town talk from `TownDeadFoe`/`TownDeadWarm`/`TownDeadMended`, survivor first. A worry whose linked counterpart has died is set aside (`InnerLife.CounterpartDead` = `Engine.LinkedDead`: `WorryNow` false, `WorryThought` null, so no worry scenes either; the name is kept, never swapped for a made-up one, since `WorryFill` must never return null for `Exchanges`), and `ThoughtDay` drops links whose worrier has died. Every scene line is logged as `SCENE   name (N m from you): line`, and the end as `SCENE over: ...` or `SCENE stopped after i of n beats: why` (scenes are easy to miss in game: anyone within 22 m, behind the player too).
- Thoughts: `Engine.ThoughtDay(town, id)` adds `Context.Ties` (`Ties.Of`, 4 days) and `Context.TownTies` (`Ties.TalkOf`: same town, warm or hostile, under 2 days, not the thinker's own). In `InnerLife.Thought` a fresh tie takes 45% of the worry band (about 9% of thoughts, offline), and town ties share the town-talk band with the player's deeds. 
- Reasons (0.6.1, slice 1 of reactive NPCs, Lex's call: no timer-driven scenes on whoever stands closest): `Core/Reasons.cs` `Between(a, b, tie, day, now, place)` gives the strongest `Reason` (scene kind, strength, why, `BFirst` = b is A in the scene). With a tie: `PairRest` 120 game minutes (a day after a brawl); hostile -> argue, then brawl for two men from `Stage` 1 if a Hothead/Proud is in it or warmth -3 or stage 2; warm and mixed -> flirt, propose at warmth 2 and stage 2, not at night; else smalltalk/gossip. Without a link: no debt is called in and no courting done by whoever is standing there (that would contradict their thoughts; slice 4 only); any worry at its crisis -> x:snap, at its end -> x:goodnews or x:badnews; both waking -> hosts; player's deeds here and rumours -> gossip; Hothead in a saloon -> argue. `Ties.Note` raises `Stage` each time a pair meets again.
- `Game/WestworldScript.Reasons.cs`: `DramaTick` tries `SpecialScene`, then `ReasonScene` (named, free people within `ReasonRange` 16 m, pairs within `ReasonApart` 7 m, the top 4 by score, the first with one of them on screen and in line of sight; a pair more than 2.6 m apart gets `face` and `meet`, see slice 4b), then a chance meeting (closest standing pair, at most every 3x `DramaGapSeconds`, one of them on screen, never the debt scene). `_sceneAtHandle`: anyone in a scene sits `PersonRestMs` (2 real min) out, chance pairing too (the two who fought 15 min since 0.6.3, see "Drama, first pass"). Log `REASON a & b: why -> kind (strength, apart, from you)`; F7 `TEST reasons`.
- Gestures (`Core/Gestures.cs`): a line's voice tag maps to a mood (angry, mild, merry, greet, afraid, odd) and a verified ai_gestures clip per sex; `Director.Gesture` plays it upper body (8|16) on 55% of spoken lines without another act, not in a scenario, once the set (requested at scene start) has loaded.
- Exchanges (slice 2, `Core/Exchanges.cs`): reasons `x:snap` (worry at stage 2: B greets, A snaps with `Snap[worry]` filled by `InnerLife.WorryFill`), `x:goodnews`/`x:badnews` (stage 3), `x:quarrel` (hostile tie past its first argue: B makes up when kind, or when cold at stage 2+ half the time; A takes it unless Hothead/Proud, 30% then), `x:sweet` (romantic tie past its first flirt). B's answer: `Manner(b, bAtWorst)` = sharp (at the worst of their own, Hothead, Proud), cold (Cold, Greedy, Drifter), kind (Loyal, Coward), else 55% kind per person. The scene's Kind is the outcome (comfort, brushoff, snap, cheer, envy, makeup, argue, sweet), which `Ties.Effect` scores (makeup +2) and `Ties.Thought` pools follow (`Kindness`, `MadeUp`, `Shrugged`); the town talks only of hostile, romantic (`Ties.Romantic`) and made-up ties. `ReasonScene` builds `x:` kinds with `Exchanges.Make`; pairs are scored by strength less 2x their distance apart and 2x the nearer one's distance from the player; `ReasonRange` 16 m. Offline: 312 exchanges over the 13 worries x crisis/good/bad x tempers, every name filled, every gesture in its set.
- Worry links (slice 4a, `Core/Links.cs`, `Store.Links`): `Engine.EnsureLink(p)` is the only place a link is made (called from `ResidentThought`, `FolkThought`, and `PostThought`'s worry roll), only for `Lasting` ids (folk keys, saved residents) whose current worry is debt, feud or courting. `Links.Choose`: living regulars of `p.Home`, not counter keepers or themselves; men for debt and feud (their lines say "he"), the other sex and within 15 years (under 46, not `old`) for courting; a tie first (debt scene, hostile, romantic), else a hash pick. None suitable: asked again 60 game minutes later (`_noLinkUntil`, not saved). `InnerLife.Counterpart` (set in the Engine constructor, read-only) fills `{who}` with `Links.WhoName` (surname for debt and feud, first name for courting). The other end: `ThoughtDay` adds `Context.Linked` (`Links.Against`: the worrier's arc still current), which takes 40% of the worry band when the tie roll doesn't (`Links.OtherThought` by stage and `EndsWellFor`). Pruned to the current and previous arc, cap 400, `Rewind` by `At`. Log `LINK name's kind: owes|at odds with|sweet on other`; F7 `TEST links` (in town, stage, who is around).
- Linked meetings (slice 4b): `PairReasons` finds a current link between a pair and lets them be `LinkedApart` (18 m) apart; `Reasons.Between(..., link)` returns `x:debt|x:feud|x:courting`, strength 85, with the worrier as A (after the tie's rest check). `Exchanges.MakeLinked` by the worrier's stage and `EndsWellFor`: debt (B calls in the eleven dollars of the worry lines, A answers by stage, B by manner per stage; paid, or the mule), feud (fence post, dog, threat; shake or no more talking), courting (B warms only when it ends well; yes or a kind no; ends as `propose` or `brushoff`). Scenes with the pair more than 2.6 m apart open with `face` then the Director's `meet` (B walks over; the next beat waits until they're within 2.4 m, `MeetUntil` 15 s, else the scene is dropped). Offline: 1,440 linked meetings across kinds, stages and endings.
- Bystanders (slice 3, `Core/Crowd.cs`): `AddBystander` (before `StartScene`, reason and chance scenes of two people) gives a scene whose `Crowd.Mood` is quarrel or fight (60%), warm (40%) or sad (30%) a third cast member: a free named candidate within 10 m of the pair, one with a tie to either first. Their one line (`Crowd.Word`: friend of both calms, foe of both eggs on, friend of one or foe of the other takes a side, else by `Exchanges.Manner`) goes in after the second spoken line, labelled with their name (`cast.Names[2]`); never someone with the same first name as either of the two (seen in game: "Gideon & Isaac & Gideon"). Fights use only `Cast[0..1]`. `Onlookers` (after a scene starts): up to six other bystanders within 12 m get a 9 s `TASK_LOOK_AT_ENTITY` on the first speaker, nothing more. Log `BYSTANDER name watches a & b (kind) and calls out`.

## Drama, first pass (0.6.3)

- Lex (2026-10-26): no NPC fights seen all day. The log had 38 REASON scenes (31 `x:*`, 6 gossip, 1 court), 2 brawls and 1 argue, and 7 walk-overs that failed with "a scenario held the other". Fights stay reasoned (never a timer), just reached more often.
- `Core/Reasons.cs`: `Hot(s)` = Hothead or Proud. A hostile tie rests `HostileRest` 45 game minutes (a warm one `PairRest` 120; a brawl a day; not a rough, so the timid victim can still keep clear of the man soon after). Blows (two men, hostile tie): as before from `Stage` 1, and now at the first quarrel too when both are `Hot`, or one is `Hot` and either is `Drunk`. A `Hot` x at the worst of a worry snapping at a `Hot` y: `brawl` (56) on a 20% roll per x and day (`snapblows`), else `x:snap` as before. Drink and temper (two men, saloon, night, x `Hot`, x or y `Drunk`, `DrinkTemperChance` 35% per x and day, `drinktemper`): `argue` 46 if y is a Coward, else `rough` 57 (fallback brawl) at `RoughChance`, else `brawl` 56; otherwise the saloon hothead's stranger argue/rough as before, +14 strength at night, the rough roll now per x and day. Offline: ticktest `DramaTest` (300 saloon-at-night crowds with a drunk hothead: 190 fights, rough 144 / brawl 46; 300 street-by-day crowds: 24 brawls).
- Game side: `NoteInScene(people, kind, now)` gives the first two of a brawl, rough or drunk scene `FoughtRestMs` (15 real min, `_restMsOf`) instead of `PersonRestMs` (2 min), back to 2 min if the Director's `meet` gives up (`NeverMet`); `Resting(h, now)` is the one check (`ReasonCandidates`, the chance pool in `DramaTick`). F7 `TEST reasons:` starts with how many who came to blows are sitting it out. Walk-overs: in `ReasonScene` the walker is whoever isn't in a scenario (the second, unless the second sits or leans and the first doesn't), and the Director's `meet` accepts 3.6 m instead of 2.4 m when the one waiting is in a scenario (across a counter or a table). Not yet seen in game.

## Self-defence (0.6.0)

- `WestworldScript.Watch.cs`: `Hit.CameAtYou` is set once at the first damage event (`CameAtYou`): not a lawman, not in `_youStarted` (he hurt them first; kept 5 min), and enRAGE's `_cameAtYou`/`_selfDefence` hold them (`Bridge.Defence.cs`, read only), or enRAGE's `RoleOf` gives a fighter role (Active, Crew, HiredGun, Patrol, Skirmish, Hunter, Rival, Camp: red on the map), or `DefenceScan` saw them in combat with him before he hurt them and without his aim on them in the last 10 s (`GET_ENTITY_PLAYER_IS_FREE_AIMING_AT` / `GET_PLAYER_TARGET_ENTITY`, 8-byte slots), or relationship 5, or a mission. `Hit.Fighting` is separate (it may be upgraded later; only kill vs murder wording). `Deeds.KillKind` decides the deed (null = none); `namedkill` is skipped too, `NoteDeath` and Fallen still run. `OnCrime` skips an assault while `OnlyDefending` (last 30 s). `DefenceBounty` (from `DefenceScan`; the choice is `Core/DefenceLaw.Decide`): `Defending` on every hit and every kill on someone who came at him (not in missions) starts the fight if needed (`StartDefence`: bounty base = min of the last 4 s of `_bountyHistory`, wanted before, start point and state via `_GET_MAP_ZONE_AT_COORDS(..., 0)`) and extends it; until 90 s after the last one, a rise is put back with `LAW.SET_BOUNTY` and a new wanted level cleared (`SelfDefenceNoBounty`). Tainted (no change) if `_lastOffenceAt` or `_lastRealCrimeAt` falls within 5 min before the fight or during it. Animals don't taint. Moving 300 m from the start or into another state rebases to the current bounty (bounty is per region in the game, so a debt elsewhere is never wiped). Up to enRAGE 0.9.5.2 its FairGame also reset bounty for its own outlaw outfits (not soldiers). 0.9.5.3 dropped that and makes its outlaws gang men to the game itself, so killing one is no crime at all. Either way RealNPCs only ever lowers bounty to a pre-fight value.

- `ShieldDefence` (every frame, from `PollWorldEventsImpl`, again in the frame of a hit): `LAW.SUPPRESS_CRIME_THIS_FRAME` for CRIME_MURDER, CRIME_ASSAULT, CRIME_UNARMED_ASSAULT, CRIME_LASSO_ASSAULT, CRIME_THREATEN, CRIME_DISTURBANCE (hashes from rdr3_discoveries crime_types.lua) while within 1.5 s of a hit on a came-at-you ped, while one he hit (in `_hits`, under 20 s) lives, or while aiming at one; off if any offence or confirmed crime within 5 min or since the fight began, in missions, or `SelfDefenceNoBounty` off. Law and animal crimes are never suppressed.

- Pleading at gunpoint (`PleadTick`, `WestworldScript.Frontier.cs`) skips anyone `Bridge.EnrageHandlesAtGunpoint` names (`Bridge.Gunpoint.cs`): enRAGE's own `Interrogable(ped, player, 30f)` (its gang men and men in an outfit's colours, not story or game-event people, not mid-fight), or anyone at all while its `_interro` is set. Since enRAGE 0.9.5.3 those men stand their ground at gunpoint unless scared and their crew draws on you, so a plea with hands up would fight it. Ordinary townsfolk are never interrogable in enRAGE, so they still plead.

## Feeling bars and the grudge meter (0.6.1)

- `[Display]`: `FeelingBars`, `FeelingBarsMetres` (3-40, default 15), `GrudgeMeter`, all on by default; `FeelingBarsShowEmpty` (off: Lex wants only the filled part for now) draws the whole bar's length behind the fill. `Core/Feelings.cs` chooses and orders (`Bars`: fear, liking, anger, from `ShowFrom` 3 up; `Meter`: the nemesis after you first, then strangers by score; `LineAt`; `SizeAt`), with the words. `Game/Bridge.Feelings.cs` reads, by name: a nemesis's `Hate` and enRAGE's `EffectiveFear(rec)`; a townsperson's `Acquaintance.Fear/Like/Grudge`; any stranger's `_provoked[handle].Score` (skipping `Record` set or `Settled`) and `UneaseOf(handle)`; `ActiveRecord`; `Balance.GsThreshold` (static) for the nemesis mark; `_debugOn`, so nothing is drawn while enRAGE's own overlay has its bars up; and `_named` for names. `Game/WestworldScript.Feelings.cs` (called from `Frame`): it looks every 300 ms (nearest `BarPeople` 3 within range, on screen, clear line of sight; meter strangers within `MeterRange` 80 m, at most `MeterRows` 4) and draws every frame from that. Bars sit `BarsAboveHead` 0.30 m above head bone 21030, just under where enRAGE's name card is anchored (a guess from the 0.9.5.2 code; tune it there). The meter is a panel at `MeterX/MeterY/MeterW` (0.80, 0.06, 0.18). It hides with `TickerVisible`, the settings panel and the reader. Never reopen enRAGE's own card or bar drawing code for this: the look and the words must stay ours.

## The ticker and the news box (0.6.2)

- Thoughts over heads (0.6.2, `[Ticker] ThoughtsOverHeads`/`ThoughtsOverHeadScale` 0.24, settings version 57; Lex: smaller radius, thoughts above the NPC's head in a smaller font): `Game/WestworldScript.OverHead.cs` `ThoughtOverHead` (its own `Safe`, "thoughts over heads", from `FrameImpl`) draws the ticker's current "near" line with a `Handle` at `ThoughtAboveHead` 0.62 m over head bone 21030 (over our bars at 0.30 m and enRAGE's name card), wrapped at `ThoughtWidth` 0.20 to at most 4 lines, quoted, dark box with the bar's gold edge, scale times `Feelings.SizeAt` (not under 0.75); then neither the bar nor the gold "..." is drawn. Since 2026-10-27 (Lex: thoughts were on the bar again) a `near` line is never drawn on the bar while the setting is on: one that can't go over a head (the thinker off screen or unknown) waits `OverHeadWaitMs` (1.5 s) and the ticker skips to the next (`OverHeadOnly`, `IsOverHeadKind`); `Feed.Thinking` passes the speaker's ped, so a scene speaker's written line goes over their head too; the news and the summary stay on the bar. `TickerNearbyMetres` default 30 -> 20 (old inis on 30 moved). Log `THOUGHTS over heads: the first, ...`; F7 `TEST thoughts:` (over a head / on the bar counts). Not yet seen in game: whether 0.62 m clears enRAGE's card.
- The bottom ticker is for thoughts of people you can see. `RefreshNear` computes `seen` (`IS_ENTITY_ON_SCREEN` and clear line of sight, trace 17) and only adds people in view to `_near`; a counter keeper's visit is still noted when they aren't. `TickerBrain.GapMs` (`TickerGapSeconds`) puts a blank between lines; `_prev` remembers the last line through it, so the one beside you still gets every other turn. With no summary given and nothing to say, `Current` goes null and the bar disappears. The ticker key on a thought goes to the news only when the news is on the ticker (`toNews`), else to the next thought. `TickerLine.Handle` carries the thinker's ped; `DrawThinkingMark` (`WestworldScript.News.cs`, `ThoughtMarker`) draws a gold "..." at `BarsAboveHead`, under their feeling bars if they have any. `Feed.Thinking` (a scene speaker's own line, when the subtitle shows the recording's words) still goes to the ticker.
- Every thought over the thinker's head (Lex, 2026-10-27, after Emilio Castellano's thought showed as a top-left notice from F7): no thought goes to a notice or the bar any more, and to the "Around town" box only when the thinker is out of view and it's about the player (Lex, the same day: far-off thoughts there only about the main character). F7's nearest-record thought is pushed over his head (`recordHandle` in `Test()`) and logged on its `TEST <name>` line; a wake act's `Think` pushes over `act.Handle`; the engine's thoughts about you (`NextThought`: memories, hearsay, your name, the heart, sightings, kin's replies) are all about the player; people in view are asked first (`NextThought(now, inView)`, `_inViewId` = `NearHandleOf(id) != 0` from `_near`), anyone who knows him only when none of them has one, and `Route`'s "thought" case pushes it over the head when the thinker is in `_near`, else to `Notice` (the "Around town" box by default) as before. Offline: ticktest `KnowingTest` (nobody in view, no thought; only the one in view thinks; the one far off kept his). `Notice`'s "thought" branch (the "to himself" notice) has no callers left. Everything pushed as "near" with a handle waits `OverHeadWaitMs` and is passed over if they're out of view.
- News: `Notice` with `NewsRoute == "corner"` (the default) calls `NewsPush`; world news by `Settings.WarRoute` (feed by default: the game's top-left updates through `Feed.Notice`; corner; ticker). `WestworldScript.News.cs` shows one item at a time under the "Sore at you" panel (`_meterBottom`, set as the meter draws, 0 when it doesn't) or in its place, titled "Around town": the label line, then up to 5 wrapped lines (quoted for thoughts and diaries), 7 s + 45 ms a character (16 s at most), fading in and out; its time doesn't run while hidden (`TickerVisible`, the panel, the reader). At most 6 wait. With the corner route the ticker gets no summary.

## Real-voice talk (0.6.2, roadmap 1, first slice)

- `Core/RealTalk.cs`: turns as line names (`TalkTurn`: who, lines to try in order) and the captions, one per kind of line, in our own words (never a paraphrase of a particular recording). `Speech.PlayFirst` plays the first line the ped's voice has, by name: a chosen take with its words when `BankOf` is certain and the book has the line (takes from banks.txt, or from words.txt, which proves a take exists), else `Say` (the game picks the take). It has no model-wide Worked/Failed cache, since conversation lines differ between voices of one model. `Feed.Overheard` shows a line that is already playing, ahead of the queue: its words (`Real`) or a caption (`Caption`, only with `Feed.CaptionsOn` from `[Output] OverheardTalk`).
- `Game/WestworldScript.Talk.cs` (`TalkStep` in `Tick04`): Shift+F7 (`TalkTestKey`) picks the nearest bystander (a voice the book knows first) and the nearest other within 20 m. If they're over 2.4 m apart, the second walks over (`TASK_GO_TO_ENTITY`, 15 s) and nothing is said until they're close. Then they face each other, and the turns play; each waits on `Speech.Busy` (8 s at most) before the next. It stops if someone is gone, fighting or fleeing, or once together they drift over 5 m apart. Log `TALK test:` at the start and `TALK a & b over|stopped: line take N "words", x s | ...`. Scheduler (`TalkAuto`, `[Scenes] RealVoiceTalk`, `RealVoiceTalkSeconds` 120, 70-130% of it between conversations, a look every 4 s once due): two `TalkCandidates` (bystanders within 15 m: at 21-24 m Lex couldn't hear them; not story models, not in a Director scene or a wake act, not enRAGE fighters, not speaking) standing within 4 m, at least one on screen with clear line of sight, the pair nearest you; never while the Director is busy or Feed is talking; no walking. Rests: a person 5 min, a pair 15 min. Kinds by `RealTalk.KindsFor(WeatherKind(_GET_PREV_WEATHER_TYPE_HASH_NAME), hour, seed)`: bad weather first in rain/storm/snow, good weather by daylight 07-19 in sun/clearing, then how-it's-going, gossip (CHAT_RESPONSE_A-E answered by RESPONSE_<letter> or GENERIC) and greeting in a seeded order, each tried with either person opening. A finished one notes a `smalltalk` tie (`NoteSceneTie`). Log `TALK begins|test:` and `TALK a & b over|stopped:`. Seen working 2026-10-25: valtownfolk gossip C and D answered by reactions C and D (4.3-5.7 s, then 1.6-1.9 s), so the game plays these lines by name and `Speech.Busy` tracks them. The test key picks the pair nearest the player, preferring two already within 4 m. Holding them (after Lex heard a reply go missing: the listener walked off): `SET_BLOCKING_OF_NON_TEMPORARY_EVENTS` true for both at `TalkBegin`, as the Director does for its cast, and false at `TalkEnd`, which also `CLEAR_PED_TASKS` anyone not in a scenario. `TalkFace` (`TASK_TURN_PED_TO_FACE_ENTITY` + look-at for `TalkHoldMs` 9 s) is renewed before every line. Automatic talks only pick two people both slower than 0.6 m/s (`GET_ENTITY_SPEED`), never passers-by crossing. Lawmen never talk (`Natives.Bystander` leaves them out). Heard working by Lex 2026-10-25 (Cole and Lark: the good-weather pair, then a smalltalk TIE). Moments (settings version 42, Lex's call: "tie those lines in for when the conditions are right"): `TalkOpen` builds a `TalkMoment` (weather, hour, `_placeKind == "saloon"`, `TalkSweethearts`, `VoiceTalk.Riders`, `RealTalk.StoryCodes(_placeTown)`, `CrudeTalk`) for `RealTalk.KindsFor(moment, seed)`: riders -> `Posse` (CHAT_POSSE) first; a place story (`PlaceStory`: CHAT_STORY_LS + the town's code, VAL, RHD, SD/NBX, STR, ASB, ARM, TUM, VHT, BLW, LAG, EMR, WAP; only voices that have one play it) 25%; sweethearts `Flatter` (CHAT_FLATTER -> FLATTER_RESPONSE) 60%, plus `CrudeSweet` (CHAT_PERV -> PERV_RESPONSE, then TEASE_RESPONSE) at night (21-04) 30%; otherwise `CrudeStranger` (CHAT_PERV -> CUT_THAT_OUT, SHAME_ON_YOU, GENERIC_INSULT_MED_NEUTRAL, DISMISSIVE_REACT) in a saloon of an evening (18-04) 35%; then the weather, then the plain kinds. `RealTalk.Fits` skips an opener: crude goes only man -> woman (`IS_PED_MALE`). `TalkSweethearts`: a warm `Ties.Romantic` tie, or a current courting `WorryLink` either way. The replies were chosen from the farmhand's transcripts (CHAT_POSSE is about a band of riders; FLATTER_RESPONSE and PERV_RESPONSE are the game's answers; TEASE_RESPONSE answers a TEASE, so only a fallback); town names match enRAGE's zone table (`GetZoneName`). `RidersPassing` (every 2 s): three or more humans on mounts moving over 1.5 m/s within `RidersNear` 40 m and within `RidersTogether` 12 m of one another, not the player; it starts a talk whether or not one is due (`RiderTalkRestMs` 180 s between), and doesn't reset the usual turn (next at least 30 s later). The test key passes `RidersPassing` too. The end line names the moment: `TALK a & b over (riders going by, saloon, town EMR, foul weather, 22:00): ...`. Offline TalkTest covers the order, the rates, CrudeTalk off and Fits. The riders, stories and sweethearts haven't been seen in game yet. `GENERIC_INSULT_MED_NEUTRAL` is a game line name enRAGE also names (fuzzy 199 = 197 + those two). The test key sets `TalkMoment.Sure`: every kind the moment allows is tried, without the chance rolls (so Shift+F7 by a man and a woman in a saloon after 18:00 always tries the crude remark first). It also prefers a pair the moment suits (sweethearts, or a man and a woman in a saloon of an evening: 8 m off their score) and logs everyone within 15 m, nearest first, with why anyone is left out (`TalkTestRoll`, `TalkLeftOut`: `TALK test: around you: Rachel (f, a_f_m_valprostitute_01, 3 m); Sam (m, u_m_m_valbartender_01, 4 m, left out: one of the story's own people)`). Played in the Valentine saloon 2026-10-25 22:50-22:53 by the log, three times (CHAT_PERV then CUT_THAT_OUT, a_m_m_valtownfolk_02 to a_f_m_valprostitute_01, one automatic), and HEARD by Lex at 22:56:45 (Earl & Belinda Adair, 4.4 s then 2.4 s). Seen in the same minute: the first press on that pair said nothing and the next, 7 s later, worked. The "missing" women's replies were the same thing: banks.txt gives 0461 (a_f_m_valtownfolk_01) RESPONSE_GENERIC and RESPONSE_GOOD_WEATHER and 0462 (a_f_m_valprostitute_01) RESPONSE_GENERIC, and one saloon girl refused GREET_GENERAL_FAMILIAR that two others with voice 0462 played. `PLAY_PED_AMBIENT_SPEECH_NATIVE` just returns false now and then (all six calls `PlayFirst` makes for a line), probably while the game's speech is busy in a crowd. So `TalkTryAgain` retries a turned-down line `TalkRetries` (4) times, `TalkRetryMs` (750) apart, re-facing them each time; the log says `the game turned down the opening|answer (X was speaking); trying again`, a line that then plays gets `(on try N)`, and a stop says how many tries. Seen working 23:05 (Ethel's RESPONSE_A on try 3); one saloon pair refused every opening for all 5 tries at 23:03 and spoke a minute later. The test key's preference for a suiting pair applies only to pairs already within `TalkApart` (it picked mixed pairs 5-8 m apart and both walk-overs failed in the saloon); Rhodes saloon 23:10 (Lex: working): CHAT_PERV -> CUT_THAT_OUT three times from a_m_m_rhdupperclass_01 to a_f_m_rhdprostitute_01 (voices not in the book, takes chosen by the game), one stopped after the remark when they drifted apart. A failed walk now logs `still N m apart; X moved M m[, in a scenario]` (`WalkFrom`). A voice could be swapped for a line it really lacks (the speech struct takes a voice name, `Speech.Play`'s `voice`), but she'd change voice mid-conversation; not needed while RESPONSE_GENERIC covers the gaps. Order: riders, the pair (sweethearts or the saloon remark), the town's story, the weather, the plain kinds. Next, in the README's order: chained exchanges (a hello into news), friends teasing (TEASE -> TEASE_RESPONSE, between people with a warm tie; added by Lex 2026-10-25; the farmhand's TEASE is a jibe and his TEASE_RESPONSE a curt rebuff), CHAT_GOOD/BAD_MEMORY about a passer-by with a tie, reactions aloud to street events, greetings in passing (item 17).

## Flirting by chance, and trysts (0.6.3, roadmap 26)

- Lex (2026-10-26): every woman said no, mostly from fear (Arthur's self-defence kills feed enRAGE's witness fear, and `Interest.Answer` made "afraid", "witnessed" and "hostile" flat noes) or from being spoken for. Wanted: the saloon's women always, single women a high roll, married women a low roll, their thoughts still counting.
- `Interest.ForPlayer(ask, WillingSingle, WillingTaken, out why, out chance)`, from the three player flirt paths through `Engine.PlayerAnswer` (NPC courtship keeps `Interest.Answer`): for a woman, a flat no only for spam, "scorned" or not drawn; `Ask.SaloonGirl` (a woman in the saloon the player is in, `NowPlace`, or of the "girl" trade group) is always yes (why "saloon"); otherwise chance = WillingSingle (70) or, if `Interest.Spoken` (Taken, or married, or a spouse's name), WillingTaken (20), plus `RegardTilt` (smitten +30, grateful/fond +15, met +5, wary -10, witnessed/afraid -15, hostile -35), 5 per Taste weight, glad +5, sore -5, a worry at its worst -15; clamped 10-95 single, 3-50 taken. The roll is `Voice.Hash(id|willing|game day)`: the same all day. Under the chance: yes (why smitten, "stolen" for the spoken for, else the best Taste key or the regard); within 15 over: maybe; else no with the reason (married/elsewhere, hate, fear, badrep, the worst Taste, sore, busy, cold). Men: `Answer` as before. `Romance.Welcome` gained "saloon" and "stolen". The FLIRT log line adds `chance N% (kind)`.
- Trysts (`[Romance] Trysts`, settings version 59; `Core/Trysts.cs`, `Game/WestworldScript.Trysts.cs`): a woman's yes (`FlirtResult.Tryst`, kind saloon|taken|single) leaves an offer for 2 minutes (`TrystOffered`, a hint toast the first 3 times a session); flirting with her again (`TrystAsked`, checked in `Flirt` after the grown-up and story checks) says `Trysts.Ask` and her `Trysts.Yes` unless a mission, a fight, horseback, her fear or distance (over 6 m) stands in the way; then `TrystTick` (own `Safe`, run from `Tick04` before the control check since the controls are off in the dark): she stands still (if `MayDirect`), controls off, `DO_SCREEN_FADE_OUT(1500)`, `ADD_TO_CLOCK_TIME(1, 0, 0)` and `Engine.Tryst` (affection +15, +4 for the saloon's women, a `tryst:<day>` mark, `trysts` count, a townsperson's enRAGE like +5), `DO_SCREEN_FADE_IN`, controls on, her `Trysts.After` thought over her head. `TrystWatchdog` (outside the guard, every tick) brings the light and the controls back after 12 s of dark whatever happened; `TrystAbort` on mission start and shutdown. Log `TRYST offered|asked|not now|over`, `TRYST <name> (kind)` from the engine; F7 `TEST trysts:`. Offline: ticktest `TrystTest` (saloon always, single ~72%, married ~20%, afraid ~57%, hostile ~36%). Not yet seen in game.

## Who can know what, and when (0.6.3, a player's report through Lex, 2026-10-26)

- The report: notices from strangers that "a stranger killed my friend", constant; people angry within seconds about an O'Driscoll camp killed in the wilds; a diary on one of them swearing revenge for a kill 30 s old. Causes: `Engine.Observe` did `WriteDiary` and `Spread` in the same second a memory about the player appeared (enRAGE gives witnesses within 60 m `SAW YOU KILL` at once; its friends' `DeathNews` come 3-48 game h later, `ByYou` whether or not anyone saw: enRAGE's rule, untouched); `NextThought` turned any enRAGE person's memory anywhere into a corner notice (+25 for under a day old), and "a stranger" is `PlayerRef` for someone who doesn't know his name; `Knows` counted anyone with a memory of him; unwitnessed violent deeds started town talk and four locals at once.
- `Core/Knowing.cs`: `LaterAct` (Store.Later, `Fix`, `Rewind` by At, cap 400), `DiaryDue` (that evening from 20:00, at least 3 h on; late at night means the next evening), `WordAfterMinutes` 60, `TalkDue` (seen: 60 min; unseen: found 360-1080 min on), `Met` (Talks > 0, Sightings >= 2, Heart != 0: from the player's side, never KnowsName), `Topic` (lowercased, pronouns out). `Engine.DueLater` runs the due acts for the living only (`LATER X didn't live to ...`). `Knows` = `Met`, so life updates and romance gossip follow it; `NextThought` skips the unmet; `CornerFresh` (not saved) gives one notice per topic per game day across people (`Skip` marks the rest thought); `PushAbout` for the diary, word and relative-letter notices; `DeliverDeath`'s telegram notice once per death. `DayEntry`'s known deed needs 3 h too.
- Deeds: `OnDeed` keeps the witnesses' memories immediate, and for `Deeds.IsViolent` kinds sets `Deed.TalkAt`/`Unseen` and returns; `DeedTalkTick` (WorldTick) starts `DeedTalk` (rumour, locals, paper) when due; a seen deed whose witnesses are all dead by then becomes `Unseen` and waits to be found. A deed nobody saw gets a body watch (`DeedEyes.Body`, `BodyWatchMs` 120 s): `CameUponBody` makes anyone within 20 m of it with clear sight of the player, while he's within 30 m, a witness (Unseen off, TalkAt an hour on, their life watched; `DEED ...: X came upon the body and saw you by it`). Game side: `NoteDeedEyes` keeps the handles within 35 m at the deed (`DeedEyes`, a witness seen dead stays dead), `DeedEyesStep` (Tick04, every 1 s) lowers `Deed.Witnesses`. Unseen: `Rumours.Version` says somebody / someone nobody saw / whoever it was at every fidelity and no embellishment, `Deeds.Paper` "Nobody saw who did it." and "persons unknown", locals get a `heard:` mark instead of `KnownDeeds` (which feeds what is said to his face). Offline: ticktest `KnowingTest`. Not yet seen in game.
- `Deed.Known` (not saved: `!Unseen && TalkAt == 0`) gates everything that stands for the town knowing it was him: `ThoughtDay`'s `TownDeeds` (which feeds the flirt's `BadHere` refusal, `Regard.OfResident`'s wary and town talk), `Arrival` and its notoriety, `Legend`, `PlayerNameKnownIn`, the fame thought; `RecentCrime` (posses) needs the talk to have started. Lex's save at the time: 263 of 1,356 people met (190 talked to); `Sighted` skips anyone in combat, so a nemesis only ever fought may not count yet.
- Greet first (Lex, same day): `PollGreets` notes each greeting (`_greetedAt`, game time); `Flirt` sets `Engine.GreetedFirst` (1 within `GreetCountsMs` 3 min, else -1) into `Interest.Ask.Greeted`, worth `Interest.GreetTilt` 15 either way for women. The night is its own roll: `FlirtResult.Night` from `Trysts.NightChance(kind, greeted)` (saloon 100/80, single 85/55, taken 70/40; 100/70/55 when not known) and `Trysts.NightYes` (id|night|day); `TrystOffer.Night`; `TrystAsked` on a no says `Trysts.NotTonight` (a cold approach is told about manners) voiced with the no set; CasualHookups' key stays held on her. Our lock-on prompt reads "Hook up" while she has an offer (`_flirtPromptHook`). CasualHookups' own "Flirt" text on B can't be changed (it's in its .asi). Offline: ticktest `GreetTest`.

## Trysts in game, flirt voices, CasualHookups (0.6.3, after Lex's first tryst test, 2026-10-26)

- The black screen (19:15): `InWorld()` is false while `Game.IsScreenFadedOut`, and `Step` returned before `Tick04`, so after the tryst's own fade-out neither `TrystStep` nor `TrystWatchdog` ever ran again (our log stopped dead; enRAGE's went on, "held: fade"). `Step` now calls `TrystInTheDark` when `InWorld()` fails: the tryst and its watchdog step while a tryst is going on or dark, unless loading or paused. Anything else that fades the screen must do the same.
- Flirt voices (Lex: "not much voice dialogue"): `Core/FlirtVoices.cs` gives line names by answer and reason (`Reply`), the night's yes (`Night`) and the player's (`You`: GREET_SECOND_COMPLIMENT_CONV and GREET_SECOND_FEMALE_CONV first, names the CasualHookups mod plays and no townsfolk voice lists, so probably the player's; then CHAT_FLATTER, GREET_FLIRTY), each a pool turned by a seed (person and every 10 game minutes) then a tail. `Speech.WhenFirst` plays the first a voice has by name (`PlayFirst`, so FLATTER_RESPONSE and PERV_RESPONSE, missing from the decoded bank list, are tried) and sets `Speech.LastLine`; `FlirtVoice` logs `FLIRT voice: who: LINE take N | none of its lines played` on every try. `FlirtResult.Heart` carries how fond she was. Lines picked by the farmhand's words (never quoted); TEASE_RESPONSE is a curt rebuff, only in noes. Offline: ticktest `FlirtVoicesTest` (82 sets: known names, no warm line in a no or cold in a yes, variety).
- CasualHookups (Lex's choice, "RealNPCs decides"): a closed native .asi by another author in the game folder, with its own Flirt prompt (its `PromptKey`, G as it comes, the same `InteractOption1` enRAGE's Interrogate and social prompts use on foot) and its own dice (notInterested 5 / givesInImmediately 45 / needsSecondFlirt 50). Nothing can start its scene from .NET. `Game/WestworldScript.Hookups.cs`: `HookupsFind` (first `HookupsStep`, from Tick04 before the control check) looks for `CasualHookups.asi` beside the scripts folder; with `[Romance] CasualHookupsDecides` (and Romance and Trysts on) `HookupsLinked`. `HookupsSetUpOnce` (Lex's choices, 2026-10-26): if `RealNPCs\backup\CasualHookups.ini.before-realnpcs` doesn't exist, copy the ini there, then G -> B (`INPUT_INTERACT_HORSE_BRUSH`; in the lock-on menu R greet, F antagonize, E rob, Q info, G enRAGE, H our Flirt, B the horse brush and shop bounty; on foot B is also `INPUT_OPEN_SATCHEL_MENU`, unproven whether it opens while locked on), femaleStaysNaked false (enRAGE's look fingerprint), and 0/100/0 only when the key is off G (on G nothing can be held, so every woman would go along); never again after (the panel's changes stand). `HookupsHold` never disables G (`HookupsGHash` 0x760A9C6F): enRAGE's Interrogate and social prompts are on it. It reads its ini with `GetPrivateProfileStringA`; whether only at start is unknown, so `_hookupsStartKey` (the key at our start, or the copy's when that was made after the game process started, i.e. an Insert reload since) is held too. A yes (`TrystOffered`) makes a `TrystOffer` with `Hookups` (5 minutes, `HookupWillingMs`); the second flirt (`TrystAsked`) says the words, extends it and doesn't fade. Every frame, a lock-on target that is a woman and not the willing one gets `DISABLE_CONTROL_ACTION` on its key(s); a press of the held key logs `HOOKUPS held`, a toast the first 3 times, and watches for a scene anyway (to learn whether the hold works). On the willing one, its key pressed starts `HookupRun`: a scene is taken to have begun when the player's controls are off or he plays `mech_inventory@clothing@outfit_change` `outfit_change_unarmed`, or they're within 0.9 m, for 3 s within 60 s (judged from his side: its strings speak of hiding the woman and of a stale one; `HerState` logs her handle at the press, the start and the end) (`Engine.Tryst`, `HOOKUPS with X: its scene seems to have begun`), over when he's out of it and apart from her (or she's gone) for 3 s (her `Trysts.After` over her head, `HOOKUPS over`). A scene with a woman who never said yes logs that the hold failed and toasts once how to give it back its chances. First test (20:20, Saint Denis saloon): CasualHookups said "Lady is not interested" for Willa Tate and Pearl Babineaux (`a_f_m_nbxwhore_01`) with 0/100/0, so it turns that model down itself (its strings list the four towns' saloon models together; only this one is proven). So (Lex: "all NPCs voiced dialogue should be consistent with the NPCs inner thoughts and actions"): no sign of its scene (`Stirred`: controls off, undressing, within 0.9 m) `HookupFallbackMs` (10 s) after its key on a willing woman -> `HookupsLearnWont` (Store.Counts `hookups won't:<model>`, seeded by `HookupsWontSeen`) and `TrystFallback` (her `Trysts.Yes` voiced through `FlirtVoices.Night`, then our fade). A "won't" model's yes is a fade offer (`TrystOffered`), and its key is held on her with "CasualHookups won't take her, so flirt with her again". A new press on a willing woman replaces a watch that saw nothing (a watch on Pearl hid the press on Willa). Next, at Lex's call: a check on every voiced line, each of the game's line names given a feeling (warm, cold, afraid, angry) and the speaker's feelings filtering them, mismatches logged. F7 `TEST casual hookups:`. Panel: `SettingsPanel.HookupsIniPath` (set in `OpenPanel`), rows on the Romance page under "CasualHookups (another mod)" with `Row.Hookups`, our own `Label`/`Help` (never its comments), `HookupsNext` (B/G, 1-3, chances 0-100, booleans), `BalanceChances` (yes-at-once takes up the difference; asking-twice when yes-at-once changed), a backup per session. The game folder is writable by Users (icacls). Not yet seen in game: the hold, the scene detection, Arthur's GREET_SECOND_* lines.

## Rough handling (0.6.2, in progress, roadmap 7)

- `Game/WestworldScript.Roughs.cs`. Only a test so far: Shift+F9 (`RoughTestKey`; F6 is the game's camera) picks the man nearest you in a bar scenario (else the nearest man not in a scenario) as the victim and the nearest other man as the attacker, all within `RoughNear` 10 m, men who pass `TalkLeftOut` and `CanDirect` and aren't enRAGE records; each press tries the next of `SmashAngles` (the game's paired grapple move `mech_grapple@unarmed@_male@_ambient@_healthy@front@environment@table_smash`, `<angle>_att` and `<angle>_vic`: near front/left/right/backleft/backright, far front/left/right). `RoughPlace` works out the shared origin with `GET_ANIM_INITIAL_OFFSET_ROTATION`/`_POSITION` so the victim starts on his own spot facing his own way; the attacker walks to his half's start (`TASK_GO_STRAIGHT_TO_COORD`, 8 s), the victim is let go of his scenario (`CLEAR_PED_TASKS_IMMEDIATELY`), then both play with `TASK_PLAY_ANIM_ADVANCED` from that origin, flags OverridePhysics | ExtractInitialOffset (`RoughFlags`), p13-p16 = 0, 2, 0, 0 (a guess; GTA's are anim time, rotation order, IK flags). Animation only: no damage, so no crime. The attacker says an `Angry` line as he grabs, the victim a `Scared` one after. Events blocked for both while it runs, released in `RoughEnd` (also on shutdown and mission start). Log `ROUGH test:`, the origin and offsets, `playing yes|NO / yes|NO`, how far the victim moved, `over|stopped`. F7 `TEST rough`: whether `table_smash`, `wall_smash` and `script_re@drunk_dueler` exist, each angle's lengths, and everyone in a scenario within 20 m with its bar scenario name (`BarScenarios`: WORLD_HUMAN_BARCUSTOMER, _BEER, _WHISKEY, SC_WORLD_HUMAN_STAND_BAR, WORLD_HUMAN_LEAN_BAR_READ_NEWSPAPER, WORLD_HUMAN_DRUNK_BAR_SLUMPED, from rdr3_discoveries' animations/scenarios/scenario_types_with_conditional_anims.lua; names guessed from animation set names didn't match), `ScenarioName` for a few others, else the hash from `_GET_SCENARIO_POINT_TYPE_PED_IS_USING` (seen in the Rhodes saloon 23:32: the bar customer WORLD_HUMAN_BARCUSTOMER_WHISKEY, the barman WORLD_HUMAN_BARTENDER_CLEAN_GLASS, PROP_HUMAN_PIANO, saloon girls PROP_HUMAN_SEAT_CHAIR_TABLE_FAN_WHORE). First try (23:32): the attacker never moved from his scenario and only the victim's half played, 1 m high; now an attacker in a scenario is preferred last, gets `CLEAR_PED_TASKS` and 2 s to stand, walks by navmesh (`TASK_FOLLOW_NAV_MESH_TO_COORD`), and the origin is corrected in height too. Angle lengths (in game): near front 2.3 s, near left 2.9, near right 2.7, near backleft 2.3, near backright 2.7, far front 3.5, far left 3.5, far right 3.8. Lex (2026-10-25, after the test grabbed the piano player): anywhere the move can play, not just the bar, between two men with the temper and the grudge for it, and now and then by chance. So the test now looks for a surface by the victim (`RoughSurfaces`, raycasts with `Natives.Probe`, which reads the game's 8-byte vector slots: a top `TopLow`-`TopHigh` 0.55-1.3 m above the floor straight down at 0.55/0.85/1.15 m in eight directions, a wall level at chest height within 1.3 m) and reads which way each angle of both moves (`table_smash`, and `wall_smash`'s `WallAngles`) drives the victim (`RoughMoves`: `GET_ANIM_INITIAL_OFFSET_POSITION` at phases 0.3/0.45/0.6 against phase 0, turned to his start facing; logged once as `ROUGH moves:`; whether p8 really is the phase is unproven, and if every push reads 0 m it falls back to taking angles in turn). `RoughChoose` takes the angle whose push is within `RoughFit` 40 degrees of a surface, nearest in distance, tops before walls. Next: confirm in game that the chosen angle lands him on the surface, then a reason in `Reasons.cs` (hostile tie or feud, a Hothead or Proud attacker, a surface by the victim; anywhere, not only saloons; plus a rare chance roll, Lex's 'some randomness'), onlookers, its own setting (violence between people, like `CrudeTalk`), and stand down near any `re_` mission ped (the game's own drunk dueler). The drunk challenge comes after, from `script_re@drunk_dueler` (main_challenge_drunk, challenge_idle_drunk, pass_out_fwd_v1/v2 and their loops and exits) and the voices' DRUNK and FIGHT lines.

## Trouble between people (0.6.2, built overnight 2026-10-26 at Lex's request, not yet seen in game)

- The Director's `fight` beat also makes both fight with their fists the way enRAGE makes its people (combat attributes 5 fight unarmed, 46 always fight, 58 never flee the fight, each one's own read with `_GET_PED_COMBAT_ATTRIBUTE` and put back at the end, `FightAttrs`): Lex's second stand-up test (00:43) reported "at it 0.0 s" from `IS_PED_IN_COMBAT` the moment they were told, and nobody swung; so "at it" is now melee (`IS_PED_IN_MELEE_COMBAT`) or a blow landed (health under 97%), and the abort says which it was (still sat, in combat but never in melee, never in combat). Seen in game (Lex, 2026-10-28 00:58, the test cast: Tobias and Dennis, Rosa stepping in): punches thrown.
- The Director's `fight` beat (2026-10-28, from Lex's stand-up test: Booker and Ethan, both leaning or sat, were cleared and ordered to fight in the same moment, got up and never swung, and the beat's 9 s ran out on the words): `FightOrder` gives `TASK_COMBAT_PED` only once neither `IS_PED_USING_ANY_SCENARIO` (after 3 s, `CLEAR_PED_TASKS_IMMEDIATELY`), again every 2 s; `FightUntil` is a 12 s start window (`FightStartMs`) until `IS_PED_IN_MELEE_COMBAT` or `IS_PED_IN_COMBAT` either way (`FightOn`, log `SCENE fight: at it N s after the word`), then the beat's own length from then; never at it in 12 s: `Abort("they never came to blows ...")`, so the scene never claims a fight that didn't happen.

- Lex's brief: the game's own NPC encounters (the bar slam in the Rhodes saloon, the Valentine drunk) played between NPCs, each from temperament and grudges plus some randomness; pickpocketing by the right temperaments; more thought types. Lex chose NPC versions started by a reason (never a timer, never the game's own encounter scripts), bar slam first.
- Scenes (`Core/Scenes.Trouble.cs`, `Scenes.N` is internal for it): `Rough(c, seed, grudge, answer)` (two lines, `slam`, the victim's cry, a threat, then by `Exchanges.Manner`: sharp swings back with `fight`/`endfight`, cold walks off, kind flees), `Lift(c, seed, noticed)` (approach, a word, the thief walks on; noticed: the mark finds it gone 4 s later), `Drunk(c, seed, answer)` (`challenge`, `passout`, the other mocks and walks off, `cometo`), `Avoid(c, seed)` (B sees A and walks off), `Beg(c, seed, answer)` (kind = "charity" or "scorn" by the answer), `Barkeep(kind, seed)`, `TroubleSamples` for the tests.
- Reasons (`Core/Reasons.cs`): `Someone.WellOff` (Director.Kind gentleman/lady) and `Drunk` (WORLD_HUMAN_DRUNK_BAR_SLUMPED / WORLD_HUMAN_DRINKING_DRUNK scenario, or in a saloon 20:00-04:00 with the "drunk" quirk or a drink worry). `Reason.Fallback`: what to play if the game side can't. Hostile tie coming to blows with a `Rough` temper (Hothead, Proud, Cold) in it: `RoughChance` 45% "rough" (the rough one first), fallback brawl. `Own()` now gathers every reason that fits and returns the strongest (first of equals, so the old order decides ties): snap 55/45, hosts 45, a thief 47 (money worry at its worst) or 43 (`Thief`: Greedy, Drifter or Honesty < 30 by nature 25%, or a debt/savings/bank/drink worry at stage 1-2 30%, per id and day; never Loyal or "honest"; marks `WellOff` or `Drunk`; "pickpocket" when caught: not a woman thief, not a drunk mark, 55% if the mark is `Alert` (Proud, Cold, Hothead) else 30%, less 20 for a Drifter; else "lift"), drunk 44 (saloon, night, two men, not a Coward), goodnews 42, beg 41 (`Beggar`: the same money worries in the Loyal, Coward, "honest" or Honesty >= 60, 35% per id and day; never Greedy or Proud; only when not a thief that day; well-off sober marks), gossip 40, saloon hothead vs a stranger: `StrangerRoughChance` 15% rough (fallback argue) else argue 30. A hostile tie whose kind is rough, pickpocket or avoid, where the victim's `Manner` is kind: "avoid" (strength 50, the one who did it first). A warm tie where one of the two is `Someone.Hurt` ("rough" or "robbed": the victim of a rough or caught-pickpocket tie in the last 90 game minutes, `HurtLately`): "tend" (58, the friend first; `Scenes.Tend`, kind "comfort", so +1 and the Kindness pool). Diaries (`Engine.DayEntry`) add the day's tie with someone (any trouble kind or any tie with an effect), from the writer's side (`Ties.Thought`), once per tie per day (`dt:` marks).
- Ties (`Core/Ties.cs`): `SceneTie.Doer` (who did it; `Did(id)`), set for `Trouble(kind)` (rough, pickpocket, lift, drunk, charity, scorn: A did it) and kept through "avoid". Effects: rough and pickpocket -2, drunk and scorn -1, lift and avoid 0, charity +1. Role thoughts (RoughDid/Done, LiftDid/Done: the mark never names the thief, CaughtDid/Done, DrunkDid/Done, AvoidDid/Done, CharityDid/Done, ScornDid/Done) and town lines (TownRough, TownCaught, TownDrunk, TownCharity, TownScorn) with the one who did it named first whichever way the tie was made. `TalkOf` never talks of a lift, and does talk of charity. `Crowd.Mood`: rough fight, drunk quarrel, avoid and lift none.
- Game side: `TroubleCant` (WestworldScript.Reasons.cs) checks each kind's setting (`RoughHandling`, `NpcThieves`, `Pickpockets` too for the caught kind, `DrunkChallenges`) and needs (rough: `RoughCanPlay(victim)`, a surface the move fits; caught: no other thief running, a man thief; drunk: the dict exists) and falls back or passes the pair over. For trouble kinds a pair apart has the doer walk over (`meet` by A), never the victim. `AddBarkeep`: in a saloon, a counter keeper whose role is "the barkeep" within 18 m joins rough, brawl and drunk scenes as C with one line after the slam, the end of the fight or the fall, but only if he passes the casting rules (`Natives.StoryModel` false, `IsGrownUp`, `CanDirect`); every counter barkeep is a u_ model, which `StoryModel` counts as the story's own, so for now he is always left out (log `BARKEEP left out (...)`). The Insert carry-over for self-defence (`DefenceCarry.cs`) was built and dropped at Lex's call, 2026-10-26. The Director: `slam` calls `Slam` (= `RoughForScene`) and holds while `SlamGoing`, `SlamStop` on an early end, a hard shove if it can't start; `challenge` (clears a slumped drunk's scenario at once, turns him to the other, `main_challenge_drunk`), `passout` (`pass_out_fwd_v1_drunk` held on its last frame; a ragdoll without the dict), `cometo` (`pass_out_fwd_v1_exit_drunk`); a scene with these requests `script_re@drunk_dueler` and `SET_PED_IS_DRUNK` on A. `Roughs.cs` keeps both grapple dicts loaded while `RoughHandling` is on; a scene's victim moved off the surface gets a shove; up to two onlookers say a `Shame` line as the move begins.
- Reactions (0.6.2, `[Scenes] Reactions`, settings version 45; Lex: "the NPC who gets grabbed and slammed doesn't really react"): `Core/Reactions.cs` `Listener(tone, temper, seed)` maps a line's Speech tag (through `Gestures.Mood`, with mock/won, shame and drunk on their own) and the listener's temperament to a kind (bristle, flinch, dismiss, shrug, nod, puzzled, shamed, hurt), `Clip` picks from that sex's silent gesture set only (clips checked offline against rdr3_discoveries anims.lua; women have no wince, so their hurt is a gasp). `SceneCast.Tempers` (set in `BuildCast` by `CastTemper` = `SomeoneOf(p).Temper`, and for the bystander in `AddBystander`). The Director sets `Live.ReactAt` 700 ms into every spoken line with a voice, for the one it's said to (`other`; a bystander's line goes to A), and calls `React` unless that one's own beat is due within 1 s, a fight is on, a meet is under way, or he's the downed drunk; `Scenes.Rough`'s cry after the slam carries the act `hurt`, which calls `React` with `Reactions.HurtTone`. `Game/WestworldScript.Reactions.cs` (`OnReaction` -> `Safe("reactions")`): `MayDirectWhyNot` before any task (records react fully since 2026-10-26); anyone in a scenario gets the look-at only (`TASK_LOOK_AT_ENTITY` 3.5 s, logged), story characters nothing; then `TASK_PLAY_ANIM` upper body 8|16, or for a man's hurt `script_re@street_fight@v3` `hurt_exit_victim` full body (flag 0) when `ReactionSets` (every 10 s with the rough moves) found the set (`REACT sets:` once in the log). Log `REACT name bristles at X's threat line (Hothead; clip)`, skips in `Log.Detail`; F7 `TEST reactions:`. Offline: ticktest `ReactTest` (every Speech tag x temperament x sex x seed, the clips against the game's lists, the hurt beat in every rough scene). Shift+F9 now plays the whole rough scene through the Director (`StartScene(..., test: true)`, which notes no tie), the answer turning sharp, cold, kind per press (`RoughTestAnswers`). Not seen in game yet; the full-body hurt after the paired move may snap (watch the first one).
- Thoughts (`Core/InnerLife.Now.cs`): a "now" band at rolls 49-57 of `InnerLife.Thought` (timeless frames moved to 58-99, with a `Pleasure` frame at 91-93): `NowThought` picks among what's there, a scene just seen two times in three when there is one (`BySeen` by kind, never for the two in it, `Engine.SeenForMinutes` 45, the same town), someone else in sight (`Regard`: known as a bully or a thief this week, else a drunk pitied, a hothead feared by the timid, the well-off envied by the greedy, drifters and the poor, else `Watching` by temperament), the weather when outdoors (`WeatherOf`: rain, storm, snow, dust, fog, sun, grey), the place when indoors (saloon, church, store, genstore, hotel, barber, bank, doctor, sheriff, station, stable, tailor, gunsmith, theatre). `Engine.NowWeather/NowIndoors/NowPlace` (set in `Places` every 1.5 s), `LastScene` (`SawScene` from `StartScene`, never for a lift), `Nearby` (`NoteNearby` every 5 s from the ticker's `_near`, via `SomeoneOf`, with `Known` from the ties). Offline: lifetest 256,000 thoughts, 6,334 distinct, now/pleasure 8%, worry 22% and day/era/talk 15% as before; TroubleTest covers the scenes, every rate, the roles and the band. Night 2026-10-28 (after item 14): every weather, place and watching-by-temper pool under four lines brought to four (53 lines; `propose` and `jealous` in `BySeen` too), so lifetest's distinct count is 6,570 and ticktest's quiet day 239 distinct; these show with MindLive off. The same night, the fillers and the look at the stranger (`InnerLife.cs`: the hours of the day to 8 lines each, the body 12, the mirror 12, the bias 10, the age 10, the mask 10, him again 8, what they see of him 10, armed 6, mounted 6; 42 lines), the sources poolsurvey showed repeating most in two hours of Valentine (now, look, filler:body, filler:mirror): lifetest 6,604 distinct, ticktest's quiet day 259 and the townsfolk's own 299. Then the worry arcs (`InnerLife.Worries.cs`): every kind's crisis, good and bad pools to three lines and the two after-pools to three (96 lines over the fourteen kinds, the slots as each kind fills them); lifetest 7,963 distinct, ticktest's quiet day 281 (worry 15%) and the townsfolk's own 307; its sample arc's picks moved. Then the one-line pools poolsurvey listed smallest (58 lines): the sentence-shaped mood frames a person says to you (`Voice.MoodSentenceFrames`, three a mood), the worry links' endings (`Links`: paid, took, mended, bitter, yes, no), the town's talk of a tie made up or a sweetheart dead (`Ties`), Friday and the horse-thief talk (`InnerLife.Days`), the hot-tempered's look at a known bully and the envy, pity and wary looks (`InnerLife.Now`), the counter keeper's cold view of the man who shot him (`Posts`); lifetest 7,979, ticktest's quiet day 289. Then the weather and place pools again, four lines to six (48; a psychologist's and a period editor's pass by a subagent on the batch before the commit): lifetest 8,023, ticktest's quiet day 293. Then the look at the stranger again (what they see of him 14, armed 9, mounted 9, him again 12) and the body 16 (18 lines, the same pass): lifetest 8,031. The same night, `Voice.Work` (what a person says they do for a living when you talk to them, `WorkLine`, `{home}` their town) went from two lines a trade to four for all forty trades (80 lines; the goldens don't reach it). Moods (`MoodOf`, roadmap 18's first step): a trouble or warm tie in the last 6 game hours makes them "sore" (roughed up, robbed, sent off asking, the drunk the morning after) or "glad" (charity, comfort, cheer, makeup, sweet, propose, flirt), else a foul day out of doors sore 1 in 3: `Sore`/`Glad` lines in the now band, and `Someone.Hurt` counts as "at the worst" for `Exchanges.Manner` (sore people answer sharp). `JustSaw` is matched to `Engine.CurrentTown` (where the player is), not the thinker's home; `Nearby` only while `NearbyAt` is under 3 game minutes old.
- Fixed after an independent review the same night (none of it in game yet): a stranger stepping into a trouble scene mid-fight now stops the fight (`Intrude` clears both when the new beats have no `endfight`; `Live.WasFighting` makes `End` clear tasks however it ended); "meet" lets a walker out of his scenario first (`CLEAR_PED_TASKS`, sets off 1.8 s later, `MeetGoAt`; `MeetWalker` says who walks, for the log too); `RoughForScene` gets a seated attacker up (2 s) as the test does, and a move whose attacker half doesn't start stops at once with a shove (the victim alone would smash his own face); a drunk still lying there when a scene ends is played his get-up clip and his drunk voice is reset (`Downed`, `MadeDrunk`); rough, drunk and lift scenes note their tie and the "just saw" memory only when it happens (`Director.Happened`: the move began, or "shove" noted as a quarrel; he went down; the thief walked off), others still on starting; the grapple and drunk sets are re-requested every 10 s if unloaded; `ROUGH: no angle's push could be read` is logged once if every push reads 0 m (then rough scenes stay fistfights). A second review round: `NearbyAt` uses `Store.LastGameMin` (the clock `ThoughtDay` reads; `Bridge.Now()` ran ahead and the list was dropped) with a 3-minute window either way; `End` clears only the two who fought (not the barkeep or a bystander); a rough is reported only once it's over (`Director.SlamResult` = `_roughResult`: "rough" when stage 2 sees the attacker's half playing, "shove" from `RoughEnd(shoved)`, null when it never came to anything, then no tie at all); a downed drunk only looks at a stranger who steps in.

## Quiet while still (0.6.0)

- `Game/WestworldScript.Quiet.cs` + `Game/Bridge.Calm.cs`: after `QuietStillSeconds` without moving (position within 0.4 m, no move input; camera ignored) or while `CAM._IS_IN_CINEMATIC_MODE()` / `IS_CINEMATIC_CAM_RENDERING()`, call enRAGE's private `Grace(int seconds, string why)` by reflection: 10 s, renewed every 7 s. enRAGE's `NoSpawnReason` then returns "settling in", which holds `UpdateTension` and is checked by `RunRandomEncounter` (not the test encounters of enRAGE's debug spawner, Ctrl+the `ForceAmbush` key with `[Debug] Enabled`; a plain press of that key does nothing), `UpdateRaids`, `UpdateWarPatrolRoll`, `UpdateWarEncounterRoll`, `UpdateRoadAmbush`, faction responses and town arrivals. Grace only lengthens, so the quiet ends up to 10 s after moving. It does nothing if enRAGE's `[Calm] Enabled` is false (`Bridge.EnrageCalmOn`, warned once). Each Grace call writes a "CALM grace" line to enRAGE's own log. Since enRAGE 0.9.5.3 its calm also makes planned events wait ("it waits: settling in"), so while a `funeral` or `habit` plan is due or going on within 250 m (`PeacefulPlanDue`: not Done, from DueAt - 5 to DueAt + max(60, WindowMin) game minutes, at `PlanInfo.Pos`, looked at every 2 s) no Grace is asked for; gang `job` plans and any other kind stay held. Log `QUIET: not asked for while enRAGE's funeral (title) is due here...`. Runs after the player-control check in `Tick04`, so not during missions or cutscenes (enRAGE is calm there anyway).

## Voices (0.6.0)

- No Windows voice any more (Tts.cs removed). Every spoken line is a recorded game line on the speaker's own ped (`Game/Speech.cs`: `Speech.For(ped, line, ...)` reads the tone with `Core/Tone.cs`, `fallback` when nothing fits; `Speech.When(ped, tag)` for a set by tag). Uncanny lines use the `uneasy` set (WHATS_GOING_ON, GENERIC_SHOCKED_MED, NO_IDEA, GENERIC_SHOCKED_DISBELIEF); the stalker falls back to `mock`. The game can't say new words: the subtitle carries the meaning, and where `voices\words.txt` knows a take's words (`VoiceBook`, models with one bank only), `Feed` shows the real words and sends the written line to the ticker as the thought (roadmap 14 fills words.txt).
- Which voices carry which lines: the decoded bank list (scratchpad `amb_takes.txt`, BANK|LINE|takes, from rdr3_discoveries audio_banks.lua). Put specific lines first in a set: `SayAny` falls through to the next line in the same call when a voice lacks one, and `VoiceBook.Pick` (voices in `banks.txt`) takes the first line the bank lists, so `banks.txt` must be regenerated (from amb_takes.txt) whenever a set gains a line name. Rough travellers carry warnings (WARNED_ALREADY, FINAL_WARNING), camp heads-ups (RT_HEADS_UP_*), CONFRONT_UNSEEN; most townsfolk carry the wordless VOCAL_FX_*, which rough travellers mostly don't. Use a wordless sound where the subtitle is specific; a worded line only where its words agree. Subtitles (0.6.2, Lex: "white subtitles appearing with no voice lines"; only what NPCs say to Arthur): the game gives mods no way into its own dialogue subtitles. No native writes to them, and a probe (2026-10-26, Valentine, the game's Subtitles option on) played GREET_GENERAL_STRANGER eight ways (listener the player, another ped or none; the speech struct's sync flag and its two unnamed flags v7/v8 at 1/1, 0/0, 1/0, 0/1; the scripted-speech natives `_CREATE_NEW_SCRIPTED_PED_AMBIENT_SPEECH` / `_PLAY_SOUND_FROM_SCRIPTED_PED_AMBIENT_SPEECH`): all played, none was subtitled. Don't re-run it. So RealNPCs draws them itself the game's way (Lex picked that over the objective line, `Screen.PrintSubtitle` = `_UILOG_SET_CACHED_OBJECTIVE`, after a screenshot of the game's own: white serif text, no speaker name, a dark box, centred around y 0.81-0.85 just above the ticker): `Feed.DrawSubtitle` every frame from `Frame` (`SubtitleDraw`, its own `Safe`, hidden with the ticker, the panel and the reader), `SubtitleScale` 0.42, bottom 0.846, wrapped at 0.62, 150 ms fade in and 250 out, `Draw.Text(..., face)` with `Feed.SubtitleFace` (`$body`: Lex's pick, line 1 of the font test). Shift+F2 (`FontTestKey`) draws the line in each of `FontFaces` for 20 s, numbered, to find the game's face. Under `Core/SubtitleRule.Text`: `toYou` only, the voice really played (`Speech.LastPlayed`, set by `When`'s callback; Feed clears it before `OnShow`) or a later turn of a line whose voice played (`_voicedGroup`), `[Output] Subtitles` on (on | off; old matched/game -> on, settings version 47), the game's own option on (`HUD.IS_SUBTITLE_PREFERENCE_SWITCHED_ON`), the speaker in view when the ped is known; the text is the recording's words (`words.txt`) else the written line (`Line.Part`). No colour markup: raw `<FONT>` tags would show there. Log `SUBTITLE Name: "..." (the recorded words | the written line)`; `SUBTITLES:` once (the game's option); F7 `TEST subtitles:`. Lines said to you with no voice stay silent and unsubtitled (the kin's first line, `Life.cs` passes no voice for line 0; the gathering's "All of them, at once"); overheard talk (Director scenes, real-voice talk, mutters, wake acts) never gets one. `Feed.Notice` and `Interrupt` post to `Toaster` (the game's top-left updates, `_UI_FEED_POST_SAMPLE_TOAST`; seen on the left in game), or the "Around town" box through `Feed.Fallback` once the Toaster has marked itself broken. The one other `PrintSubtitle` is the old-copy warning in the constructor. `NoticesAsSubtitles` and `OverheardTalk` are gone; `NewsRoute` maps subtitles/enrage (and the ticker when it's off) to corner; `WarRoute` is feed (default; "right" was the old name), corner or ticker; `RaiseOldDefaults` moves WarNewsTo corner/right to feed and NewsTo subtitles/enrage to corner below version 46. enRAGE's own white text (`ShowNotification`, its voiceless speech lines) is enRAGE's: `[Menu] Messages`, `[World] WorldNews` and its experimental `GameFeed` in NemesisMod.ini; a white line with no `SUBTITLE` entry at that moment is enRAGE's. Territory relay (`[Output] TerritoryNews`, settings version 51; Lex's screenshot of enRAGE's territory line on the subtitle line): `Game/Bridge.Territory.cs` reads enRAGE's `NemesisMod._feed` (List<(int ms, string text, (int,int,int) colour)>, its last 30 news lines; checked in the 0.9.5.3 member dump), `_worldCatchingUp` and `_quietControl` (enRAGE's own reasons to keep territory news quiet) and `Config.GrudgeMessages` (whether it prints its own copy: `[Menu] Messages` 3); `Game/WestworldScript.Territory.cs` (`TerritoryStep` in Tick04, every 2 s, its own `Safe`) passes each new line `Core/Territory.FromFeed` accepts (the tag, the sentence, the shares in brackets dropped) on by `WarRoute`; lines present at load are only marked seen. Log `TERRITORY: passing ... on` once, `TERRITORY (feed): ...`; F7 `TEST territory:`. The avenger's arrival (`Life.cs`) was RealNPCs' only call making enRAGE print white text (`Bridge.GrudgeNotice`); it's a toast now. `ThoughtSounds`: `ThoughtSound` in Core04 (NearThought.Handle, within 8 m, 3 min per person).

## Master switches and gating (0.6.0)

- `Settings.Gated()` applies the masters to a copy: `WestWorld` false zeroes AwakeningShare/Despair, HostsQuestionReality, Glitches, Reveries, ReturnChance, StrangerReturnChance. `_cfg` (game and engine) is always the gated copy (`Load(...).Gated()`, `CopyFrom(fresh.Gated())`); nothing writes `_cfg` back to disk, so the ini keeps the player's values. Settings.Load ignores sections, so moving keys between sections needs no migration.
- Paths that look at already-woken people check `WestWorld` directly (ticker thoughts for residents and townsfolk, wake acts, the wake key, reverie lines, the summary counts, revenant stalkers). Pending reveries wait while it's off instead of being cancelled.

## Settings panel (0.6.0)

- The world slows while the panel is open (Lex, 2026-10-27: like the weapon wheel or the satchel; `[Output] PanelSlowsTime`, on, and `PanelTimeScale`, 0.25, 0.05-1; settings version 84; `Game/WestworldScript.PanelTime.cs`): `PanelTimeStep` at the top of `Step` (in or out of the world) sets `MISC.SET_TIME_SCALE` every frame the panel is open (never loading or in a story mission) and puts it back to 1 the first frame it isn't; `PanelTimeRestore` also from `Shutdown04` and the error switch-off; only ever back to 1 when the panel slowed it. The game's wheels slow the world through the same engine time scale; `HUD._ENABLE_REDUCED_MENU_TIME_SCALE` (the native database: "Enables reduced time scale while menus such as weapon wheel and satchel are open") only lets the game's own menus do it, so it isn't used. The wheel's blur has no postfx name in the native database; not added. Log `PANEL time:` (Detail). Not yet seen in game. Note: Dead Eye running when the panel opens is slowed to the panel's scale and ends at normal speed when it closes.
- Settings-key notice (0.6.2, `SettingsKeyNotice` in WestworldScript.Settings.cs, under its own `Safe` so a fault can't switch the panel off): 15 s of game time after the first in-world frame. If `_bridge` is null or `!CanReplaceSettings`: every session, "RealNPCs settings: <SettingsKey>. With this enRAGE version, Alt+F11 opens enRAGE's own panel." Else once per player store (`told:settings key` in `Store.Counts`, so once for Arthur and once for John), waiting for `_store`. Sent through `KeyNoticeOut`: News, or `Feed.Notice` when the engine isn't up or the ticker route has the ticker off. Logged as `SETTINGS key: ...`. The line names both keys.
- `SettingsKey2` (Alt+F, 0.6.2, settings version 44): `SettingsKeyHit` (either key; `None` parses to Keys.None, which `KeySpec.Matches` never matches) opens from `KeyDown04` and, from the panel's key queue in `SettingsImpl`, closes, unless `SettingsPanel.Capturing` (then it's the key being set). F7 dump: `TEST settings:` (keys, what Alt+F11 does, which notice case). Offline check: ticktest `KeysTest` (KeySpec.cs and Log.cs compiled in with Core). Only enRAGE 0.9.5.3 has the `_iniPanel` the takeover needs. `ResolvePanel` caches on first call, so "missing the panel" in a 0.9.5.3 user's log would mean it was looked for too early.
- Flirt in the lock-on menu (0.6.2, `[Romance] FlirtPrompt`, settings version 52; Lex looked for it there): `Game/WestworldScript.FlirtPrompt.cs` (`FlirtPromptStep` in Tick04, its own `Safe`). Each frame, the lock-on target (`GET_PLAYER_INTERACTION_TARGET_ENTITY`, as `Flirt` uses) passing `Core/FlirtPromptRule.Offer` (since 2026-10-27 also never the player's own sex while `SameSexRomance` is off: Lex saw it on men) gets the game's own prompt in `_UI_PROMPT_GET_GROUP_ID_FOR_TARGET_ENTITY`'s group: `_UI_PROMPT_REGISTER_BEGIN`, control `INPUT_INTERACT_OPTION2` (0x84543902: H, D-pad down; rdr3_discoveries Controls lists the defaults: nothing is on F2, F6 is `INPUT_PHOTO_MODE`, and the lock-on menu already uses R, F, E, G for enRAGE's dollars and Q), text through `_UI_PROMPT_SET_TEXT` (0x5DD02A8318420DD7) with a `LITERAL_STRING` var string kept for good, standard mode, `_UI_PROMPT_REGISTER_END`. Pressed (`_UI_PROMPT_HAS_STANDARD_MODE_COMPLETED`; seen working in game 13:16, 15 presses) it sets `_flirtRequested` and is remade the next frame; deleted when the lock-on ends and in `Shutdown04`. enRAGE uses OPTION2 only for a newspaper prompt, not on townsfolk. Log `FLIRT prompt pressed: name` (offers in `Log.Detail`); F7 `TEST flirt prompt:`. `Flirt` now ignores story models too (`Natives.StoryModel`). It used to return silently while `Feed.Talking` (any line anywhere waiting, so wake acts nearby swallowed 4 presses in a row); now it only waits for its own last exchange (`_flirtSaidUntil`, 7 s), logs every refusal, and says its lines with `Feed.Say(..., ahead: true)` (Feed's `_first` queue, before `_speech`). Feed retries a `ToYou` line whose voice didn't start (`Speech.LastPlayed` false) up to `VoiceTries` (4), `VoiceRetryMs` (700) apart (`_again`), then logs `VOICE: ...`. Arthur's own lines are not `ToYou` (flirts, and `you` lines in conversations): only NPCs speaking to him get subtitles (Lex). Interest (Lex: "the lady be interested or not based on their inner thoughts on the main character"): `Core/Regard.cs` gives one word for what someone thinks of the player (`OfFolk` in exactly `Folks.Thought`'s order, which now switches on it: smitten, scorned, grateful, witnessed (`Townsfolk.Recognised`), hostile, afraid, fond, met, stranger; `OfPerson` for enRAGE records from Hate/Vengeful/Murderous, Fear, Friendly/Affection, Talks; `OfResident` from hearts, Affection, the town's talk of his bad deeds (`ThoughtDay.TownDeeds`: wary, or afraid for a Coward) and Talks/Sightings). `Core/Interest.Answer` (no dice): spam, not drawn (`Romance.Suits`: sex, adult, 22 years), scorned/hostile/afraid/witnessed are no; smitten yes; spoken for (`Engine.SpokenFor`: a warm romantic tie, or a courting link) and a worry at its worst (`TheirDay`, `InnerLife.WorryNow` stage 2) no; else warmth (grateful/fond 3, met/stranger 1, wary 0) + `Interest.Taste` + mood (`InnerLife.MoodOf`: glad +1, sore -1): 3+ yes (reason: the biggest plus), 1-2 maybe (shy or troubled if those weighed), else no (the biggest minus, else sore, badrep for wary, cold). Taste (Lex: "a lot of personal influence on the type NPC thoughts they have"), from the profile their thoughts come from (`PersonOfFolk` for townsfolk now, the record, `PersonOfResident`) and `Engine.AskFor`: temper vs him (Hothead danger +1 if `BadHere`; Coward frightened -2 / shy -1; Proud money +1 at $500 else beneath -1; Cold -1 under $500; Greedy money +2 at $300, poor -1 under $50; Loyal decent +2 with `GoodHere` (mercy, spared, thiefcaught, peace in town, 3 days), killer -1 with bad; Drifter restless +1), Devout/Churchgoer with bad pious -2/-1, Outgoing +1, Introvert with a stranger shy -1, quirk honest with bad -1, drunk in a saloon +1, widowed lonely +1, Facet Humour 60+ playful +1, worry debt/savings/bank money +1 at $300 else troubled -1, feud troubled -1, secret/law wary -1, letter elsewhere -1 (and taken + letter answers elsewhere). `PlayerCash` is set from `_MONEY_GET_CASH_BALANCE` (cents) before each flirt; `BadHere` is `ThoughtDay.TownDeeds`. `Interest.Explain` names each weight in the FLIRT log line; `Romance.Welcome/Unsure/Rebuff` have lines per reason; `Interest.Soft` separates weighed noes (FlirtedAt, -2 affection) from never-in-question ones. `FlirtResult.Answer/Why`; the reply's voice falls back by answer (flatter, respond, scared, no). Offline: ticktest `TasteTest` (each nature against his money and name, faith, ways, troubles; a line for every reason; 23,060 combinations). `FlirtWith`, `FlirtWithFolk` and the new `FlirtWithResident` (the game side's stranger branch now asks `ResidentOf` first) all use it; their effects (Feel, affection, hearts, enRAGE like) are as before. New `Romance.Rebuff` reasons: badrep, busy, sore. Offline: ticktest `InterestTest` (every regard x drawn x taken x worst x spam x temper x mood; the thought and the regard agree for all nine townsfolk regards).
- Action rows (0.6.3): `Row.Action`/`ActionLabel`/`ActionAsk`, picked like settings (`Pickable`), drawn with "Enter" (red "Enter again to confirm" while asking); the first Enter sets `_confirm` and shows `ActionAsk`, a second Enter on the same row runs the panel's `runAction` (the script's `PanelAction`) and shows what it returns, any other key cancels ("Nothing was wiped."). "Wipe every NPC's memory" (Lex, 2026-10-26, for testing; now on the Testing page): `WipeMemories` (`Game/WestworldScript.Wipe.cs`) saves, copies the save to `RealNPCs\backup\<player>.before-wipe-<time>.json`, lets go of everyone held (Director, encounters, wake acts, trysts, a rough), clears the game side's identity caches (`_resBind`, `_bodyWas`, the ledger's, the courtship thoughts, disbelief, wake cooldowns), deletes the save and calls `LoadStore` (a fresh store and engine, as when the character changes). Not in story missions. enRAGE's memory is untouched. Log `WIPE:`.
- Mouse wheel (0.6.2): `WheelInput` (keyboard and mouse only, not while a key is being captured) reads the disabled controls `CursorScrollUp`/`CursorScrollDown` (1652558994u, 2346611779u, from refs-decomp `eInputType`) with `IS_DISABLED_CONTROL_JUST_PRESSED` and sends Up/Down through `Key`, so the list scrolls with the selection as it always has.

- Pages: RealNPCs sections (OurOrder, minus the key settings), then Hotkeys (OurKeys in KeyOrder under a "RealNPCs" heading, then enRAGE's [Keys] under "enRAGE"), then one enRAGE page (every other NemesisMod.ini section, each under a heading row). Rows are `Row { E, Heading, Theirs }`: which file a row writes to and its section come from the row (`Theirs`, `E.Section`), not the page. Headings can't be chosen (`Selectable`, `Step`); Shift+Up/Down is `Section` (next or previous section). The navigation helpers were tested by lifting them into a scratch project.
- `Game/SettingsPanel.cs` draws it; `Core/IniDoc.cs` reads and edits both inis line-faithfully (only the value part of one line changes; encoding, BOM, line endings kept; saved via temp file + `File.Replace`). Tested byte-for-byte on real copies of both inis.
- `Game/Bridge.Settings.cs` reaches enRAGE's `IniPanel` by name: `_iniPanel`, `IsOpen`, `Toggle()`, `_reload`, `_path`, static `Internal(section, key)` (entries it hides), static `Bindable` (keys it rebinds itself). Missing pieces are logged and Alt+F11 stays with enRAGE.
- Alt+F11 redirect (`WestworldScript.Settings.cs`): each frame, if enRAGE's panel is open and `ReplaceEnrageSettings` is on, close it with its own `Toggle` and toggle ours. `_letEnrageShow` lets enRAGE's panel stay open when ours handed a key binding to it. While ours is open, `KeyDown04` queues every key for it, so no RealNPCs hotkey fires; game controls and the HUD are hidden, the ticker isn't drawn, and `Draw.FrameBudget` is raised to 120 (back to 48 on close).
- Our ini writes call `ApplyOurSettings` (reload + gate at once, stamp updated so `IniTick` doesn't reload twice); a value outside `Clamp()` is rewritten to what's in force. enRAGE ini writes: one backup per session to `RealNPCs\backup`, then `_reload`, then re-read to show what enRAGE kept.
